# Polity Flashcards — Constitution of India

> One flashcard per Part, Schedule, Amendment (1–106) and Article (every Article, lettered and omitted ones included).
> Card layout: **Front** · **Provision** · **Learn** · **Cases / Amendments / Links** · **Trap** · **Back** (memory hook).
> Order: A Parts → B Schedules → C Amendments → D Articles.
> **Verification status:** drafted from the repository Polity owners and the Constitution text from memory; two verification passes were run against the Constitution text (Wikisource, 2020 edition), Wikipedia, case sources and the repository owners. Confirmed or corrected items carry a ✅ tag; every detail that could not be verified is still marked ⚠️ inside its card. Treat ⚠️ items as "re-check before relying". Mnemonics are original.

---

## A. PARTS

### Flashcard Part-I — Part I
**Front:** Part I — The Union and its Territory (Articles 1–4)
**Provision:**
- Art 1 — India, that is Bharat, shall be a Union of States (not "federation" in the text).
- Art 2 — Parliament may admit into the Union, or establish, new States on such terms as it thinks fit.
- Art 2A — inserted by the 35th Amendment (1975) to make Sikkim an "Associate State"; omitted by the 36th Amendment (1975).
- Art 3 — Parliament may form a new State, increase/diminish an area, alter boundaries or names of States by ordinary law; President refers the Bill to the State legislature(s) for views (proviso time limit added by the 5th Amendment, 1955).
- Art 4 — laws under Arts 2 and 3 are not amendments under Art 368 (so a simple majority suffices).
**Learn:**
- Art 3 Explanation I (inserted by the 18th Amendment, 1966): "State" includes a Union Territory for Art 3 purposes.
- Current: 28 States and 8 Union Territories (Telangana formed 2014; J&K split into UT of J&K and UT of Ladakh 2019; Dadra & Nagar Haveli and Daman & Diu merged 2020).
- Cession of territory is different from reorganisation: *In re Berubari Union* (1960) held that ceding territory to a foreign State needs an Art 368 amendment, which is why the 9th Amendment (1960) was passed.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** *In re Berubari Union* (1960, SC advisory opinion); 9th Amendment (1960) (Berubari transfer, Sch 1); 5th Amendment (1955) (Art 3 proviso); 18th (1966); 35th/36th (Sikkim, 1975); 7th Amendment (1956, reorganisation).
**Trap:** Art 3 reorganisation needs only an ordinary law (Art 4 says it is not an Art 368 amendment); but ceding territory to another country needs an Art 368 amendment (Berubari). Art 1 says "Union of States", not "federation"; Art 2A is an omitted Article, not a live one.
**Back:** *"One Union, four Articles: 1 names it, 2 admits, 3 reshapes, 4 says no Art 368."*

### Flashcard Part-II — Part II
**Front:** Part II — Citizenship (Articles 5–11)
**Provision:**
- Art 5 — citizenship at commencement (domicile + birth/parentage/residence 5 years).
- Art 6 — rights of persons who migrated from Pakistan to India before 19 July 1948 (or registered after residence 6 months).
- Art 7 — migrants to Pakistan after 1 March 1947 who came back under permit; not citizens.
- Art 8 — persons of Indian origin living abroad (parents/grandparents born in India) may register.
- Art 9 — a person voluntarily acquiring foreign citizenship ceases to be an Indian citizen.
- Art 10 — continuance of citizenship subject to Parliament's law.
- Art 11 — Parliament may regulate citizenship by law (Citizenship Act, 1955).
**Learn:**
- Part II covers the *commencement* position only; later acquisition/loss is governed by the Citizenship Act 1955 under Art 11.
- Dual citizenship is not recognised; OCI status is a statutory device under the Citizenship Act (not a constitutional category).
- Art 6 and 7 cut-offs (19 July 1948; 1 March 1947) are the exam dates.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** *Sarbananda Sonowal v Union of India* (2005) — Assam IMDT Act 1983 struck down for Art 14/Art 355 failure; Citizenship (Amendment) Act 2019 extends naturalisation for specified religious minorities from Pakistan, Bangladesh and Afghanistan (⚠️ check case status before stating post-2024 developments); Assam NRC context.
**Trap:** Art 5–8 deal with citizenship *at commencement* (26 Jan 1950), not later acquisition; Art 9 loss on foreign citizenship is automatic. Art 11 is the power, not the substantive rule.
**Back:** *"Part 2 = Who is 'We': 5 at birth, 6 & 7 for migrants, 8 for diaspora, 9 losing it, 10–11 Parliament decides."*

### Flashcard Part-III — Part III
**Front:** Part III — Fundamental Rights (Articles 12–35)
**Provision:**
- Art 12 — definition of "State" (government, Parliament, State legislatures, local/other authorities, instrumentalities).
- Art 13 — (1) pre-Constitution laws void to extent of inconsistency; (2) State shall not make laws abridging FRs; (3) defines "law"; (4) Art 368 amendments not "law" (24th Amdt).
- Art 14 — equality before law and equal protection of laws.
- Art 15 — (1) no discrimination on grounds of religion, race, caste, sex, place of birth; (2) access to shops/wells/roads; (3) special provisions for women and children; (4) SEBC/SC/ST advancement (1st Amdt); (5) special provisions for SEBC/SC/ST in private aided/unaided educational institutions (93rd Amdt, 2005; excludes minority institutions); (6) EWS reservation (103rd Amdt, 2019).
- Art 16 — equality of opportunity in public employment; 16(4) backward class reservation; 16(4A) promotion reservation (77th Amdt 1995); 16(4B) carry-forward (81st Amdt 2000); 16(5) religious office; 16(6) EWS (103rd Amdt, 2019).
- Art 17 — untouchability abolished; practice in any form forbidden.
- Art 18 — abolition of titles (except military/academic); citizens may not accept foreign titles.
- Art 19 — six freedoms: (a) speech & expression; (b) assembly without arms; (c) association; (d) movement; (e) residence; (g) profession, occupation, trade or business (the old (f) property was deleted by the 44th Amendment 1978). Reasonable restrictions under 19(2)–(6).
- Art 20 — protection in respect of conviction (no ex post facto law; no double jeopardy; no self-incrimination).
- Art 21 — protection of life and personal liberty (procedure established by law; Maneka Gandhi 1978 gave it content).
- Art 21A — right to free and compulsory education for children 6–14 (86th Amdt 2002).
- Art 22 — protection against arrest and detention (rights to know grounds, consult lawyer, produce before magistrate within 24 hours; preventive detention limits).
- Art 23 — prohibits traffic in human beings, begar and other forced labour.
- Art 24 — no child below 14 employed in factories/mines/hazardous work.
- Art 25 — freedom of conscience and free profession, practice and propagation of religion (25(2) State may regulate secular activity; social reform; Hindu temple entry to all classes).
- Art 26 — freedom to manage religious affairs.
- Art 27 — no tax to promote a particular religion.
- Art 28 — no religious instruction in wholly State-funded institutions.
- Art 29 — protection of language, script and culture of minorities; no denial of admission to State-aided institutions.
- Art 30 — right of minorities to establish and administer educational institutions (30(1A) compensation added by the 44th Amdt).
- Art 31 — omitted by the 44th Amendment (1978); right to property moved to Art 300A.
- Art 31A — saving of laws on estates acquisition, mergers of corporations, etc. (1st Amdt; widened by 4th and 17th).
- Art 31B — validation of laws in the Ninth Schedule (1st Amdt, 1951).
- Art 31C — laws giving effect to DPSPs 39(b) and (c) protected from Arts 14, 19 and 31 (25th Amdt 1971); 42nd widened to all DPSPs; Kesavananda (1973) struck the 42nd extension and the judicial-review ouster.
- Art 31D — anti-national activities (inserted by 42nd Amdt, 1976) omitted by the 43rd Amdt (1977).
- Art 32 — right to move the Supreme Court for enforcement of FRs; SC can issue writs (habeas corpus, mandamus, prohibition, certiorari, quo warranto). Dr Ambedkar called it "the heart and soul" of the Constitution.
- Art 32A — inserted by 42nd Amdt (1976), omitted by 43rd Amdt (1977).
- Art 33 — Parliament may restrict or modify FRs for armed forces and police.
- Art 34 — restriction on FRs while martial law is in force in an area.
- Art 35 — Parliament alone may make laws for certain FR-related matters (e.g., Art 16(3), 32(3)).
**Learn:**
- Art 14 (equality) and Art 21 (life & liberty) are for all persons; Arts 15–16, 19, 29, 30 and 17 are for citizens or defined groups.
- Art 19(1)(f) (property) deleted; the six freedoms are now (a),(b),(c),(d),(e),(g) — the letter-gap is a classic trap.
- *Golak Nath* (1967) held FRs cannot be amended; the 24th Amdt (1971) and *Kesavananda Bharati* (1973) reversed that for Art 368 amendments but preserved the basic structure.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** *Golak Nath v State of Punjab* (1967); *Kesavananda Bharati* (1973) (basic structure); *Maneka Gandhi* (1978) (Art 21 due process, Art 14 and 19 read together); *Minerva Mills* (1980) (balance of FRs and DPSPs); *Indra Sawhney* (1992) (50% ceiling on reservations, creamy layer); *Champakam Dorairajan* (1951) (led to the 1st Amdt); *Shankari Prasad* (1951).
**Trap:** Art 15(4) and 16(4) are enabling clauses ("nothing ... shall prevent"); Art 15(5) (93rd) and 16(6) (103rd) are later additions; Art 14 covers "any person" while Arts 15–16 are for citizens; Art 31 (property) is omitted, while 31A/31B/31C remain live.
**Back:** *"Six freedoms, no 'f': speech, assembly, association, movement, residence, trade — 19(1)(f) property was deleted in 1978."*

### Flashcard Part-IV — Part IV
**Front:** Part IV — Directive Principles of State Policy (Articles 36–51)
**Provision:**
- Art 36 — definition of "State" for Part IV (same as Art 12).
- Art 37 — DPSPs not enforceable by any court; yet fundamental in governance.
- Art 38 — State shall promote welfare of people; 38(2) minimise inequality of income, status, facilities and opportunities (44th Amdt 1978).
- Art 39 — (a) adequate means of livelihood for men and women; (b) distribution of material resources for common good; (c) prevent concentration of wealth; (d) equal pay for equal work; (e) health and strength of workers, children's tender age; (f) children's healthy development.
- Art 39A — equal justice and free legal aid (42nd Amdt 1976).
- Art 40 — organisation of village panchayats.
- Art 41 — right to work, education and public assistance in certain cases.
- Art 42 — just and humane conditions of work and maternity relief.
- Art 43 — living wage, decent standard of life, cottage industries.
- Art 43A — participation of workers in management of industries (42nd Amdt).
- Art 43B — promotion of voluntary formation, autonomous functioning and democratic control of co-operatives (97th Amdt 2011).
- Art 44 — Uniform Civil Code for citizens.
- Art 45 — provision for early childhood care and education to all children until age six (86th Amdt 2002 replaced the old free and compulsory education clause).
- Art 46 — promotion of educational and economic interests of SC, ST and weaker sections.
- Art 47 — raise nutrition and standard of living, improve public health, prohibit intoxicating drinks and drugs harmful to health.
- Art 48 — organise agriculture and animal husbandry on modern lines; prohibit slaughter of cows, calves and other milch/draught cattle.
- Art 48A — protect and improve environment and safeguard forests and wildlife (42nd Amdt).
- Art 49 — protection of monuments and places of national importance.
- Art 50 — separation of judiciary from executive in public services.
- Art 51 — promotion of international peace and security, honourable relations, respect for international law and treaty obligations, arbitration.
**Learn:**
- Borrowed in concept from the Irish Constitution (Dr Ambedkar's standard exam reference); Art 37 says they are non-justiciable.
- DPSPs are non-justiciable (Art 37) but fundamental; Art 39(b),(c) protected by 31C.
- *Minerva Mills* (1980) — FRs and DPSPs must balance; "harmony and balance" is part of the basic structure.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** *State of Kerala v N.M. Thomas* (1976) (affirmative action); *Unnikrishnan* (1993) (right to education, later 21A); *Minerva Mills* (1980); *Kesavananda Bharati* (1973); 42nd Amdt (1976) added 39A, 43A, 48A; 44th Amdt (1978) added 38(2); 86th (2002) changed Art 45 and inserted 21A.
**Trap:** Art 45 is "early childhood care to six" (86th), not free education to 14 (that is 21A, a FR). Art 48 cow slaughter ban is "as far as possible" (DPSP). Art 44 is UCC (DPSP), not FR. Art 37 says non-justiciable.
**Back:** *"Four = Directions, 36 to 51: 39 for justice, 44 for UCC, 45 early years, 51 world peace."*

### Flashcard Part-IVA — Part IVA (Fundamental Duties)
**Front:** Part IVA — Fundamental Duties (Article 51A only)
**Provision:** Art 51A — it shall be the duty of every citizen of India:
- (a) to abide by the Constitution and respect its ideals and institutions, the National Flag and the National Anthem;
- (b) to cherish and follow the noble ideals which inspired the national struggle for freedom;
- (c) to uphold and protect the sovereignty, unity and integrity of India;
- (d) to defend the country and render national service when called upon;
- (e) to promote harmony and the spirit of common brotherhood, and to renounce practices derogatory to the dignity of women;
- (f) to value and preserve the rich heritage of our composite culture;
- (g) to protect and improve the natural environment, including forests, lakes, rivers and wildlife, and to have compassion for living creatures;
- (h) to develop the scientific temper, humanism and the spirit of inquiry and reform;
- (i) to safeguard public property and to abjure violence;
- (j) to strive towards excellence in all spheres of individual and collective activity;
- (k) for a parent or guardian to provide opportunities for education to his child or ward between the age of six and fourteen years (86th Amdt 2002).
**Learn:**
- Inserted by the 42nd Amendment (1976) on the Swaran Singh Committee's advice; 10 duties originally, the 11th added by the 86th Amendment (2002).
- Not enforceable in a court (Art 51A has no enforcement clause); used as an aid in interpretation.
- Duties apply to "citizens", not foreigners; Art 51A(d) includes national service as a "call".
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** 42nd (1976), 86th (2002), Swaran Singh Committee (1976), *AIIMS Students' Union* and other 'duties' cases (interpretive use); *Bijoe Emmanuel* (1986) (Art 51A(a) read with national anthem freedom).
**Trap:** Art 51A(k) is the 86th Amendment duty (only parent/guardian for 6–14); no duty to "vote" in the list; duties are not justiciable; the 11th duty is about education of the child (not a State duty).
**Back:** *"IVA = Duty, one Article: 51A, letters a to k: the 11th child-education duty is the 86th's gift."*

### Flashcard Part-V — Part V
**Front:** Part V — The Union (Articles 52–151)
**Provision (article by article):**
- Art 52 — there shall be a President of India.
- Art 53 — executive power of the Union vested in the President (exercised through officers under the Constitution).
- Art 54 — election of President by electoral college (elected MPs + elected MLAs of States/UTs).
- Art 55 — manner of election; proportional representation by single transferable vote (secret ballot).
- Art 56 — term of office 5 years; resignation to VP; re-election eligible.
- Art 57 — eligibility for re-election.
- Art 58 — qualifications (citizen, 35 years, qualified for LS).
- Art 59 — conditions of office (no Parliament/State seat; no office of profit; emoluments per Second Sch).
- Art 60 — oath or affirmation (Third Sch).
- Art 61 — procedure for impeachment (charge by either House; investigation; 2/3 of total membership vote).
- Art 62 — election to fill vacancy within 6 months.
- Art 63 — there shall be a Vice-President of India.
- Art 64 — VP is ex officio Chairman of Rajya Sabha.
- Art 65 — VP acts as President on vacancy/inability (6 months to fill).
- Art 66 — election of VP by electoral college (both Houses; 11th Amdt 1961).
- Art 67 — term 5 years; resignation to President; removal by RS resolution (majority of all members) agreed by LS.
- Art 68 — time of election (before term ends).
- Art 69 — oath.
- Art 70 — other contingencies (Parliament may provide).
- Art 71 — matters relating to election of President/VP; disputes decided by SC; 39th Amdt added 71(2)/(3) on PM/Speaker etc. (struck).
- Art 72 — pardoning powers (judicial, not Governor's Art 161).
- Art 73 — extent of executive power.
- Art 74 — Council of Ministers headed by PM aids and advises President (42nd: binding; 44th: reconsideration once).
- Art 75 — PM and ministers; Council collectively responsible to LS; oath (Third Sch); 75(4) oath; 75(6) salaries (Second Sch).
- Art 76 — Attorney-General for India.
- Art 77 — conduct of government business.
- Art 78 — duties of PM to President (information).
- Art 79 — Parliament = President + two Houses.
- Art 80 — composition of Rajya Sabha (238 seats of States/UTs + 12 nominated; Fourth Sch).
- Art 81 — composition of Lok Sabha (not more than 530 from States + 20 UTs; 31st Amdt 1973).
- Art 82 — readjustment after each census; proviso freezes seat allocation on the 1971 census and constituency division on the 2001 census until the figures of the first census after 2026 are published (✅ corrected: was '2001 freeze lifted by 84th'; Wikisource Const. 2020, Part V). Freeze status as of 2026-10-30 ⚠️ not verified against a dated source.
- Art 83 — duration of Houses (RS permanent; LS 5 years; extension in emergency by law).
- Art 84 — qualification for membership (citizen, age 30 for RS, 25 for LS).
- Art 85 — sessions; President summons; 6-month gap.
- Art 86 — address and messages by President.
- Art 87 — special address by President.
- Art 88 — rights of ministers and AG to speak in Houses.
- Art 89 — Chairman and Deputy Chairman of Rajya Sabha.
- Art 90 — vacation, resignation and removal of Deputy Chairman.
- Art 91 — powers of the Deputy Chairman / acting Chairman.
- Art 92 — Chairman not presiding when resolution for removal is considered.
- Art 93 — Speaker and Deputy Speaker of Lok Sabha.
- Art 94 — vacation, resignation, removal of Speaker/Deputy Speaker.
- Art 95 — powers of Speaker/Deputy Speaker when acting.
- Art 96 — Speaker not presiding when removal resolution is considered.
- Art 97 — salaries of Chairman/Speaker (Second Sch).
- Art 98 — secretariat of each House.
- Art 99 — oath or affirmation of members (Third Sch).
- Art 100 — voting; quorum (one-tenth of members).
- Art 101 — vacation of seats (dual membership; absence 60 days; disqualification).
- Art 102 — disqualification for membership (office of profit; unsound mind; insolvent; foreign allegiance; Parliament law; Tenth Sch).
- Art 103 — decision on disqualification (President, after EC opinion).
- Art 104 — penalty for sitting without oath or while disqualified (₹500 per day).
- Art 105 — powers, privileges and immunities (parliamentary privilege).
- Art 106 — salaries and allowances of members.
- Art 107 — procedure for legislation (bills; passing both Houses).
- Art 108 — joint sitting of Houses (deadlock; 6 months; disputes).
- Art 109 — special procedure for Money Bills (RS 14 days; no amendment; Speaker certifies).
- Art 110 — definition of Money Bill.
- Art 111 — assent to bills.
- Art 112 — Annual Financial Statement (Budget).
- Art 113 — procedure in Parliament regarding estimates (demands for grants).
- Art 114 — appropriation bills.
- Art 115 — supplementary, additional or excess grants.
- Art 116 — votes on account, votes of credit, exceptional grants.
- Art 117 — financial bills (not money bill) — President's recommendation etc.
- Art 118 — Rules of procedure of each House (Speaker/Chairman).
- Art 119 — Parliament may regulate procedure in financial business (President may prescribe).
- Art 120 — language in Parliament (Hindi or English; 15-year rule).
- Art 121 — no discussion on conduct of SC/HC judges except on removal motion.
- Art 122 — courts shall not inquire into proceedings of Parliament.
- Art 123 — President's power to promulgate ordinances (when Houses not in session; 6 weeks of reassembly).
- Art 124 — constitution of Supreme Court (CJI + up to 33 judges; 124(2) appointment; 124(3) qualifications; 124(6) oath, Third Sch).
- Art 124A — National Judicial Appointments Commission (99th Amdt 2014; struck down in 2015).
- Art 124B — functions of NJAC (struck with 124A).
- Art 124C — Parliament may regulate NJAC procedure (struck).
- Art 125 — salaries of judges (Second Sch).
- Art 126 — appointment of acting CJI.
- Art 127 — ad hoc judges.
- Art 128 — attendance of retired judges at sittings.
- Art 129 — SC is a court of record.
- Art 130 — seat of SC (Delhi; CJI may fix other place).
- Art 131 — original jurisdiction of SC (Union vs State / inter-State disputes).
- Art 131A — (inserted by the 42nd Amdt 1976 ⚠️ insertion not in fetched text; omitted by the 43rd Amdt 1977, s.4, w.e.f. 13-4-1978 ✅ Wikisource Const. 2020, Part V).
- Art 132 — appellate jurisdiction in constitutional cases (certificate of HC).
- Art 133 — appellate jurisdiction in civil cases (30th Amdt: "substantial question of law of general importance").
- Art 134 — criminal appeals (acquittal reversed; death sentence; certificate).
- Art 134A — certificate for appeal (HC).
- Art 135 — jurisdiction of Federal Court under existing laws.
- Art 136 — special leave petition (SLP): SC discretion.
- Art 137 — review of SC judgments/orders.
- Art 138 — enlarging jurisdiction of SC (by law or agreement).
- Art 139 — conferment of writ jurisdiction (Art 32) on SC for other purposes.
- Art 139A — transfer of cases (on SC's own motion or on request).
- Art 140 — ancillary powers of SC.
- Art 141 — law declared by SC binding on all courts in India.
- Art 142 — enforcement of decrees; "complete justice" orders.
- Art 143 — advisory jurisdiction (President may refer question of law/fact; SC may decline).
- Art 144 — civil and judicial authorities to act in aid of SC.
- Art 144A — inserted by the 42nd Amdt (1976) ⚠️ insertion not in fetched text; omitted by the 43rd Amdt 1977, s.5, w.e.f. 13-4-1978 (✅ Wikisource Const. 2020, Part V).
- Art 145 — rules of court (SC).
- Art 146 — officers and servants of SC (independence).
- Art 147 — interpretation (includes 'substantial question' of constitutional interpretation).
- Art 148 — Comptroller and Auditor-General of India (CAG); 148(2) oath (Third Sch); salaries (Second Sch).
- Art 149 — duties and powers of CAG (for Union & State accounts).
- Art 150 — form of accounts of Union and States.
- Art 151 — audit reports (President/Governor to lay before legislatures).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** 42nd Amdt (Arts 74, 131A, 139A, 144A); 99th (NJAC) struck in *Supreme Court Advocates-on-Record Association v Union of India* (2015, 4:1); 39th (Art 71 PM/Speaker); 30th (Art 133); 24th (Art 368). Second Judges (1993) and Third Judges (1998) cases govern collegium (Art 124(2)); *Kesavananda* (basic structure, Art 368); *Golak Nath*; *Indira Gandhi v Raj Narain* (1975, Art 71 election).
**Trap:** Art 74 — President bound by advice (42nd); 44th allowed one reconsideration. Art 123 ordinance is sub-legislation only; Art 142 enforcement is SC-only; Art 143 is advisory, not binding; Art 109 money bill: RS may only recommend and cannot reject; joint sitting (Art 108) applies to ordinary bills only, not money bills.
**Back:** *"Five-two Centre: 52 President, 63 VP, 79 Parliament, 124 SC, 148 CAG, 151 audit reports — 99th NJAC died 2015."*

### Flashcard Part-VI — Part VI
**Front:** Part VI — The States (Articles 152–237)
**Provision (article by article):**
- Art 152 — definition of "State" in Part VI (Jammu & Kashmir originally excluded; ended 2019).
- Art 153 — there shall be a Governor for each State (one Governor may be appointed for two or more States).
- Art 154 — executive power of the State vested in the Governor.
- Art 155 — Governor appointed by President (in practice on Centre's advice).
- Art 156 — Governor's term 5 years; holds office during pleasure of President.
- Art 157 — qualifications (citizen; 35 years).
- Art 158 — conditions of office (no office of profit; emoluments per Second Sch).
- Art 159 — oath or affirmation (Third Sch).
- Art 160 — discharge of Governor's functions in contingencies (by Parliament/President's order).
- Art 161 — Governor's power to pardon, suspend, remit or commute sentences.
- Art 162 — extent of executive power of State.
- Art 163 — Council of Ministers headed by CM aids Governor; discretion clause (163(2)).
- Art 164 — CM appointed by Governor; ministers; collectively responsible to LA; oath (Third Sch).
- Art 165 — Advocate-General for the State.
- Art 166 — conduct of government business (Governor's rules).
- Art 167 — duties of CM to Governor (information).
- Art 168 — legislatures (Governor + Houses; Legislative Council optional).
- Art 169 — abolition or creation of Legislative Councils (by Parliament; State request + 2/3 of members present/vote).
- Art 170 — composition of Legislative Assembly (max 500, min 60; 170(2)).
- Art 171 — composition of Legislative Council (max 1/3 of LA, min 40).
- Art 172 — duration of State Legislature (5 years; extension by law during emergency).
- Art 173 — qualification for membership (citizen; 25 years for LA, 30 for LC).
- Art 174 — sessions of State legislature (6 months gap).
- Art 175 — address and messages by Governor.
- Art 176 — special address by Governor (first session of year; first session after elections).
- Art 177 — rights of ministers and AG to speak.
- Art 178 — Speaker and Deputy Speaker of LA.
- Art 179 — vacation, resignation and removal of Speaker/Deputy Speaker.
- Art 180 — Deputy Speaker's power as Speaker.
- Art 181 — Speaker not presiding when removal resolution.
- Art 182 — Chairman and Deputy Chairman of LC.
- Art 183 — vacation, resignation, removal of Chairman/Deputy Chairman.
- Art 184 — Deputy Chairman (or a person the Governor picks if that office is also vacant) performs the Chairman's duties when vacant, and acts as Chairman in the Chairman's absence (✅ corrected: was 'Chairman acting as Governor?'; Wikisource Const. 2020, Part VI).
- Art 185 — Chairman not presiding during removal.
- Art 186 — salaries of Speaker/Chairman (Second Sch).
- Art 187 — secretariat of State legislature.
- Art 188 — oath or affirmation of members (Third Sch).
- Art 189 — voting; quorum (one-tenth; majority of members).
- Art 190 — vacation of seats (dual membership; absence 60 days).
- Art 191 — disqualification for membership (Governor's decision on EC opinion; Tenth Sch).
- Art 192 — decision on disqualification (Governor after EC).
- Art 193 — penalty for sitting unqualified (₹500 per day).
- Art 194 — powers, privileges and immunities of State legislators.
- Art 195 — salaries and allowances of members.
- Art 196 — procedure in legislation (bills; passing).
- Art 197 — restriction on powers of Legislative Council (non-money bills; 14-day delay).
- Art 198 — special procedure for money bills in LA (LC 14 days; no amendment).
- Art 199 — definition of money bill for State.
- Art 200 — assent to bills (Governor may withhold, return once, reserve for President).
- Art 201 — bills reserved for President's consideration (President's assent; return with message).
- Art 202 — Annual Financial Statement (State budget).
- Art 203 — procedure regarding estimates (demands for grants).
- Art 204 — appropriation bills.
- Art 205 — supplementary grants.
- Art 206 — votes on account, credit, exceptional grants.
- Art 207 — financial bills (procedure).
- Art 208 — rules of procedure (Speaker/Chairman).
- Art 209 — legislature regulates financial business (Governor's recommendation).
- Art 210 — language of legislature (Hindi/English; mother tongue exceptions).
- Art 211 — no discussion on conduct of HC judges except removal motion.
- Art 212 — courts shall not inquire into legislative proceedings.
- Art 213 — Governor's power to promulgate ordinances (when LA not in session; 6 weeks after reassembly).
- Art 214 — High Court for each State (may be common HC: Art 231).
- Art 215 — High Courts are courts of record.
- Art 216 — constitution of High Court (CJ + other judges).
- Art 217 — appointment and conditions of HC judges (CJI consulted; retirement at 62; 15th Amdt 1963).
- Art 218 — application of Art 124 provisions to HCs.
- Art 219 — oath or affirmation of HC judges (Third Sch).
- Art 220 — restriction on practice after HC judgeship (no practice in SC/HC; except SC).
- Art 221 — salaries of HC judges (Second Sch).
- Art 222 — transfer of HC judge (President after CJI consultation).
- Art 223 — appointment of acting Chief Justice.
- Art 224 — appointment of additional and acting judges.

- Art 226A — (inserted by 42nd Amdt 1976, omitted by 43rd Amdt 1977).
- Art 227 — superintendence over all subordinate courts and tribunals (HC).
- Art 228 — transfer of certain cases to High Court.

- Art 231 — establishment of common High Court for two or more States (7th Amdt 1956) or for a State and UT.
- Art 232 — substituted by Arts 230 and 231 by the 7th Amdt 1956, s.16, w.e.f. 1-11-1956 (✅ corrected: was 'omitted'; Wikisource Const. 2020, Part VI); now an editorial note only.
- Art 233 — appointment of district judges (Governor, in consultation with HC).
- Art 233A — validation of certain appointments of district judges (20th Amdt 1966).
- Art 234 — recruitment of persons other than district judges (via Governor & HC/PSC).
- Art 235 — control over subordinate courts (HC; subordinate courts under HC control).
- Art 236 — definitions (district judge; judicial service).
- Art 237 — application of Part VI to certain class of magistrates (Governor's public notification; ✅ wording checked, Wikisource Const. 2020, Part VI).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** 7th Amdt (Arts 231, 232, reorganisation); 15th Amdt (Arts 217, 224A, 226(2)); 20th Amdt (Art 233A); 42nd (Art 226A) and 43rd omission; 43rd (Art 228A omission, w.e.f. 13-4-1978 ✅ corrected from 44th); 2019 Art 370 presidential order and J&K Reorganisation Act 2019 (Art 152 exclusion ended); *Rameshwar Prasad* (2006) (Governor's dissolution of assembly — ⚠️ check holding); *S.R. Bommai v Union of India* (1994) (Art 356 judicial review; secularism basic structure; floor test).
**Trap:** Art 213 ordinance vs Art 123 ordinance: Governor's power is not "an emergency" power; Art 226 writ power of HC is broader than Art 32 (any other purpose); Art 233 district judges via Governor in consultation with HC (not PSC); Art 234 non-judicial recruitment via PSC.
**Back:** *"Six = States: 153 Governor, 164 CM, 168 Legislature, 214 HC, 226 writs, 237 magistrates."*

### Flashcard Part-VII — Part VII (repealed)
**Front:** Part VII — States in Part B of the First Schedule (Article 238; repealed)
**Provision:** **Art 238 — repealed by the 7th Amendment (1956).** Art 238 governed the Part B States (Hyderabad, Jammu & Kashmir, Madhya Bharat, Mysore, Patiala & East Punjab States Union, Rajasthan, Saurashtra, Travancore-Cochin, Vindhya Pradesh, ⚠️ list from memory, verify) which were abolished as a category by the States Reorganisation Act 1956 (Part A/B/C/D classification abolished).
**Learn:**
- Part VII is a repealed Part; its numbering is kept so Part VIII begins at Art 239.
- The 7th Amendment (1956) inserted new Articles 350A, 350B and the common High Court Art 231 and removed Part B and Part C/D distinctions.
- Part VII is left vacant in the Constitution text; it is not a live Part (repealed, not omitted in the 19th-style sense).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** 7th Amdt (1956); States Reorganisation Act 1956; Part VIII (Arts 239–242) for UTs.
**Trap:** The question "which Part is repealed?" answer is VII (Art 238). Part A/B/C classification was not restored after 1956; the 1956 Act (not Art 3) did the reorganisation.
**Back:** *"VII is the vacant chair: Art 238 repealed in 1956."*

### Flashcard Part-VIII — Part VIII (Articles 239–242)
**Front:** Part VIII — The Union Territories (Articles 239–242)
**Provision:**
- Art 239 — administration of Union Territories (President through an Administrator; Parliament may create a common Lt Governor).
- Art 239A — creation of local Legislatures or Council of Ministers or both for certain UTs (inserted 14th Amdt 1962; for Puducherry).
- Art 239AA — special provisions for the National Capital Territory of Delhi (69th Amdt 1991; Delhi legislature & CM).
- Art 239AB — provision in case of failure of constitutional machinery in a UT (President's rule-type power).
- Art 239B — Parliament-created UT legislatures; ordinance power for UT administration (inserted 27th Amdt 1971).
- Art 240 — power of President to make regulations for certain UTs (Andaman & Nicobar, Lakshadweep, DNH, Daman & Diu, Ladakh, Puducherry as applicable).
- Art 241 — High Courts for Union Territories (Delhi HC; Parliament may create).
- Art 242 — (omitted by the 7th Amdt 1956, s.29 and Sch., w.e.f. 1-11-1956; formerly the Coorg Legislative Council) (✅ Wikisource Const. 2020, Part VIII).
**Learn:**
- The 69th Amdt (1991) gave Delhi a legislature and CM; the NCT Delhi Act 1991; Art 239AA(4) Council of Ministers aid to Lt Governor with exceptions.
- Puducherry: 14th Amdt (1962) made it a UT with a legislature and council of ministers (Art 239A).
- Art 240 regulations have the same force and effect as an Act of Parliament and may repeal or amend Parliament's Acts; no laying-before-Parliament requirement appears in the text (✅ corrected: 'after Parliament approves' removed; Wikisource Const. 2020, Part VIII).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** *Government of NCT of Delhi v Union of India* (2018) (Constitution Bench: Lt Governor is not independent; Delhi's aid and advice binding except land, police, public order); 69th (1991); 14th (1962); 27th (1971); 2019 Ladakh and J&K UT reorganisation.
**Trap:** Delhi is an NCT with a legislature under Art 239AA, not a State under Art 1; Puducherry is a UT with legislature (Art 239A); Art 239B is for ordinance power in UT legislatures (not Art 239AA).
**Back:** *"Eight = UTs: 239 Admin, 239A/AA legislatures, 240 regs, 241 HCs."*

### Flashcard Part-IX — Part IX (Panchayats)
**Front:** Part IX — The Panchayats (Articles 243–243O)
**Provision (article by article):**
- Art 243 — definitions (gram sabha, panchayat, village, intermediate level, district level).
- Art 243A — Gram Sabha in a village may exercise powers Legislature provides.
- Art 243B — constitution of Gram Panchayats at village level (three-tier system).
- Art 243C — composition (elected members by direct election; seats at each level; Chairperson).
- Art 243D — reservation of seats for SC/ST (population proportion) and women (not less than one-third, including SC/ST women).
- Art 243E — duration 5 years; election before expiry.
- Art 243F — disqualification by or under a State Legislature's law; disputes decided as the State law provides (✅ Wikisource Const. 2020, Part IX).
- Art 243G — powers, authority and responsibilities (Eleventh Schedule: 29 subjects).
- Art 243H — power to impose taxes, duties, tolls and fees (Legislature law).
- Art 243I — State Finance Commission (every 5 years).
- Art 243J — audit of accounts of Panchayats.
- Art 243K — elections to Panchayats; State Election Commission (Governor appoints).
- Art 243L — application to Union Territories.
- Art 243M — Part not to apply to Scheduled Areas (244(1)) and tribal areas (244(2)); also not to Nagaland, Meghalaya, Mizoram or Manipur's hill areas with district councils (✅ corrected: was 'certain tribal areas of Assam'; Wikisource Const. 2020, Part IX, 243M(1)-(2)); Parliament may extend with modifications (243M(4)(b)).
- Art 243N — continuance of existing laws until Panchayat laws are made (1 year).
- Art 243O — bar to interference by courts in electoral matters (election petitions to the Election Tribunal/Authority).
**Learn:**
- 73rd Amdt Act (1992), in force 24 April 1993 (Art 243 onward); Eleventh Schedule lists 29 subjects.
- Seats for SC/ST and women reserved; one-third for women includes SC/ST women quota.
- State Finance Commission and State Election Commission are constitutional bodies.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** 73rd (1992); 11th Schedule (29 subjects); Art 243O (ouster of courts on electoral matters); Art 40 DPSP (village panchayats) is the DPSP link.
**Trap:** Panchayats are a State subject (Art 243G, 11th Sch); the State list entry is not the source; Art 243M excludes Fifth Schedule areas from Part IX unless Parliament extends them. Parliament has no direct power to legislate on Panchayats except via Art 243M.
**Back:** *"IX = village: 73rd, 243–243O, 11th Schedule, 29 subjects."*

### Flashcard Part-IXA — Part IXA (Municipalities)
**Front:** Part IXA — The Municipalities (Articles 243P–243ZG)
**Provision (article by article):**
- Art 243P — definitions (municipal area, municipality, Municipal Corporation, Municipal Council, Nagar Panchayat).
- Art 243Q — constitution of three types of municipalities (Nagar Panchayat for transitional areas; Municipal Council for smaller urban areas; Municipal Corporation for larger).
- Art 243R — composition (elected members; direct elections; chairperson).
- Art 243S — constitution of ward committees (in municipalities with population ≥3 lakh).
- Art 243T — reservation of seats (SC/ST; women 1/3 incl. SC/ST women).
- Art 243U — duration (5 years).
- Art 243V — disqualification.
- Art 243W — powers and responsibilities (Twelfth Schedule: 18 subjects).
- Art 243X — taxes, duties, tolls and fees.
- Art 243Y — State Finance Commission (every 5 years).
- Art 243Z — audit of accounts.
- Art 243ZA — elections to municipalities; State Election Commission.
- Art 243ZB — application to Union Territories.
- Art 243ZC — Part not to apply to Scheduled and Tribal areas (Fifth & Sixth Sch) except Parliament may extend.
- Art 243ZD — District Planning Committee (metropolitan & district plans).
- Art 243ZE — Metropolitan Planning Committee (population ≥10 lakh).
- Art 243ZF — continuance of existing laws.
- Art 243ZG — bar to interference by courts in electoral matters.
**Learn:**
- 74th Amdt Act (1992), in force 1 June 1993; Twelfth Schedule lists 18 subjects.
- Metropolitan Planning Committee under Art 243ZE (population over 10 lakh); District Planning Committee under Art 243ZD.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** 74th (1992); 12th Schedule (18 subjects); Art 243W; Art 243ZE (MPC); Art 243ZD (DPC).
**Trap:** Ward committees are mandatory only where population ≥3 lakh (Art 243S); Nagar Panchayat is for transitional areas, not a Municipal Corporation; Art 243ZC keeps Fifth/Sixth areas out (with Parliament's power to extend).
**Back:** *"IXA = urban: 74th, 243P–ZG, 12th Schedule, 18 subjects, 3 types (Nagar Panchayat, Council, Corporation)."*

### Flashcard Part-IXB — Part IXB (Co-operative Societies)
**Front:** Part IXB — The Co-operative Societies (Articles 243ZH–243ZT)
**Provision (article by article; sub-clauses checked against Wikisource Const. 2020, Part IXB):**
- Art 243ZH — definitions (co-operative society; multi-State co-operative society).
- Art 243ZI — State legislation on incorporation, regulation and winding up of co-operative societies (✅ Wikisource Const. 2020, Part IXB).
- Art 243ZJ — board size (maximum 21 directors, plus up to two co-opted members), term of five years, and reserved seats (one for SC/ST, two for women) (✅ corrected: was 'number of members and rights'; Wikisource Const. 2020, Part IXB). The right to form co-operatives is Art 19(1)(c), not Art 243ZJ.
- Art 243ZK — elections to the board, to be held before its term ends (✅ corrected: was 'board of directors'; Wikisource Const. 2020, Part IXB).
- Art 243ZL — supersession or suspension of the board: not beyond six months (one year for certain banking societies) (✅ corrected; Wikisource Const. 2020, Part IXB).
- Art 243ZM — audit of accounts, within six months of the close of the financial year (✅ corrected: reservation is in 243ZJ; Wikisource Const. 2020, Part IXB).
- Art 243ZN — annual general body meeting, within six months of the financial year's close (✅ corrected; Wikisource Const. 2020, Part IXB).
- Art 243ZO — member access to books and information, participation requirements, co-operative education and training (✅ corrected: was 'audit'; Wikisource Const. 2020, Part IXB).
- Art 243ZP — annual returns within six months of year-end (✅ corrected: was 'registration and supervision'; Wikisource Const. 2020, Part IXB).
- Art 243ZQ — offences and penalties (✅ corrected: was 'winding up'; Wikisource Const. 2020, Part IXB).
- Art 243ZR — application to multi-State co-operative societies (State references read as Parliament, Central Act and Central Government) (✅ Wikisource Const. 2020, Part IXB).
- Art 243ZS — application to Union Territories (✅ Wikisource Const. 2020, Part IXB).
- Art 243ZT — inconsistent State laws continue until amended or repealed, or for one year from the 97th Amdt, whichever is less (✅ Wikisource Const. 2020, Part IXB).
**Learn:**
- The 97th Amendment (2011) added the right to form co-operative societies as a FR under Art 19(1)(c), and inserted Art 43B (DPSP on co-operatives); Part IXB came into force on 15-2-2012 (✅ Wikisource Const. 2020, Part IXB).
- Part IXB applies in part to multi-State co-operative societies (Union list entry 44 is their basis).
- Co-operatives are a State subject (State list entry 32) except multi-State co-ops (Union entry 44).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** *Union of India v. Rajendra N. Shah* (2021): Constitution Bench struck Part IXB to the extent it applied to State co-operative societies (State subject), but upheld the provisions for multi-State co-operative societies; 97th Amdt (2011); Art 43B; Art 19(1)(c); State List 32 / Union List 44.
**Trap:** Art 19(1)(c) is the 97th-amended co-operative freedom, but the Part IXB *State* provisions were struck; multi-State co-ops remain valid. Co-operatives are not a Panchayat subject.
**Back:** *"IXB = Co-op, 97th; SC cut it to multi-State only."*

### Flashcard Part-X — Part X (Scheduled and Tribal Areas)
**Front:** Part X — The Scheduled and Tribal Areas (Articles 244–244A)
**Provision:**
- Art 244 — administration of Scheduled Areas and Scheduled Tribes; Fifth Schedule (States other than Assam, Meghalaya, Tripura, Mizoram) and Sixth Schedule (Tribal Areas of the four NE States).
- Art 244A — formation of an autonomous State in Assam comprising certain tribal areas, with a local legislature and Council of Ministers (inserted by 22nd Amdt 1969; used to form Meghalaya 1972).
**Learn:**
- Fifth Schedule: Governor's special responsibility; Tribes Advisory Council; Scheduled Area orders by President.
- Sixth Schedule: Autonomous District Councils and Regional Councils (Assam, Meghalaya, Tripura, Mizoram).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** 22nd Amdt 1969 (Art 244A); Fifth & Sixth Schedules; Art 275(1) grants; *Samatha v State of AP* (1997) (leases of tribal land to non-tribals struck; ⚠️ verify holding).
**Trap:** Fifth Schedule is for States other than the four NE States; Sixth Schedule is for those four. Art 244A is for Assam only (Meghalaya created under it).
**Back:** *"Tribes = 244: Fifth (Governor's rule) for most States, Sixth (councils) for A-M-T-M."*

### Flashcard Part-XI — Part XI (Relations between Union and States)
**Front:** Part XI — Relations between the Union and the States (Articles 245–263)
**Provision (article by article):**
- Art 245 — extent of laws made by Parliament and State legislatures (Parliament for India; State for State territory).
- Art 246 — subject-matter of laws (246(1) Union List; 246(2) Concurrent; 246(3) State; 246(4) Parliament for non-State areas).
- Art 247 — Parliament may provide for additional courts for better implementation of Union laws.
- Art 248 — residuary legislative power (Parliament); Union List Entry 97 (residuary).
- Art 249 — Parliament may legislate on State List in national interest (2/3 RS resolution; 1 year).
- Art 250 — during Emergency (Art 352), Parliament's power over State List.
- Art 251 — Union law prevails over State law inconsistent with Art 249/250 (or Emergency).
- Art 252 — Parliament may legislate for two or more States by consent.
- Art 253 — Parliament may implement international treaties and agreements.
- Art 254 — repugnancy between Union and State laws in Concurrent List (Union prevails; State law with President's assent prevails in that State).
- Art 255 — requirements as to recommendations and previous sanctions (formal only).
- Art 256 — obligation of States and Union to comply with laws.
- Art 257 — (1) State executive power not to impede the Union executive; (2) Union directions on means of communication of national or military importance (national highways/waterways proviso); (3) protection of railways in the State; (4) extra costs borne by the Union, arbitrator appointed by CJI if no agreement (✅ Wikisource Const. 2020, Part XI).
- Art 258 — power of Union to confer functions on States (with consent) (258A inserted by 7th Amdt 1956: States to confer functions on Union).
- Art 258A — (inserted by 7th Amdt 1956) — States may confer functions on Union with consent.
- Art 259 — (omitted by the 7th Amdt 1956; armed forces in Part B States).
- Art 260 — Union jurisdiction in territories outside India (acquired by treaty/agreement).
- Art 261 — full faith and credit to public acts, records and judicial proceedings in India.
- Art 262 — adjudication of disputes relating to waters of inter-State rivers (Parliament law; Inter-State River Water Disputes Act 1956; SC/Tribunals bar on jurisdiction).
- Art 263 — Inter-State Council (President may establish; investigate, coordinate, advise).
**Learn:**
- Art 262 keeps inter-State river water disputes away from ordinary courts; Parliament sets up tribunals under the 1956 Act.
- Art 263 Council — 1990 Sarkaria Commission recommended; Inter-State Council set up 1990; Punchhi Commission (2010) recommended strengthening (⚠️).
- Art 256/257: Union may give directions to States.
- Art 249: 2/3 RS resolution; valid for one year at a time.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** *State of West Bengal v Union of India* (1962); *State of Rajasthan v Union of India* (1977) (Art 356 centre-state); *Sarkaria Commission* (1988) (Art 263 Council); *Punchhi Commission* (2010); *Art 262* — Inter-State River Water Disputes Act 1956; *S.R. Bommai* (1994) (federalism basic structure).
**Trap:** Art 249 (national interest; 2/3 RS) vs Art 250 (Emergency; no RS needed); Art 254 consent for State repugnant law; Art 262 is about *water* disputes only; Art 263 Council is advisory (not a court).
**Back:** *"Eleven = Centre-State: 246 (three lists), 254 (repugnancy), 262 (water), 263 (council)."*

### Flashcard Part-XII — Part XII (Finance, Property, Contracts and Suits)
**Front:** Part XII — Finance, Property, Contracts and Suits (Articles 264–300A)
**Provision (article by article):**
- Art 264 — interpretation (Finance; Consolidated Fund; Public Account).
- Art 265 — no tax except by authority of law.
- Art 266 — Consolidated Fund of India and of each State; Public Account.
- Art 267 — Contingency Fund of India (Parliament's law; advance; Imprest).
- Art 268 — duties levied by Union but collected and appropriated by States (stamp duties, excises on medical preparations).
- Art 268A — service tax levied by Union, collected and appropriated by the Union and States (originally inserted by the 88th Amdt 2003, never brought into force; omitted by the 101st Amdt 2016, w.e.f. 16-9-2016) (✅ corrected: was 'inserted by 101st'; Wikisource Const. 2020, Part XII).
- Art 269 — taxes levied and collected by Union but assigned to States (inter-State trade; 101st Amdt 2016 added 269A).
- Art 269A — GST on inter-State supply (101st Amdt 2016) — levied by Union; collected & apportioned between Union/States.
- Art 270 — distribution of net proceeds of taxes between Union & States (Finance Commission).
- Art 271 — Union may impose surcharge on certain taxes for Union purposes.
- Art 272 — (omitted by the 80th Amdt 2000, w.e.f. 9-6-2000) (✅ corrected: was 'omitted by 101st/GST'; Wikisource Const. 2020, Part XII).
- Art 273 — grants in lieu of export duty on jute and jute products (Assam, Bihar, Odisha, West Bengal).
- Art 274 — Bills affecting taxes in which States interested need President's recommendation.
- Art 275 — grants from Union to certain States (Sixth Schedule tribal welfare; ⚠️ details).
- Art 276 — taxes on professions, trades, callings and employments (State; capped ₹2,500 per annum).
- Art 277 — savings (taxes continue).
- Art 278 — (omitted by the 7th Amdt 1956, w.e.f. 1-11-1956) (✅ Wikisource Const. 2020, Part XII).
- Art 279 — calculation of net proceeds (for GST).
- Art 279A — GST Council (101st Amdt 2016; Union FM chair; 2/3 weighted voting: Centre 1/3, States 2/3).
- Art 280 — Finance Commission: President sets it up within two years of commencement and reconstitutes it at the expiry of every fifth year (or earlier); a Chairman and four other members (✅ Wikisource Const. 2020, Part XII).
- Art 281 — recommendations of Finance Commission laid before Parliament with Union action memorandum.
- Art 282 — expenditure from Consolidated Fund for public purposes (not Union/State mandatory purpose).
- Art 283 — custody, disbursement and audit of Consolidated Funds.
- Art 284 — accounts of persons collecting public money.
- Art 285 — exemption of Union property from State taxes.
- Art 286 — restrictions on taxes on sale/purchase of goods (outside State sales; import/export; 6th Amdt for inter-State).
- Art 287 — exemption of electricity consumed by Union/State (sold/consumed).
- Art 288 — exemption from State taxation of water or electricity handled by authorities for inter-State rivers/river-valley schemes; a State tax on them needs the President's assent (✅ corrected: was 'State property from Union taxes'; Wikisource Const. 2020, Part XII).
- Art 289 — exemption of State property and income from Union tax.
- Art 290 — adjustment of pensions and salaries.
- Art 290A — annual payment to certain Devaswom Funds (inserted by the 7th Amdt 1956, w.e.f. 1-11-1956; 'Madras' later read as 'Tamil Nadu') (✅ corrected: was 'Kerala/Karnataka'; Wikisource Const. 2020, Part XII).
- Art 291 — privy purses (omitted by 26th Amdt 1971).
- Art 292 — Union borrowing (Consolidated Fund; limits by law).
- Art 293 — State borrowing (State Consolidated Fund; Union consent conditions).
- Art 294 — succession to property, assets, rights, liabilities (Dominion/Part A/B/C States).
- Art 295 — succession: property and liabilities of Part B States vest in the Union if their purposes are Union List purposes; otherwise the corresponding State government succeeds (✅ Wikisource Const. 2020, Part XII).
- Art 296 — property accruing by escheat/lapse/bona vacantia (goes to State or Union).
- Art 297 — things of value in territorial waters, continental shelf, EEZ (40th Amdt 1976 vests in Union).
- Art 298 — power to carry on trade and business (Union/State; 7th Amdt).
- Art 299 — contracts expressed to be made by the President/Governor, executed in the manner directed; neither the President/Governor nor the executing person is personally liable (✅ Wikisource Const. 2020, Part XII).
- Art 300 — suits and proceedings (Union/State sue/sued).
- Art 300A — no person deprived of property except by authority of law (44th Amdt 1978).
**Learn:**
- Art 300A is a constitutional (not Part III) right; *Jilubhai Nanbhai Khachar* (1995) (Art 300A; ⚠️ holding not verified; the insertion by the 44th Amdt s.34, w.e.f. 20-6-1979 is ✅ Wikisource Part XII).
- GST: Art 246A (101st Amdt) gives Union & States concurrent power on GST; Art 279A GST Council; 2/3 weighted voting (Centre 1/3; States 2/3).
- Art 280 Finance Commission (every 5 years); recommends share of tax distribution.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** 26th (Art 291 omitted); 40th (Art 297); 44th (Art 300A); 101st (GST: Arts 246A, 268A, 269A, 279A); 7th (Art 290A, 298, 278?); *Madhav Rao Scindia* (1971) (privy purses); *Jilubhai Nanbhai Khachar* (1995) (Art 300A: ⚠️ verify); *State of WB v Union of India* (1962) (Art 131 original jurisdiction; ⚠️ verify).
**Trap:** Art 300A (right to property) is not a Part III FR (Art 31 omitted by 44th); Art 297 vests resources in Union (40th); Art 268 vs Art 269 (Union levies vs collects-and-assigns); Art 276 professional tax capped at ₹2,500 per annum; Art 279A GST Council votes 2/3 for States.
**Back:** *"Twelve = Money: 265 no tax without law, 280 FC, 279A GST Council, 300A property by law."*

### Flashcard Part-XIII — Part XIII (Trade and Commerce)
**Front:** Part XIII — Trade, Commerce and Intercourse within the territory of India (Articles 301–307)
**Provision (article by article):**
- Art 301 — freedom of trade, commerce and intercourse throughout India (subject to Part XIII).
- Art 302 — Parliament may impose restrictions in public interest.
- Art 303 — restrictions on Union/State legislatures discriminating between States (Parliament may by law allow preference or discrimination where declared necessary to deal with a shortage of goods in any part of India) (✅ Wikisource Const. 2020, Part XIII).
- Art 304 — State may tax goods imported from other States at non-discriminatory rates and impose reasonable restrictions in public interest; a restriction Bill needs the President's prior sanction (✅ Wikisource Const. 2020, Part XIII).
- Art 305 — existing laws and laws providing for State monopolies (4th Amdt 1955 amended).
- Art 306 — (omitted by the 7th Amdt 1956).
- Art 307 — Parliament may appoint an authority to carry out Part XIII (Inter-State Trade and Commerce Commission).
**Learn:**
- Art 301 is a free-trade provision (not a FR), but its restrictions are judicially reviewable.
- Inter-State Trade and Commerce Commission (Art 307) is a constitutional body on paper; not constituted as a body in practice (⚠️ verify).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** 4th Amdt (Art 305); 7th Amdt (Art 306 omitted); 6th Amdt (Art 269/286 inter-State sales tax); *Atiabari Tea Co v State of Assam* (1961) (Art 301 Part XIII; ⚠️ verify holding); *Automobile Transport v State of Rajasthan* (1962) (Art 301 restrictions; ⚠️ verify).
**Trap:** Art 301 freedom is not one of Art 19 freedoms; restrictions under Art 302–304 apply; Art 307 authority not created by default.
**Back:** *"13 = Trade freedom, 301–307: Art 301 free, 302 Parliament restricts, 304 State restricts."*

### Flashcard Part-XIV — Part XIV (Services)
**Front:** Part XIV — Services under the Union and the States (Articles 308–323)
**Provision (article by article):**
- Art 308 — definitions (services under Union/State; recruitment).
- Art 309 — Parliament/State legislature may regulate recruitment & conditions of service (President/Governor may make rules pending law).
- Art 310 — tenure at pleasure of the President/Governor (doctrine of pleasure; exceptions in 310(2) & 311).
- Art 311 — dismissal, removal or reduction in rank only after inquiry and reasonable opportunity (311(2)); exceptions (311(2)(a) conviction; 311(2)(b) impracticable; 311(2)(c) security); 42nd Amdt (s.44, w.e.f. 3-1-1977) omitted words in clause (2) (✅ Wikisource footnotes; the 15th Amdt 1963 had replaced clauses (2) and (3)); the claim that the 44th Amdt removed the 42nd restriction ⚠️ not in fetched text. 
- Art 312 — All-India Services (IAS, IPS, IFS; Rajya Sabha 2/3 resolution; 312(2)).
- Art 312A — power of Parliament to alter conditions of service of certain officers (28th Amdt 1972).
- Art 313 — transitional provision (continuance of existing services).
- Art 314 — omitted by 28th Amdt (1972) (protection of conditions of service of certain officers, e.g., ICS).
- Art 315 — Public Service Commissions for the Union and States (UPSC & SPSC).
- Art 316 — appointment and term of PSC members (6 years or until 65 for Union / 62 for State after 41st Amdt 1976).
- Art 317 — removal and suspension of PSC members (Supreme Court reference).
- Art 318 — power to make regulations as to conditions of service of PSC members.
- Art 319 — post-office bars: UPSC Chairman barred from any further Government of India or State employment; a State PSC Chairman may become UPSC Chairman/member or another State PSC Chairman but takes no other office; a UPSC member may become UPSC Chairman or a State PSC Chairman; a State PSC member may become UPSC member/Chairman or State PSC Chairman (✅ corrected/expanded; Wikisource Const. 2020, Part XIV).
- Art 320 — functions of PSCs (consultation on recruitment, disciplinary matters).
- Art 321 — additional functions (Parliament/Legislature by law).
- Art 322 — expenses of PSC (Consolidated Fund).
- Art 323 — report of PSC (annual report to President/Governor).
**Learn:**
- Art 311 protects civil servants from arbitrary dismissal (enumerated exceptions in 311(2)).
- Art 316: UPSC chairman/member age 65; SPSC member age 62 (41st Amdt).
- Art 312 All-India Services (IAS, IPS, IFS); Art 312A allows changing their conditions (28th Amdt).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** *Union of India v. Tulsiram Patel* (1985) (Art 311; ⚠️ verify); *Chandra Mohan v State of UP* (1966) (⚠️ verify); 28th Amdt (Art 312A, Art 314 omitted); 41st Amdt (Art 316 SPSC age 62); 42nd Amdt (Art 311 proviso).
**Trap:** UPSC chairman retirement age 65 (Art 316), SPSC members age 62 (41st); Art 311 is for civil servants only (not defence personnel under Art 33); Art 314 is omitted, Art 312A is live (inserted 28th).
**Back:** *"14 = Services/Commissions, 308–323: 311 protects, 312 All-India, 315–323 commissions."*

### Flashcard Part-XIVA — Part XIVA (Tribunals)
**Front:** Part XIVA — Tribunals (Articles 323A–323B)
**Provision:**
- Art 323A — Parliament may make laws for adjudication of disputes relating to recruitment and conditions of service of public servants by Administrative Tribunals (Union/State).
- Art 323B — Parliament/State legislatures may make laws for tribunals for other matters (taxation, foreign exchange, industrial disputes, elections (Art 329 bar); land ceiling; tax; etc.).
**Learn:**
- Inserted by the 42nd Amendment (1976) on Swaran Singh Committee advice.
- Art 323A tribunals are Administrative Tribunals; Art 323B tribunals may be for taxation, land reforms, elections (Art 329A excluded), industrial disputes etc.
- *L. Chandra Kumar v Union of India* (1997): High Court writ jurisdiction under Arts 226/227 and SC under Art 32 cannot be excluded; tribunals are subject to High Court scrutiny (not final).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** 42nd (1976); *L. Chandra Kumar* (1997); *Administrative Tribunals Act 1985* (Art 323A); Art 33; Art 226/227/32.
**Trap:** Tribunals under 323A/B cannot oust High Court or SC writ jurisdiction (L. Chandra Kumar); the 42nd's Art 323A(2) exclusion was held unconstitutional for HC/SC.
**Back:** *"XIVA = Tribunals, 323A–B: Chandra Kumar — courts can't be shut out."*

### Flashcard Part-XV — Part XV (Elections)
**Front:** Part XV — Elections (Articles 324–329A)
**Provision (article by article):**
- Art 324 — superintendence, direction and control of elections vested in Election Commission (Union and State elections; President/Governor appoints ECI; CEC removal like SC judge).
- Art 325 — single electoral roll (no exclusion on religion, race, caste or sex).
- Art 326 — elections to House of the People and State Assemblies on adult suffrage (18+ after 61st Amdt 1988).
- Art 327 — Parliament may make laws on elections to legislatures.
- Art 328 — State legislature may make laws on elections (subject to Parliament).
- Art 329 — bar to interference by courts in electoral matters (delimitation/constituencies & election petitions only via election petition).
- Art 329A — (inserted by the 39th Amdt 1975 for PM/Speaker election disputes; omitted by the 44th Amdt 1978, s.36, w.e.f. 20-6-1979 ✅ Wikisource Const. 2020, Part XV; struck in *Indira Gandhi v Raj Narain* (1975) for the part that barred judicial review (⚠️ verify).
**Learn:**
- Art 324 ECI powers continue beyond statutory provisions; Art 324(5) CEC removal like SC judge.
- Art 326: voting age reduced from 21 to 18 (61st Amdt 1988, w.e.f. 28-3-1989 ✅ Wikisource Const. 2020, Part XV).
- Art 329(a) delimitation bar; 329(b) election petition via election petition (Representation of the People Act 1951).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** *Indira Gandhi v Raj Narain* (1975) (basic structure: free and fair elections; 39th Amdt struck); *Kihoto Hollohan* (1992) (Tenth Schedule decisions by Speaker judicially reviewable); *Mohinder Singh Gill* (1978) (ECI's power under Art 324 to ensure fairness); 61st Amdt (1988) (voting age 18).
**Trap:** Art 324(2) ECI is the constitutional authority; Art 326 adult suffrage is for House of the People & State Assemblies; Art 329(b) bars courts except election petitions; Art 329A is omitted (not a live Article) — the Ninth Sch "validation" clause was a 39th Amdt device.
**Back:** *"15 = Elections, 324 starts: 325 one roll, 326 adult suffrage, 329 courts out, 329A gone (1978)."*

### Flashcard Part-XVI — Part XVI (Special Provisions for Certain Classes)
**Front:** Part XVI — Special Provisions relating to Certain Classes (Articles 330–342A)
**Provision (article by article):**
- Art 330 — reservation of seats for SC/ST in House of the People (Lok Sabha).
- Art 331 — nomination of Anglo-Indian members to LS (ended by 104th Amdt 2019).
- Art 332 — reservation of seats for SC/ST in State Legislative Assemblies.
- Art 333 — nomination of Anglo-Indian members to State Assemblies (ended by 104th Amdt 2019).
- Art 334 — reservation and special representation to cease after a fixed period (extended repeatedly; the 104th Amdt 2019, w.e.f. 25-1-2020, fixes eighty years for clause (a) and seventy years for clause (b) from commencement, i.e. 25-1-2030 for SC/ST seats (✅ Wikisource Const. 2020, Part XVI; date by arithmetic from 26-1-1950)).
- Art 335 — claims of SC/ST considered consistent with maintenance of efficiency of administration (services & posts).
- Art 336 — special provision for Anglo-Indians in services (✅ heading checked; Wikisource shows no omission note; 'ended by 104th' ⚠️ not verified).
- Art 337 — special provision for educational grants for Anglo-Indian community (✅ Wikisource Const. 2020, Part XVI; 1956 note on 'Part A or Part B of the First Schedule' words).
- Art 338 — National Commission for SCs (65th Amdt 1990; replaced by NCSC under 89th 2003).
- Art 338A — National Commission for STs (89th Amdt 2003).
- Art 338B — National Commission for Backward Classes (102nd Amdt 2018).
- Art 339 — control of Union over administration of Scheduled Areas and welfare of STs; Commission on tribal areas.
- Art 340 — Commission to investigate conditions of backward classes (basis of Mandal Commission 1979; ⚠️ verify).
- Art 341 — President's notification of Scheduled Castes (State/UT; Parliament may include/exclude).
- Art 342 — President's notification of Scheduled Tribes.
- Art 342A — President's notification of Socially and Educationally Backward Classes (102nd Amdt 2018) — per State/UT (President's list; Parliament alone can alter central list).
**Learn:**
- Art 342A (102nd Amdt 2018): President notifies the SEBC list; Parliament alone may include or exclude groups in the central list (342A(2)). Post-*Indra Sawhney* (1992) the 50% cap and creamy-layer principle still apply.
- The 102nd Amdt also created NCBC (Art 338B).
- 104th Amdt 2019: ended Anglo-Indian nomination and extended SC/ST reservation to 2030.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** 65th (1990, NCSC); 89th (2003, NCST); 102nd (2018, Arts 338B & 342A); 104th (2019, Arts 330/332/334 changes); *Indra Sawhney* (1992) (OBC 50% cap; creamy layer); *Nagaraj v Union of India* (2006) (creamy layer for SC/ST promotion; ⚠️ verify); *Jarnail Singh v Lachhmi Narain Gupta* (2018) (creamy layer not for SC/ST promotion; ⚠️ verify); Art 340 (Mandal).
**Trap:** Art 342A (OBC list in 102nd) vs Art 341/342 (SC/ST lists); Art 334 is time-bound reservation (extended, not permanent); Art 331/333 Anglo-Indian nomination ended 2019 (not 1950).
**Back:** *"Classes 330–342A: 330 LS reservation, 334 time-limit, 341–342 SC/ST lists, 342A OBC list (2018)."*

### Flashcard Part-XVII — Part XVII (Official Language)
**Front:** Part XVII — Official Language (Articles 343–351)
**Provision (article by article):**
- Art 343 — Union official language is Hindi in Devanagari; international form of numerals; 343(2) English continues for 15 years (extended by Official Languages Act 1963 and later). 
- Art 344 — Official Language Commission (President's Commission at expiry of 5 years, and again at 10 years); Parliamentary Committee of 30 members (20 from LS, 10 from RS) (✅ Wikisource Const. 2020, Part XVII).
- Art 345 — State may adopt one or more languages in use in the State or Hindi.
- Art 346 — language for communication between Union and States/States (Hindi/English).
- Art 347 — special provision for language spoken by a substantial section of population (President may direct).
- Art 348 — language of SC & HC proceedings and of Acts/Bills (English until Parliament provides; 348(2) State Governor may authorise Hindi for HC).
- Art 349 — special procedure for laws on language (President's previous sanction; 15 years).
- Art 350 — language for redress of grievances (in any language of the Union or State).
- Art 350A — facilities for instruction in mother tongue at primary stage (7th Amdt 1956).
- Art 350B — Special Officer for Linguistic Minorities (7th Amdt 1956).
- Art 351 — Directive for development of Hindi (promote, enrich, Hindustani/composite culture; 8th Sch languages).
**Learn:**
- Official Languages Act 1963 (English continues as associate official language).
- Art 351 is a directive for Hindi development; Eighth Schedule (22 languages) referenced under Art 344(1), 351.
- Art 343 Devanagari script for Hindi; Art 344 Commission every 10 years (report to President).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Official Languages Act 1963 (English continuation); 7th Amdt (Arts 350A, 350B); Eighth Schedule (Arts 344, 351); 344(1) and 344(2) Committee.
**Trap:** Hindi is Union official language but NOT the "national language" (Constitution does not declare any national language); Art 343 (English 15 years) vs Art 348 (SC/HC English); Art 350A (7th) is primary education mother tongue; Art 347 is special, not a Schedule.
**Back:** *"17 = Language, 343–351: Art 343 Hindi (Devanagari), 348 courts English, 350A mother-tongue, 351 development."*

### Flashcard Part-XVIII — Part XVIII (Emergency Provisions)
**Front:** Part XVIII — Emergency Provisions (Articles 352–360)
**Provision (article by article):**
- Art 352 — national emergency (war, external aggression or armed rebellion, as amended by 44th; written Cabinet advice under 352(3)); may be proclaimed for whole or part of India; Parliamentary approval within one month (44th).
- Art 353 — effect of national emergency (Union executive direction to States; Parliament's power to make laws for the State).
- Art 354 — application of distribution of revenues during emergency (Art 275 & 276 changes; ⚠️ verify).
- Art 355 — duty of the Union to protect States against external aggression and internal disturbance and to ensure governance per Constitution.
- Art 356 — President's Rule (State emergency) on failure of constitutional machinery; Parliament approval within 2 months; max 1 year (extension beyond 1 year needs special conditions: national emergency + ECI certificate under 44th Amdt).
- Art 357 — exercise of legislative powers under Art 356 proclamation (President/Parliament).
- Art 358 — suspension of Art 19 during national emergency (only for war or external aggression, after 44th).
- Art 359 — suspension of enforcement of FRs (Arts 20 & 21 cannot be suspended; 44th Amdt).
- Art 360 — financial emergency (threat to financial stability; Parliament approval within 2 months; Union directions to States; salaries of judges may be cut).
**Learn:**
- National emergency: 1962 (Chinese aggression), 1971 (Pakistan war), 1975–77 (internal disturbance, withdrawn 1977 by 44th safeguards).
- Art 356 (Bommai): proclamation justiciable; Governor's report; floor test.
- Financial emergency never proclaimed.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** *S.R. Bommai v Union of India* (1994) (Art 356 judicial review; floor test; secularism basic structure); *Minerva Mills*; *ADM Jabalpur* (1976) (habeas corpus suspended under Art 359 — later overruled by *K.S. Puttaswamy* (2017) ✓); 38th (1975) (judicial review excluded — later reversed by 44th); 42nd (1976) (Art 355 duty); 44th (1978) (safeguards: Art 352 'armed rebellion', Art 20–21 non-suspendable, Art 359 limits, Art 352 written Cabinet advice).
**Trap:** Art 352 — 'armed rebellion' replaced 'internal disturbance' (44th); Art 358 Article 19 suspended only for external aggression/war; Art 359 cannot suspend Art 20–21; Art 356 is State emergency (not Art 352); financial emergency (Art 360) never declared.
**Back:** *"18 = Emergency, 352–360: 352 national, 356 State, 360 financial; 20–21 never suspended."*

### Flashcard Part-XIX — Part XIX (Miscellaneous)
**Front:** Part XIX — Miscellaneous (Articles 361–367)
**Provision (article by article):**
- Art 361 — protection of President and Governors (immunity from court in exercise of powers; no criminal proceedings in personal capacity; civil suit only after 2-month notice).
- Art 361A — protection of publication of proceedings of Parliament and State legislatures (fair and accurate reports; inserted by 44th Amdt 1978).
- Art 361B — disqualification of members who hold remunerative political posts (91st Amdt 2003).
- Art 362 — (omitted by 26th Amdt 1971; rights and privileges of rulers).
- Art 363 — bar on courts in disputes arising from treaties/agreements of former rulers (26th Amdt: 363A).
- Art 363A — recognition of former rulers ceased; privy purses abolished (26th Amdt 1971).
- Art 364 — major ports & aerodromes (President's power; Union law).
- Art 365 — failure to comply with Union directions: President may presume that State cannot carry on government under Constitution (basis for Art 356).
- Art 366 — definitions (e.g., "Scheduled Caste", "Scheduled Tribe", "State", "Indian State"; 366(29A) GST deemed sale — 46th Amdt 1982 ✓).
- Art 367 — interpretation (General Clauses Act 1897 applies to the Constitution, with adaptations).
**Learn:**
- Art 361(1) immunity for acts in exercise of powers; Art 361(2) no criminal proceedings in court during term; Art 361(3) no arrest/imprisonment.
- Art 365 (failure to comply with Union directions) is the textual basis for Art 356 proclamations (criticised in *Bommai*).
- Art 366(29A) (46th Amdt) defines GST-related "deemed sale".
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** 26th (Art 362 omitted; 363A); 91st (Art 361B); *S.R. Bommai* (1994) (Art 356 & 365); 44th (Art 361A); 46th (Art 366(29A)).
**Trap:** Art 361 protects the President/Governor only in exercise of powers; Art 361B (disqualification, 91st) for political posts; Art 365 is a presumption, not an independent power; Art 366 definitions are in Part XIX (not Part III).
**Back:** *"19 = Misc., 361–367: 361 immunity, 361B political posts, 365 Art 356 trigger, 367 interpretation."*

### Flashcard Part-XX — Part XX (Amendment of the Constitution)
**Front:** Part XX — Amendment of the Constitution (Article 368 only)
**Provision:**
- Art 368(1) — Parliament may amend the Constitution (power and procedure).
- Art 368(2) — amendment bill passed by each House by a majority of total membership and by 2/3 of members present and voting; amendments affecting federal matters need ratification by at least half of State legislatures; President's assent mandatory (24th Amdt 1971).
- Art 368(3) — Art 13 does not apply to Art 368 amendments (24th Amdt).
- Art 368(4)–(5) — (42nd Amdt 1976; struck in *Minerva Mills* 1980: unlimited amending power not valid).
**Learn:**
- *Shankari Prasad* (1951) and *Sajjan Singh* (1965) upheld Parliament's amendment power; *Golak Nath* (1967) reversed; 24th (1971) restored; *Kesavananda* (1973) basic structure.
- Art 368 amendments by simple majority are NOT possible; special majority needed (2/3 of present & voting + majority of total).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** *Shankari Prasad v Union* (1951); *Sajjan Singh* (1965); *Golak Nath* (1967); *Kesavananda Bharati* (1973); *Minerva Mills* (1980); *I.R. Coelho* (2007) (laws in Ninth Schedule post-24 April 1973 review); 24th (1971); 42nd (1976; 368(4)-(5)); 44th (1978).
**Trap:** Art 368 has no separate amendment power for the Basic Structure; ratification is only for federal matters (not all amendments); Art 368(4)-(5) (42nd) struck as unconstitutional (Minerva Mills).
**Back:** *"20 = Amend, one Article: 368; 2/3 + majority, half-State ratification for federal matters, basic structure limits."*

### Flashcard Part-XXI — Part XXI (Temporary, Transitional and Special Provisions)
**Front:** Part XXI — Temporary, Transitional and Special Provisions (Articles 369–392)
**Provision (article by article; selected):**
- Art 369 — for a period of five years Parliament may legislate on specified State-List matters (trade and commerce; production, supply and distribution of cotton textiles, coal, iron, steel and similar items) as if they were Concurrent-List matters (✅ corrected: was 'Bombay / Madras / Orissa area'; Wikisource Const. 2020, Part XXI).
- Art 370 — temporary provisions for Jammu and Kashmir (special status; Presidential orders; concurrence of State govt; ended by the 2019 Presidential Order C.O. 272).
- Art 371 — special provisions for Maharashtra & Gujarat (Vidarbha, Marathwada, Saurashtra, Kutch development boards).
- Art 371A — Nagaland (13th Amdt 1962).
- Art 371B — Assam tribal areas committee (22nd Amdt 1969).
- Art 371C — Manipur hill areas committee (27th Amdt 1971).
- Art 371D — Andhra Pradesh equitable opportunities (32nd Amdt 1973/74).
- Art 371E — central university in Andhra Pradesh (32nd Amdt 1973/74).
- Art 371F — Sikkim (36th Amdt 1975).
- Art 371G — Mizoram (53rd Amdt 1986).
- Art 371H — Arunachal Pradesh (55th Amdt 1986).
- Art 371I — Goa (56th Amdt 1987).
- Art 371J — Hyderabad-Karnataka region (98th Amdt 2012).
- Art 372 — continuance of existing laws; adaptation powers of President.
- Art 372A — power of President to adapt laws (inserted by the 7th Amdt 1956, s.23, w.e.f. 1-11-1956; adaptation orders before 1 Nov 1957) (✅ Wikisource Const. 2020, Part XXI).
- Art 373 — power of President to make orders about preventive detention.
- Art 374 — provisions as to Judicial Committee and Federal Court judges.
- Art 375 — courts, authorities and officers continue.
- Art 376 — provisions as to judges of High Courts (partly amended 1st Amdt; ⚠️ verify).
- Art 377 — provisions as to Comptroller and Auditor-General.
- Art 378 — provisional provisions for Public Service Commissions (✅ corrected: 378A is not a PSC provision, see below).
- Art 378A — (inserted by the 7th Amdt 1956, s.24, w.e.f. 1-11-1956) duration of the Andhra Pradesh Legislative Assembly (five years from the date in s.29 of the States Reorganisation Act 1956) (✅ corrected: was 'Andhra Pradesh PSC'; Wikisource Const. 2020, Part XXI).
- Art 379–391 — omitted by the 7th Amdt 1956, s.29 and Sch., w.e.f. 1-11-1956 (✅ Wikisource Const. 2020, Part XXI).
- Art 392 — power of President to remove difficulties.
**Learn:**
- Art 370 is "temporary, transitional and special" (hence the Part XXI title); 2019 Presidential Order C.O. 272 (⚠️ Wikisource Part XXI footnote cites 'C.O. 273' for the 5-Aug-2019 declaration; number conflict unresolved) and J&K Reorganisation Act 2019; Constitution Bench (2023) upheld the 2019 Presidential Orders (⚠️ verify date and bench composition before stating).
- Art 371 family: 371A–J special state provisions; 371J (98th Amdt 2012) Hyderabad-Karnataka.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** *Constitution Bench on Art 370 (2023)* ⚠️; 13th/22nd/27th/32nd/36th/53rd/55th/56th/98th Amdts (Art 371 family); 2019 J&K reorganisation.
**Trap:** Art 370 was 'temporary' and special; Art 371 family gives special development boards (Maharashtra & Gujarat), not statehood; Art 371J added in 2012 (98th); Art 372 continuance of laws; Art 392 power to remove difficulties.
**Back:** *"21 = Temporary and special, 369–392: 370 J&K gone (2019), 371A–J states' special pacts."*

### Flashcard Part-XXII — Part XXII (Short title, Commencement, Hindi text, Repeals)
**Front:** Part XXII — Short title, commencement, authoritative text in Hindi and repeals (Articles 393–395)
**Provision:**
- Art 393 — short title: "Constitution of India".
- Art 394 — commencement (26 January 1950).
- Art 394A — authoritative Hindi text of the Constitution (58th Amdt 1987; ⚠️ verify).
- Art 395 — repeals the Indian Independence Act 1947 and Government of India Act 1935 (with amendments; ⚠️ verify exact list).
**Learn:**
- 26 January 1950 commencement (Lahore resolution 1930 Purna Swaraj day chosen; ✓).
- Art 394A (58th Amdt 1987) allows authoritative Hindi text.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 394 (26 Jan 1950); Art 395 (repeal of GoI Act 1935); 58th Amdt (Art 394A).
**Trap:** Art 394A is inserted in 1987 — the Hindi text is authoritative (not Art 351); Art 395 repeals the 1935 Act (not the 1919 Act).
**Back:** *"22 = Last: 393 name, 394 commencement (26 Jan 1950), 395 repeal 1935 Act."*

## B. SCHEDULES

### Flashcard Sch-1 — First Schedule
**Front:** First Schedule — Names and territories of the States and Union Territories
**Provision:** Lists each State and UT with its territory (Arts 1 and 4 refer). Current count: **28 States and 8 Union Territories**.
**Learn:**
- Telangana created as the 29th State in 2014 (Andhra Pradesh Reorganisation Act 2014 — a Parliamentary law under Art 3).
- J&K (2019) was split into the UT of J&K (with legislature) and the UT of Ladakh (without), and the State count fell to 28 (after Telangana, 28 → 29 → 28).
- Dadra & Nagar Haveli and Daman & Diu merged into one UT in 2020; UT count stayed at 8.
- A reorganisation by ordinary law needs no Art 368 amendment (Art 4).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** 7th Amdt (1956, the reorganisation schedule); 9th Amdt (1960, Berubari); 10th (1961, DNH); 12th (1961, Goa, Daman & Diu); 14th (1962, Puducherry); 36th (1975, Sikkim); 2019 J&K Reorganisation Act; 2020 DNH & DD merger.
**Trap:** Telangana (2014) was created by Parliament under Art 3 without an Art 368 amendment; the 29th-State count is a transitional figure — state "28 States" today. Art 1 says "Union of States"; the First Schedule is the list.
**Back:** *"First = the map: 28 States, 8 UTs; Telangana 2014, J&K-Ladakh 2019, DNH-DD 2020."*

### Flashcard Sch-2 — Second Schedule
**Front:** Second Schedule — Emoluments, allowances, privileges and leave of constitutional office-holders
**Provision:** Salaries/allowances (Parts A–E) for: President (Art 59(2)? — Art 59 and Second Sch Part A); Governors (Art 158); Speaker & Deputy Speaker of Lok Sabha and Chairman & Deputy Chairman of RS (Arts 97 & 186 analogues); Ministers (Art 75(6) and 164(5)); Judges of SC (Art 125) and HC (Art 221); CAG (Art 148(3)).
**Learn:**
- Parts A, B, C, D, E in the Schedule correspond to the President/Governors, Speaker/Chairman, Ministers/Legislators, Supreme and High Court judges and CAG (⚠️ verify exact part-letter mapping).
- Salaries are charged on the Consolidated Fund of India/State (Art 112(3)(a) & 202(3) analogues).
- Only Parliament/legislature can change salaries of judges by law; no reduction in judges' emoluments during service in a way that is detrimental (Art 125 / 221).
