**Cases / Amendments / Links:** 42nd Amdt (1976) changed Art 74 (Council binding) — not salary; the Second Schedule was amended by the Salary and Allowances Acts (e.g., Parliament Salaries Act); *All India Judges' Association* (1992) (judicial emoluments; ⚠️ verify).
**Trap:** The Second Schedule (salaries) vs Third (oaths); Art 59 (President) and Art 158 (Governor) — Art 75(6) for PM & ministers (⚠️ verify); the Second Schedule is a list of Articles it serves, not the Constitution's salary text.
**Back:** *"Second = Salaries of the Seconded: President, Governors, Speakers, Judges, CAG."*

### Flashcard Sch-3 — Third Schedule
**Front:** Third Schedule — Forms of oath or affirmation
**Provision:** Forms for: Union Ministers (Art 75(4)); Parliament members (Art 99); SC judges (Art 124(6)); CAG (Art 148(2)); State Ministers (Art 164(3)); State legislature members (Art 188); HC judges (Art 219). Also: President (Art 60), VP (Art 69), Governors (Art 159) — listed in Part 1 of the Schedule.
**Learn:**
- Oath to "bear true faith and allegiance to the Constitution of India" and "uphold the sovereignty and integrity of India" (inserted by the 16th Amdt, 1963 — the 16th added "sovereignty and integrity" to the oath forms).
- Affirmation allowed for those who do not believe in God.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** 16th Amdt (1963) (sovereignty & integrity in oaths); Art 99 (MPs take oath before the President or someone appointed); Art 188 (MLAs before Governor).
**Trap:** Third Schedule (oaths) vs Fourth (Rajya Sabha seats); Art 99 (Parliament members) — members take oath before the President (not Speaker). Oath "sovereignty and integrity" came with the 16th.
**Back:** *"Third = Third-degree oaths: Ministers, MPs, judges, CAG, MLAs — all swear 'sovereignty and integrity'."*

### Flashcard Sch-4 — Fourth Schedule
**Front:** Fourth Schedule — Allocation of seats in the Council of States (Rajya Sabha)
**Provision:** Allocation of Rajya Sabha seats to each State and UT (Arts 4(1) and 80(2)). Maximum 250 seats: 238 representatives of States/UTs + 12 nominated (Art 80(1)(a)). **Currently 245 seats**: 233 elected (229 States + 4 UTs) + 12 nominated (⚠️ verify exact current split).
**Learn:**
- Uttar Pradesh has the largest allocation; the Rajya Sabha is a permanent House (Art 83(1)): one-third retire every second year.
- 12 members nominated by the President for literature, science, art, social service (Art 80(3)).
- Art 4 — allocation changes by law are not Art 368 amendments.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** 14th Amdt (1962, Puducherry Art 81/ Sch 1,4); 36th Amdt (1975, Sikkim seat); 7th Amdt (1956, Sch 4 reorganisation); 2019 J&K & Ladakh changes.
**Trap:** Fourth = RS seats (not LS: Art 81 is LS); RS max 250 vs current 245; 12 nominated members (Art 80(3)), not elected; Art 80(4) is the allocation of RS seats through Fourth Sch.
**Back:** *"Fourth = Four upper seats: max 250 (238+12), now 245 (233+12)."*

### Flashcard Sch-5 — Fifth Schedule
**Front:** Fifth Schedule — Administration and control of Scheduled Areas and Scheduled Tribes (Art 244(1))
**Provision:** Applies to Scheduled Areas and Scheduled Tribes in **States other than Assam, Meghalaya, Tripura and Mizoram** (Art 244(1)). Governor's special responsibility: report to President; Tribes Advisory Council (20 members, 3/4 ST MLAs; ⚠️ verify number); Governor may make regulations (para 5) with President's assent for land transfer, money-lending, etc.
**Learn:**
- President declares Scheduled Areas by order; Scheduled Areas exist in ~10 States (⚠️ verify list: AP, Telangana, Chhattisgarh, Gujarat, Himachal Pradesh, Jharkhand, Madhya Pradesh, Maharashtra, Odisha, Rajasthan).
- Para 5: Governor may prohibit or restrict transfer of land by tribals and regulate money-lending.
- Parliament can extend Panchayat law to Fifth Schedule areas (Art 243M(4)(b) — PESA Act 1996 ✓).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** *Samatha v State of AP* (1997) (tribal land leases to non-tribal/industries struck; ⚠️ verify); PESA Act 1996 (Panchayats (Extension to Scheduled Areas) Act); Art 244; Art 275(1); Art 339(2).
**Trap:** Fifth = States other than the four NE States (not Sixth); Governor's special responsibility (not the Chief Minister's); PESA extends Panchayats but the Fifth Schedule itself is not Art 243.
**Back:** *"Five-state tribes: Governor's special responsibility, not for the four NE States."*

### Flashcard Sch-6 — Sixth Schedule
**Front:** Sixth Schedule — Administration of Tribal Areas in Assam, Meghalaya, Tripura and Mizoram (Arts 244(2), 275(1))
**Provision:** Autonomous District Councils (ADCs) and Autonomous Regional Councils (ARCs) with powers to make laws on land, forests, village administration, marriage/divorce, inheritance of tribal customary law, money-lending, etc.; Governor may divide areas; Sixth Schedule councils get grants under Art 275(1).
**Learn:**
- Created for Assam Hills (Bordoloi Committee 1947); ADCs/ARCs; the State Government cannot override the council's laws without consent (⚠️ verify).
- Councils in Wikipedia's Sixth Schedule table (✅ names and formation years): Assam: Bodoland Territorial Council (Kokrajhar, 2003), North Cachar Hills (Haflong, 1951; Dima Hasao name ⚠️), Karbi Anglong (Diphu, 1952); Meghalaya: Garo Hills (Tura), Jaintia Hills (Jowai), Khasi Hills (Shillong), all 1973; Mizoram: Chakma (Kamalanagar), Lai (Lawngtlai), Mara (Siaha), all 1972 (✅ corrected: Lai and Mara were missing); Tripura: Tripura Tribal Areas ADC (Khumulwng, 1982; Sixth Schedule status granted 1984).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Sixth Schedule (1949 reference); Art 244A (Meghalaya via 22nd Amdt); Bodoland Accord (2003) (Bodoland Territorial Council) ⚠️; Art 244(2).
**Trap:** Sixth = four NE States councils; Fifth = Governor; the Sixth Schedule is in Art 244(2) (not 244(1)); Art 244A (autonomous State, Assam tribal areas) is separate.
**Back:** *"Six = councils for the four NE States: A-M-T-M."*

### Flashcard Sch-7 — Seventh Schedule
**Front:** Seventh Schedule — Division of legislative powers: Union List, State List and Concurrent List (Art 246)
**Provision:**
- **Union List** (Parliament exclusive; Art 246(1)): defence, foreign affairs, currency, atomic energy, railways, banking, RBI, Union taxes, etc.
- **State List** (State exclusive; Art 246(3)): police, public order, prisons, agriculture, land, health, local government, public health, etc.
- **Concurrent List** (both; Art 246(2); Union prevails under Art 254): criminal law and procedure, marriage and divorce, economic & social planning, trade unions, education (after 42nd), population control & family planning (42nd), forests, wild animals (42nd), etc.
- Residuary power (Art 248; Union List Entry 97) — with Parliament.
**Counts (⚠️ VERIFY):**
- Union List: **100 entries** is the standard count cited in the repository's federal owner (12_Federal-System, "Union List 100") — ⚠️ the Wikipedia Seventh Schedule page is internally inconsistent (intro 97; Union section '99 (originally 97)'; listing ends at entry 97), so cite as "100 (standard count, verify against Schedule text)". Originally 97; lettered additions (92A from 6th/46th Amdt; 92B and 92C via 101st GST Amdt 2016 ⚠️ verify exact entries).
- State List: **61 entries** (✅ Wikipedia State section: '61 (originally 66)'; its intro says 66) — the 42nd Amdt (1976) removed 5 subjects from State List to Concurrent List (Education; Administration of Justice (constitution & organisation of courts); Forests; Protection of wild animals & birds; Weights & measures) ⚠️ verify entries.
- Concurrent List: **52 entries** (✅ Wikipedia: '47 ... now it has 52'); the 42nd Amdt (1976) added five Concurrent entries 11-A, 17-A, 17-B, 20-A, 33-A (✅ listed in Wikipedia; Wikipedia does not expressly attribute 20-A to the 42nd ⚠️).
- GST (101st Amdt 2016): Union List entries 92 (newspaper taxes) and 92C (services) are shown as omitted, 92A/92B text retained (✅ Wikipedia Seventh Schedule article; any change to Union entry 84 ⚠️ not confirmed); State List entries 52 and 55 tagged to the 101st and 54 substituted (✅ Wikipedia; exact substituted wording ⚠️).
**Learn:**
- Union prevails in Concurrent List conflict (Art 254(1)); State law prevails with President's assent (254(2)).
- Entries 1–97 of Union List; residuary goes with Parliament (Art 248).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** *State of West Bengal v Union of India* (1962) (⚠️ verify — Entry 1 'Union Parliament' and Art 131); *Hoechst Pharmaceuticals v State of Bihar* (1983) (Union List entries given broad reading; ⚠️ verify); *Synthetic and Chemicals v State of UP* (1990) (entry interpretation; ⚠️ verify); 42nd Amdt (1976) (Concurrent additions); 101st (GST).
**Trap:** Entry 97 (residuary) is in the Union List, but Art 248 is the power; Education moved to Concurrent (42nd) — do not put it back in State List; the State List count is 61 (not 66) only after 42nd; "100/61/52" are standard counts — mark them ⚠️ if a question quotes different numbers.
**Back:** *"Seventh = Seventh heaven: U(100) S(61) C(52) — 42nd moved five State subjects to Concurrent; residuary to Parliament."*

### Flashcard Sch-8 — Eighth Schedule
**Front:** Eighth Schedule — Official languages recognised by the Constitution (Arts 344(1), 351)
**Provision:** 22 languages in the Schedule (✅ alphabetical order as in Wikipedia's Eighth Schedule table):
1. Assamese — original 14 (1950)
2. Bengali — original (1950)
3. Bodo — 92nd Amdt (2003)
4. Dogri — 92nd Amdt (2003)
5. Gujarati — original (1950)
6. Hindi — original (1950)
7. Kannada — original (1950)
8. Kashmiri — original (1950)
9. Konkani — 71st Amdt (1992)
10. Maithili — 92nd Amdt (2003)
11. Malayalam — original (1950)
12. Manipuri — 71st Amdt (1992)
13. Marathi — original (1950)
14. Nepali — 71st Amdt (1992)
15. Odia (originally "Oriya") — original (1950); renamed by 96th Amdt (2011)
16. Punjabi — original (1950)
17. Sanskrit — original (1950)
18. Santali — 92nd Amdt (2003)
19. Sindhi — 21st Amdt (1967)
20. Tamil — original (1950)
21. Telugu — original (1950)
22. Urdu — original (1950)
**Learn:**
- 14 original → 15 (Sindhi, 21st, 1967) → 18 (Konkani, Manipuri, Nepali, 71st, 1992) → 22 (Bodo, Dogri, Maithili, Santali, 92nd, 2003). Oriya → Odia by 96th (2011); no count change.
- Eighth Schedule languages do not make a language "official" in a State (Art 345 governs State official language); the Schedule also is the basis for Sahitya Akademi and the Language Commission.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** 21st (1967), 71st (1992), 92nd (2003), 96th (2011); Art 344(1) (Commission reports); Art 351 (Hindi development; Eighth Schedule language enrichment).
**Trap:** 96th Amdt (2011) — renaming, not addition; Sindhi (21st) is the first addition; 71st adds three (Konkani, Manipuri, Nepali), 92nd adds four (Bodo, Dogri, Maithili, Santali); Eighth Schedule ≠ official language of a State.
**Back:** *"Eight-een + 4 = 22: 14 original, Sindhi 1967, three in 1992, four in 2003."*

### Flashcard Sch-9 — Ninth Schedule
**Front:** Ninth Schedule — Laws protected against judicial review (Art 31B; added by the 1st Amendment, 1951)
**Provision:** Laws placed in the Schedule are deemed valid notwithstanding inconsistency with FRs (Art 31B); they are not open to challenge under Part III. Listed laws are mainly land reform and tenancy laws (initial 13 Acts in 1951; ⚠️ count not retrieved from the fetched Wikipedia text).
**Learn:**
- 1st Amdt (1951) created Arts 31A & 31B and the Ninth Schedule.
- *I.R. Coelho v State of Tamil Nadu* (2007): laws added after **24 April 1973** (Kesavananda date) are open to review if they violate FRs or the basic structure; laws protected before that date remain protected (✅ partial: Wikipedia Ninth Schedule note confirms the 2007 Coelho holding that Ninth Schedule laws can be reviewed for violation of Arts 14, 15, 19, 21 or the basic structure; the 24 April 1973 cut-off ⚠️ not in fetched text).
- Other amendments adding to Ninth: 4th (1955), 17th (1964; 44 Acts), 29th (1972), 34th (1974; 20 Acts), 39th (1975), 40th (1976).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** *Shankari Prasad* (1951); *Sajjan Singh* (1965); *Golak Nath* (1967); *Kesavananda* (1973) (basic structure); *Waman Rao* (1981) (pre-1973 laws protected; ⚠️ verify); *I.R. Coelho* (2007).
**Trap:** Ninth Schedule is a shield only for laws added before 24 April 1973 (Waman Rao, Coelho); Art 31B vs Art 31A (31A is the saving clause for estates; 31B is the Schedule); 39th Amdt added Acts to Ninth too.
**Back:** *"Ninth = Nine-tight shield: Art 31B; after 24 April 1973 the courts can look (Coelho 2007)."*

### Flashcard Sch-10 — Tenth Schedule
**Front:** Tenth Schedule — Anti-defection law (Arts 102(2), 191(2))
**Provision:** Disqualification of MPs/MLAs for defection: (a) voluntarily giving up membership of the political party; (b) voting or abstaining contrary to party whip without permission (not condoned within 15 days); (c) independent members joining a party; (d) nominated members joining a party after 6 months. Para 3 (split protection, one-third) deleted by 91st Amdt (2003); Para 4 (merger, two-thirds of legislature party) retained.
**Learn:**
- 52nd Amdt (1985) inserted the Tenth Schedule. Decision by Speaker/Chairman (Para 6); Art 103 & 192 for President/Governor decision if referral; Kihoto Hollohan (1992) upheld, but decision judicially reviewable.
- 91st Amdt (2003) also restricted ministers to 15% of Lok Sabha/Assembly strength (Art 75(1A), 164(1A)).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** *Kihoto Hollohan v Zachillhu* (1992); 52nd (1985); 91st (2003); *Ravi Naik* (1994) (merger/disqualification); *Keisham Meghachandra Singh* (2020) (Speaker to decide disqualification petitions within a reasonable period, reported as three months; ⚠️ secondary sources only (citing cases); judgment text and date not retrieved); Art 75(1A), 164(1A) (91st).
**Trap:** Tenth Schedule (anti-defection, 1985) vs 35th Amdt (Sikkim Tenth Schedule — later omitted); the one-third 'split' exception is gone (91st, 2003); 'merger' needs two-thirds (Para 4).
**Back:** *"Tenth = Ten-ure dies if you defect: voluntary give-up, whip violation; split ended 2003; merger needs 2/3."*

### Flashcard Sch-11 — Eleventh Schedule
**Front:** Eleventh Schedule — 29 subjects of Panchayats (Art 243G)
**Provision:** (⚠️ exact 29-entry list NOT verified: Wikipedia and Wikisource schedule pages were not reachable with the subject list) Lists 29 subjects: agriculture (including agricultural extension), land improvement, minor irrigation, animal husbandry, fisheries, forestry, small forest produce, minor forest produce, fuel and fodder, roads, drinking water, rural housing, poverty alleviation, education (primary/secondary schools), libraries, cultural activities, markets and fairs, health & sanitation, family welfare, women and child development, social welfare, welfare of weaker sections (SC/ST), public distribution, maintenance of community assets, etc.
**Learn:**
- Added by 73rd Amdt (1992); Parliament mandates transfer of functions; the State legislature decides the actual devolution.
- The Eleventh Schedule is the list; Art 243G is the power.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** 73rd (1992); Art 243G; Art 243H (taxes); PESA 1996; Art 40 (DPSP).
**Trap:** 29 (Panchayats) vs 18 (Municipalities, Twelfth Schedule); the list is a State-devolution list, not a Union list.
**Back:** *"Eleventh = 11 → villages; 29 subjects (Art 243G)."*

### Flashcard Sch-12 — Twelfth Schedule
**Front:** Twelfth Schedule — 18 subjects of Municipalities (Art 243W)
**Provision:** Lists 18 subjects (⚠️ schedule text not reachable; list below is from a secondary article, which omits 'roads and bridges' and includes 'repair street lights'; treat both points as unverified): (1) regulation of land-use and construction of buildings; (2) urban planning including town planning; (3) planning for economic and social development; (4) water supply for domestic, industrial and commercial purposes; (5) public health, sanitation conservancy and solid waste management; (6) fire services; (7) urban forestry, protection of the environment and ecological aspects; (8) safeguarding weaker sections incl. the handicapped and mentally disabled; (9) slum improvement and upgradation; (10) repair of street lights; (11) urban poverty alleviation; (12) provision of urban amenities (parks, gardens, playgrounds); (13) promotion of cultural, educational and aesthetic aspects; (14) burials, cremations and electric crematoriums; (15) cattle pounds and prevention of cruelty to animals; (16) vital statistics incl. registration of births and deaths; (17) public amenities incl. street lighting, parking lots, bus stops and public conveniences; (18) regulation of slaughter houses and tanneries.
**Learn:**
- Added by 74th Amdt (1992).
- Municipal powers are subject to State law (Art 243W).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** 74th (1992); Art 243W; Art 243X (taxes); Art 243ZD (DPC).
**Trap:** 18 (municipalities) vs 29 (panchayats); Twelfth not Eleventh; Nagar Panchayat is covered by the same schedule.
**Back:** *"Twelfth = 12 → towns; 18 subjects (Art 243W), 74th Amdt 1992."*

## C. AMENDMENTS (1–106)

> Year = year of enactment; commencement from the Wikipedia amendment table (verified against the table, with ⚠️ where sources differ). Articles/Schedules listed as changed per that table.

### Flashcard Amend-001 — 1st Amendment (1951)
**Front:** 1st Amendment (1951) — land reform protection and speech restrictions
**Provision:** Enacted 1951; commenced **18 June 1951**. Changed Arts 15, 19, 85, 87, 174, 176, 341, 342, 372, 376. Inserted **Art 31A, Art 31B** and the **Ninth Schedule**; added **Art 15(4)** (special provision for socially and educationally backward classes, SC and ST).
**Learn:**
- Trigger: *Romesh Thappar* (1950) and *Brij Bhushan* (1950) struck press/speech restrictions; *Kameshwar Singh* (1951) (land reform struck on Art 14/31 grounds).
- Art 19(2) gained "public order", "friendly relations with foreign States" and "incitement to an offence".
- Art 31A saves land-reform / estate laws; Art 31B validates Ninth Schedule laws.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** *Shankari Prasad v Union of India* (1951) (Parliament can amend FRs under Art 368); *Romesh Thappar* (1950); Art 31A/31B; 4th (1955), 17th (1964), 34th (1974), 9th Sch growth.
**Trap:** "Public order" (added 1951) is wider than "security of State" but narrower than "law and order" (per *Ram Manohar Lohia*; the draft's year 1960 is STILL-UNVERIFIED ⚠️ — the case is commonly dated 1966; source fetch failed). Art 15(4) is the first; Art 16(4) is the public employment equivalent (not 1st Amdt).
**Back:** *"First fix: land laws safe (31A/B), speech limited (public order), backward classes helped (15(4))."*

### Flashcard Amend-002 — 2nd Amendment (1952)
**Front:** 2nd Amendment (1952) — Lok Sabha population-ratio clause
**Provision:** Enacted 1952 (LS 15 Dec 1952; RS 19 Dec 1952) (✅ Wikipedia 2nd Amdt); commenced **1 May 1953**, the day of assent (✅ same source). Changed **Art 81(1)(b)** only.
**Learn:**
- Art 81(1)(b) carries the population-ratio of Lok Sabha seats; the 2nd Amdt removed the lower bound "not less than one member for every 750,000 of the population" and kept "not more than one member for every 500,000" (✅ corrected: was "removal of a 7.5 lakh cap"; Wikipedia 2nd Amdt). Clause-letter numbering in the 2020 Wikisource text is STILL-UNVERIFIED ⚠️.
- Later population-based delimitation: 42nd Amdt (1976) froze seats until 2001 (Art 82 proviso); 84th (2001) extended the freeze to after the 2026 census.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 81; Art 82 (readjustment after census); 42nd (1976); 84th (2001).
**Trap:** Do not confuse the 2nd Amdt (Art 81(1)(b)) with the 31st Amdt (1973, LS strength to 545); Lok Sabha seat freeze is a 42nd/84th matter, not 2nd.
**Back:** *"Second = Lok Sabha ratio (81(1)(b)) only; freeze came later."*

### Flashcard Amend-003 — 3rd Amendment (1954)
**Front:** 3rd Amendment (1954) — essential commodities to Concurrent List
**Provision:** Enacted 1954; commenced **22 February 1955**. Changed **Schedule 7** (Concurrent List Entry 33: trade and commerce, production, supply and distribution of foodstuffs, cattle fodder, raw cotton, jute, etc.).
**Learn:**
- Trigger: post-war food scarcity and price control.
- Parliament and State may both legislate; Union law prevails under Art 254.
- Basis of the Essential Commodities Act 1955.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Concurrent List Entry 33; Art 254 (repugnancy); Essential Commodities Act 1955.
**Trap:** Entry 33 (Concurrent) is the essential commodities entry; do not conflate with Entry 33A (weights & measures, 42nd).
**Back:** *"Third = Thali (food and essentials) goes Concurrent (Entry 33)."*

### Flashcard Amend-004 — 4th Amendment (1955)
**Front:** 4th Amendment (1955) — compensation and land reform
**Provision:** Enacted 1955; commenced **27 April 1955**. Changed **Art 31, 31A, 305** and **Ninth Schedule**.
**Learn:**
- Trigger: *West Bengal v Bela Banerjee* (1954) — "compensation" in Art 31(2) meant just equivalent.
- The amendment barred courts from questioning the adequacy of compensation for property taken by the State (Art 31(2)); broadened Art 31A estate definitions; added more land laws to Ninth Schedule.
- The 25th (1971) later replaced "compensation" by "amount".
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** *Bela Banerjee* (1954); *Golak Nath* (1967) (Ninth expansion challenged); 17th (1964) (estates); 25th (1971).
**Trap:** 4th Amdt (1955) vs 25th Amdt (1971) on "compensation" vs "amount"; 4th is about Art 31(2) adequacy bar; not 44th (property deleted).
**Back:** *"Fourth = Four-get compensation challenges (adequacy barred)."*

### Flashcard Amend-005 — 5th Amendment (1955)
**Front:** 5th Amendment (1955) — time limit for States' views on reorganisation
**Provision:** Enacted 1955; commenced **24 December 1955**. Changed **Art 3** — added the proviso that the President may fix a time limit within which a State legislature must express views on a reorganisation Bill.
**Learn:**
- Procedural amendment under Art 3 and Art 4 (Art 3 reorganisation by ordinary law).
- States' views are not binding; time-limit proviso only.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 3, Art 4; 7th Amdt (1956) (reorganisation); 18th (1966) (Art 3 explanation).
**Trap:** Views are advisory — the State does not have a veto; Art 3 does not require an Art 368 amendment.
**Back:** *"Five-xed deadline: President sets the time for States to give views on reorganisation."*

### Flashcard Amend-006 — 6th Amendment (1956)
**Front:** 6th Amendment (1956) — inter-State sales tax
**Provision:** Enacted 1956; commenced **11 September 1956**. Changed **Art 269, Art 286** and **Schedule 7** (Union List entry 92A for tax on inter-State sale or purchase of goods, other than newspapers — ✅ inserted here, Wikipedia 6th Amdt; State List entry 54 made subject to it).
**Learn:**
- Trigger: need for uniform inter-State sales tax; Central Sales Tax Act 1956.
- Art 269 distributes the tax proceeds to States.
- Art 286 restricts State sales tax on inter-State and import/export sales.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Central Sales Tax Act 1956; 46th Amdt (1982) (Art 269, 286 rewritten; GST precursor); 101st (GST, 2016).
**Trap:** 6th Amdt (1956) introduces the inter-State sales tax; the 46th (1982) changed Art 269 and 286 wording further (✅ Wikipedia 6th and 46th Amdt; entry 92A arrived in 1956, not 1982).
**Back:** *"Sixth = Sales tax goes inter-State (Art 269, 286; Sch 7)."*

### Flashcard Amend-007 — 7th Amendment (1956)
**Front:** 7th Amendment (1956) — States Reorganisation (Part A/B/C/D abolished)
**Provision:** Enacted 1956; commenced **1 November 1956**. Changed Arts 1, 3, 49, 80, 81, 82, 131, 153, 158, 168, 170, 171, 216, 217, 220, 222, 224, 230, 231, 232; **repealed Part VII (Art 238)**; inserted **Arts 231 (common High Court), 258A, 290A, 298, 350A, 350B, 371, 372A, 378A**; amended Schedules 1, 2, 4, 7. Created **14 States and 6 UTs** (implementing the States Reorganisation Act 1956).
**Learn:**
- Trigger: *State Reorganisation Commission* (Fazl Ali, 1955) report on linguistic reorganisation.
- Part A/B/C/D classification abolished; Part VII (Art 238) repealed.
- Art 350A (mother-tongue primary instruction) and 350B (Special Officer for Linguistic Minorities) inserted here.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** States Reorganisation Commission (1955); 1956 SRA; Art 231; Art 350A/350B; 14th (1962, Puducherry) & 10th/12th (UTs).
**Trap:** Part VII (Art 238) is repealed; Art 231 is the common High Court; 7th created 14 States (the count in the draft "14 States, 6 UTs" is consistent with the Act); 7th is not 1950.
**Back:** *"Seventh = Seven-ered the old classes: 14 States + 6 UTs; Part VII gone."*

### Flashcard Amend-008 — 8th Amendment (1960)
**Front:** 8th Amendment (1960) — extension of SC/ST and Anglo-Indian reservation (ten years)
**Provision:** Enacted 1959/1960; commenced **5 January 1960**. Changed **Art 334** — extended reservation of seats for SC/ST in Lok Sabha and State assemblies and Anglo-Indian nomination by ten years (to 1970).
**Learn:**
- Art 334 sets the time limit of reservation (originally 10 years from 1950).
- Later extensions: 23rd (1970, to 1980), 45th (1980, to 1990), 62nd (1989), 79th (2000), 95th (2009), 104th (2019, to 2030 and ends Anglo-Indian nomination).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 330, 332, 334; 23rd (1970); 104th (2019).
**Trap:** Reservation in legislatures is time-bound (Art 334), while reservation in services (Art 16(4)) is not time-bound.
**Back:** *"Eighth = Eight (ten) years more: to 1970 (Art 334)."*

### Flashcard Amend-009 — 9th Amendment (1960)
**Front:** 9th Amendment (1960) — Berubari Union transfer to Pakistan
**Provision:** Enacted 1960; commenced **28 December 1960**. Changed **First Schedule** (transfer of Berubari Union No. 12 and others to Pakistan under the Nehru–Noon Agreement 1958).
**Learn:**
- *In re Berubari Union* (1960) (SC advisory opinion, Art 143): cession of territory to a foreign State needs an Art 368 amendment, not an ordinary Art 3 law; the 9th Amdt was passed to comply.
- Berubari remainder and Tin Bigha corridor were settled later by the 100th Amdt (2015) (Land Boundary Agreement).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** *In re Berubari Union* (1960); Art 3/4; 100th Amdt (2015) (Land Boundary Agreement).
**Trap:** The 9th (1960) transferred Berubari; the 100th (2015) implemented the Bangladesh LBA (enclaves exchange). Do not confuse the two.
**Back:** *"Ninth = Berubari goes to Pakistan (Art 368 needed; 100th (2015) finished the LBA)."*

### Flashcard Amend-010 — 10th Amendment (1961)
**Front:** 10th Amendment (1961) — Dadra and Nagar Haveli as a Union Territory
**Provision:** Enacted 1961; commenced **11 August 1961**. Changed **Art 240** and **First Schedule**; DNH (Portuguese enclave) made a UT under Art 240 regulation.
**Learn:**
- Trigger: liberation of DNH from Portuguese rule (1954 de facto; 1961 de jure).
- UT administered by the President through an Administrator under Art 240 regulations.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 240; 2020 merger of DNH and Daman & Diu (Art 1 & First Sch); 12th (1961) (Goa, Daman & Diu).
**Trap:** Dadra and Nagar Haveli (10th, 1961) vs Goa, Daman & Diu (12th, 1961); DNH & DD merged in 2020.
**Back:** *"Tenth = Ten-der DNH joins India: UT under Art 240."*

### Flashcard Amend-011 — 11th Amendment (1961)
**Front:** 11th Amendment (1961) — Vice-President elected by electoral college
**Provision:** Enacted 1961; commenced **19 December 1961**. Changed **Art 66** (VP elected by electoral college of members of both Houses, not joint sitting) and **Art 71** (no election challenged merely due to vacancy in electoral college).
**Learn:**
- The Presidential electoral college (Art 54) includes elected MPs & MLAs; the VP's electoral college includes members of both Houses (elected & nominated). Joint sitting is no longer used for VP.
- Art 71 (election disputes) is in the same amendment.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 54, 66, 71; 39th (1975) (Art 71 PM/Speaker dispute).
**Trap:** VP electoral college (both Houses; no MLAs) vs President's electoral college (MPs + MLAs). Joint sitting is not used for VP.
**Back:** *"Eleventh = Eleven-ate the joint sitting for VP (electoral college of both Houses)."*

### Flashcard Amend-012 — 12th Amendment (1962)
**Front:** 12th Amendment (1962) — Goa, Daman and Diu as a Union Territory
**Provision:** Enacted 1962; commenced **20 December 1961** per the table (Goa liberation December 1961). Changed **Art 240** and **First Schedule**; Goa, Daman & Diu made UT.
**Learn:**
- Goa was liberated from Portuguese rule by Operation Vijay / Indian Army action in December 1961.
- Goa became a State in 1987 (56th Amdt 1987 Art 371I for Goa special provisions; Goa statehood 30 May 1987).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 240; 56th (1987) (Goa special provisions, Art 371I).
**Trap:** 12th is Goa, Daman & Diu (not DNH); Goa's statehood (1987) is not the 12th amendment; 56th (1987) gave Goa Art 371I (✅ Wikipedia 56th Amdt, commenced 30 May 1987, "transition provision to enable formation of state of Goa"; statehood date not on that page).
**Back:** *"Twelfth = Twelve-come Goa: UT under Art 240, statehood later (1987)."*

### Flashcard Amend-013 — 13th Amendment (1962)
**Front:** 13th Amendment (1962) — special provision for Nagaland (Art 371A)
**Provision:** Enacted 1962; commenced **1 December 1962**. Changed **Art 170**; inserted **Art 371A** — special provisions for Nagaland (Nagaland's laws on Naga customary law, land & religion need Nagaland Assembly consent).
**Learn:**
- Trigger: Naga insurgency and the 16-point agreement (1960).
- Nagaland became the 16th State on **1 December 1963** (Nagaland Act 1962).
- Art 371A(1) — Parliament law does not apply to Naga customary law, land/resources, religious/social practice unless Nagaland Assembly consents.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 371A; Nagaland Act 1962; 16th State (1963).
**Trap:** 13th (1962) enacted vs statehood (1963); 371A special provision vs 371G (Mizoram, 53rd, 1986).
**Back:** *"Thirteenth = Thirteen-ly Nagaland special: Naga consent before Parliament's law applies."*

### Flashcard Amend-014 — 14th Amendment (1962)
**Front:** 14th Amendment (1962) — Puducherry as a Union Territory with a legislature
**Provision:** Enacted 1962; commenced **28 December 1962**. Changed **Art 81, 240**, **First & Fourth Schedules**; inserted **Art 239A** (local legislature or council of ministers for certain UTs).
**Learn:**
- Puducherry (formerly French India) merged into India in 1954 (de facto) and became UT in 1962.
- Art 239A is the legislature power for UTs (Puducherry and later others).
- Art 240 regulations and Art 241 HC for UTs also amended.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 239A; Art 240, 241; 27th (1971, Art 239B) & 69th (1991, Art 239AA, Delhi).
**Trap:** 14th created Art 239A; Delhi's legislature came later under the 69th (1991), Art 239AA. Puducherry is a UT, not a State.
**Back:** *"Fourteen-ish: Puducherry UT with a legislature (239A)."*

### Flashcard Amend-015 — 15th Amendment (1963)
**Front:** 15th Amendment (1963) — High Court retirement age and writ jurisdiction
**Provision:** Enacted 1963; commenced **5 October 1963**. Changed **Arts 124, 128, 217, 222, 224, 226, 297, 311, 316, Schedule 7**; inserted **Art 224A** (retired judges may sit in HC); raised HC judges' retirement age from **60 to 62** (Art 217(1)); amended **Art 226(2)** (HC writ jurisdiction where the cause of action arises — not only where the seat is).
**Learn:**
- Trigger: judicial pendency and retirement issues.
- Art 224A (retired judges sitting in HC) is the new insertion.
- Art 226(2) cause of action test: HC can issue writs where cause of action arises wholly or partly.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 217(1) (62 retirement); Art 224A; Art 226(2); 42nd (1976) (Art 226A insertion, later omitted 43rd).
**Trap:** HC judges retire at 62 (15th), SC judges at 65 (no change), UPSC chairman 65 (Art 316). Art 226(1A) is not the text; the cause-of-action clause is Art 226(2).
**Back:** *"Fifteenth = Fifteen: sixty-two for HC judges; writs where cause arises (226(2))."*

### Flashcard Amend-016 — 16th Amendment (1963)
**Front:** 16th Amendment (1963) — sovereignty and integrity as restrictions and oath
**Provision:** Enacted 1963; commenced **5 October 1963**. Changed **Art 19**, **Art 84**, **Art 173**, **Third Schedule**. Added "sovereignty and integrity of India" as ground for reasonable restriction in **Art 19(2)** and (3)/(4); oath/affirmation for MPs, MLAs and candidates to uphold sovereignty and integrity (Art 84, 173; Third Sch forms).
**Learn:**
- Trigger: Chinese aggression (1962) and separatist demands (DMK's Dravida Nadu).
- Restrictions on speech, association and assembly can be imposed to protect sovereignty and integrity.
- Oath forms in Third Schedule updated.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 19(2); Art 84; Art 173; Third Schedule; *Kedar Nath Singh v State of Bihar* (1962) (sedition read narrowly; ⚠️ verify); 16th as the "sovereignty" response.
**Trap:** 16th sovereignty & integrity (Art 19(2)) vs 1st (public order) — two separate grounds. The 16th also added a candidate oath, not only members.
**Back:** *"Sixteenth = Sixteen — swear by sovereignty & integrity: oath + Art 19(2) ground."*

### Flashcard Amend-017 — 17th Amendment (1964)
**Front:** 17th Amendment (1964) — widened 'estate' in Art 31A; Ninth Schedule additions
**Provision:** Enacted 1964; commenced **20 June 1964** (✅ Wikipedia list). Changed **Art 31A** (widened "estate" to include ryotwari and other tenures, with compensation) and **Ninth Schedule** (number of Acts reported as 44 — STILL-UNVERIFIED ⚠️; Wikipedia gives no count).
**Learn:**
- Trigger: land reform laws struck down on ryotwari & non-agricultural tenures.
- Effect: more land laws placed in Ninth Schedule; compensation adequacy barred (as in 4th).
- 17th amendment was challenged in *Golak Nath* (1967).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** *Golak Nath* (1967) (Parliament cannot amend FRs; 17th amendment in issue); 24th (1971) reversal; Kesavananda (1973).
**Trap:** 17th (44 Acts ⚠️) vs 34th (20 Acts ⚠️) — both added Ninth entries; don't mix counts.
**Back:** *"Seventeen — estate widened, Ninth enlarged; Golak Nath (1967) struck the Parliament's power to amend FRs."*

### Flashcard Amend-018 — 18th Amendment (1966)
**Front:** 18th Amendment (1966) — Explanation to Art 3 ("State" includes UT)
**Provision:** Enacted 1966; commenced **27 August 1966**. Changed **Art 3** (added Explanation I: "State" in Art 3 includes a Union Territory).
**Learn:**
- Trigger: Punjab reorganisation 1966 — Haryana created (1 November 1966) out of Punjab.
- Parliament may form a State or UT by splitting; Art 3 now covers UTs.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 3; Punjab Reorganisation Act 1966; 5th (1955, Art 3 proviso).
**Trap:** Haryana created 1 Nov 1966 (after 18th). The Explanation covers "State" in Art 3 only.
**Back:** *"Eighteen-th = Haryana from Punjab; in Art 3 'State' includes UT."*

### Flashcard Amend-019 — 19th Amendment (1966)
**Front:** 19th Amendment (1966) — Election Commission and election matters
**Provision:** Enacted 1966 (assent 11 Dec 1966); commenced **11 December 1966** (✅ Wikipedia 19th Amdt). Changed **Art 324(1)** (Election Commission): deleted the words "including the appointment of election tribunals for the decision of doubts and disputes arising out of or in connection with elections" (✅ corrected: the card's "election tribunal machinery" wording; Wikisource Art 324 footnote confirms the deletion). Effect: election petitions go to the High Courts; RP Act 1951 amended separately (✅ Wikipedia).
**Learn:**
- Trigger: Election tribunal delays and EC's status debates.
- Art 324(1) gives the Election Commission superintendence, direction and control of elections (✅ Wikisource Part XV).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 324; Art 329 (election petitions); Representation of the People Act 1951.
**Trap:** 19th (1966) is not 1961 or 1971; Election Commission details verified (✅ Wikipedia 19th Amdt; Wikisource Art 324).
**Back:** *"Nineteen = Election Commission's machinery (Art 324) — verify clause."*

### Flashcard Amend-020 — 20th Amendment (1966)
**Front:** 20th Amendment (1966) — validation of district judge appointments
**Provision:** Enacted 1966 (assent 22 Dec 1966; commenced **22 December 1966** per the Wikipedia prose, the infobox says 3 Dec) (✅ Wikipedia 20th Amdt). Inserted **Art 233A** — validates appointments and posts of district judges made before the amendment (✅ Wikisource Part VI: "Notwithstanding any judgment, decree or order of any court"; scope of the protected clauses STILL-UNVERIFIED ⚠️).
**Learn:**
- Trigger: a recent SC judgment held district judge appointments in UP and some other States invalid for not following Art 233 (✅ Wikipedia 20th Amdt); the case name is STILL-UNVERIFIED ⚠️ (the page names none).
- Art 233A is a validation clause for district judge appointments.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 233; Art 233A; Art 234.
**Trap:** Art 233A is validation (retrospective), not a general power over district judges.
**Back:** *"Twentieth = Validation for district judges (Art 233A, 1966)."*

### Flashcard Amend-021 — 21st Amendment (1967)
**Front:** 21st Amendment (1967) — Sindhi added to the Eighth Schedule
**Provision:** Enacted 1967; commenced **10 April 1967**. Changed **Eighth Schedule** (Sindhi added; 14 → 15 languages).
**Learn:**
- Trigger: demand for recognition of Sindhi (post-Partition refugee community).
- Subsequent Eighth Schedule additions: 71st (1992): Konkani, Manipuri, Nepali; 92nd (2003): Bodo, Dogri, Maithili, Santali; 96th (2011): Oriya renamed Odia.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Eighth Schedule (Sch 8); Art 344(1); Art 351.
**Trap:** 21st (1967) is the first addition after the original 14; do not confuse 21st with 71st (1992).
**Back:** *"Twenty-first = Sindhi: 14 becomes 15."*

### Flashcard Amend-022 — 22nd Amendment (1969)
**Front:** 22nd Amendment (1969) — autonomous State in Assam tribal areas (Art 244A)
**Provision:** Enacted 1969; commenced **25 September 1969**. Changed **Art 275**; inserted **Art 244A** (formation of an autonomous State within Assam, with a local legislature and Council of Ministers) and **Art 371B** (special committee for Assam tribal areas).
**Learn:**
- Trigger: tribal movements in the Khasi, Jaintia and Garo hills.
- Meghalaya was created as an autonomous State (2 Apr 1970) and became a full State on 21 Jan 1972 (✅ corrected: was "Meghalaya Act 1971"; the Act is the North-Eastern Areas (Reorganisation) Act 1971 — Wikipedia Meghalaya).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 244A; Art 371B; Sixth Schedule; Meghalaya (1972).
**Trap:** 22nd Amdt (1969) is not the 22nd State (Sikkim was the 22nd State in 1975 — 36th Amdt). Art 244A is for Assam only.
**Back:** *"Twenty-two (1969): tribal autonomy in Assam (244A), Meghalaya later."*

### Flashcard Amend-023 — 23rd Amendment (1970)
**Front:** 23rd Amendment (1970) — extension of SC/ST reservation and Anglo-Indian nomination to 1980
**Provision:** Enacted 1970; commenced **23 January 1970**. Changed **Art 330, 332, 333, 334** — extended reservation of SC/ST seats in Lok Sabha and State assemblies (and Anglo-Indian nomination under Arts 331, 333) for another ten years, to **26 January 1980** (✅ corrected: was 25 Jan; Wikipedia 23rd Amdt, which also records the Art 334 wording change from "twenty years" to "thirty years"). Anglo-Indian scope ✅ same source.
**Learn:**
- Trigger: the 1969 expiry approaching (the 8th had extended to 1970).
- Subsequent extensions: 45th (1980, to 1990), 62nd (1989, to 2000), 79th (2000, to 2010), 95th (2009, to 2020), 104th (2019, to 2030).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 334; Art 330, 332; 8th (1960); 45th (1980).
**Trap:** 23rd (1970) extends the 8th; the 23rd does not change Art 16 services reservation; the Anglo-Indian nomination was ended by 104th (2019).
**Back:** *"Twenty-three = Ten more years of legislative seats (to 1980), same Articles as the 8th."*

### Flashcard Amend-024 — 24th Amendment (1971)
**Front:** 24th Amendment (1971) — Parliament's power to amend FRs restored; mandatory assent
**Provision:** Enacted 1971; commenced **5 November 1971**. Changed **Art 13** (added Art 13(4): Art 368 amendments not 'law') and **Art 368** (Parliament's constituent power is express; President must assent to amendment bills).
**Learn:**
- Trigger: *Golak Nath* (1967).
- Effect: restored the power to amend FRs; upheld in *Kesavananda Bharati* (1973), which added the basic structure limit.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** *Golak Nath* (1967); *Kesavananda Bharati* (1973); *Minerva Mills* (1980).
**Trap:** 24th (1971) reverses Golak Nath; Kesavananda then limits it. 24th is not the 42nd (which attempted unlimited amendment and was struck).
**Back:** *"24th = 24 hours: Parliament overturns Golak Nath (Art 13(4), 368)."*

### Flashcard Amend-025 — 25th Amendment (1971)
**Front:** 25th Amendment (1971) — "amount" for compensation; Art 31C
**Provision:** Enacted 1971 (LS passed 1 Dec 1971; RS 8 Dec 1971); assent and commenced **20 April 1972** (✅ corrected: was 8 Dec 1971, the RS passage date; Wikipedia 25th Amdt). Changed **Art 31** (compensation replaced by "amount"; new cl. (2B) inserted, ✅ same source) and inserted **Art 31C** (laws giving effect to DPSP 39(b) and (c) protected from Art 14, 19 and 31; judicial review ouster clause).
**Learn:**
- Trigger: bank nationalisation and *R.C. Cooper* (1970) (Bank nationalisation case struck).
- *Kesavananda* (1973) upheld the first part of Art 31C and struck the 'judicial review ouster' part.
- 42nd (1976) widened Art 31C to all DPSPs; Minerva Mills (1980) struck that extension.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** *Kesavananda* (1973); *Minerva Mills* (1980); *R.C. Cooper* (1970); 42nd (1976); 44th (1978) (property deleted from Part III).
**Trap:** 25th = "amount" and 31C; 44th = Art 31 deleted; 4th = 'compensation not questioned' — the three are linked but distinct.
**Back:** *"Quarter-century of 'amount', not compensation; 31C shields 39(b)–(c) laws, but the ouster part fell in 1973."*

### Flashcard Amend-026 — 26th Amendment (1971)
**Front:** 26th Amendment (1971) — abolition of privy purses and princely privileges
**Provision:** Enacted 1971; commenced **28 December 1971**. Changed **Art 366**; **omitted Art 291 and Art 362**; inserted **Art 363A** (recognition of former rulers ceases; no court to enforce privy purse).
**Learn:**
- Trigger: *Madhav Rao Scindia v Union of India* (1971) — SC struck the Presidential Order abolishing privy purse (Art 14 & 19).
- Effect: privy purses and privileges abolished constitutionally; Art 363A is the protection clause.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** *Madhav Rao Scindia* (1971); Art 291, 362, 363A; 28th? no.
**Trap:** 26th (privy purses) vs 28th (ICS privileges); Art 362 omitted by 26th.
**Back:** *"26th = Princes lose their purses: Arts 291/362 omitted, 363A inserted."*

### Flashcard Amend-027 — 27th Amendment (1971)
**Front:** 27th Amendment (1971) — North-East: Art 239B and Art 371C (Manipur hill areas)
**Provision:** Enacted 1971; commenced **30 December 1971** (and **15 February 1972** for parts, per the table). Changed **Art 239A & 240**; inserted **Art 239B** (ordinance power for UT legislature when not in session) and **Art 371C** (Manipur hill areas committee).
**Learn:**
- ✅ corrected: Wikipedia's 27th Amdt page gives its objective as "Reorganisation of Mizoram into a Union Territory with a legislature and council of ministers"; it ties Arunachal's assembly to the 37th (1975), not the 27th. The role of the North-Eastern Areas (Reorganisation) Act 1971 in the UT creations is STILL-UNVERIFIED ⚠️.
- Art 371C for Manipur hill areas (state committee of MLAs).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 239B; Art 371C; NEAR Act 1971; 37th (1975, Arunachal Assembly); 53rd (1986, Art 371G Mizoram).
**Trap:** 27th (1971) inserted Art 239B for UT legislatures and Art 371C for Manipur; Arunachal's Assembly came by the 37th (1975), Mizoram's statehood by 1987 (⚠️ verify).
**Back:** *"27th = NE gets re-drawn: 239B ordinance for UTs, 371C for Manipur hills."*

### Flashcard Amend-028 — 28th Amendment (1972)
**Front:** 28th Amendment (1972) — ICS-era privileges abolished (Art 314 omitted; Art 312A inserted)
**Provision:** Enacted 1972; commenced **29 August 1972**. **Omitted Art 314** (protection of conditions of service of pre-1950 civil servants, e.g. ICS) and **inserted Art 312A** (Parliament may alter conditions of service of officers in certain services, including All-India services, with power to vary/revoke).
**Learn:**
- Trigger: ICS / Secretary of State appointees' privileges and compensation issues post-1947.
- Art 312A allows Parliament to alter the conditions of service of certain officers.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 312, 312A, 314; 26th (privy purses).
**Trap:** 28th (1972) abolished ICS privileges (Art 314); 26th (1971) abolished privy purses (Art 291) — keep distinct.
**Back:** *"28th = ICS privileges ended: Art 314 gone, 312A in."*

### Flashcard Amend-029 — 29th Amendment (1972)
**Front:** 29th Amendment (1972) — Kerala Land Reforms Acts in the Ninth Schedule
**Provision:** Enacted 1972; commenced **9 June 1972**. Changed **Ninth Schedule** — added two Kerala Land Reforms (Amendment) Acts (1969 and 1971).
**Learn:**
- Trigger: Kerala land reform after the land ceiling and tenancy disputes.
- Effect: shields the Kerala land reform laws from FR challenge (Art 31B).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** *I.R. Coelho* (2007) (laws placed in the Ninth before 24 April 1973 keep their protection); 9th Schedule; Art 31B.
**Trap:** 29th (Kerala, 1972) is before the 24 April 1973 cut-off, so its Acts stay protected under Coelho; 34th (1974) Acts are after the cut-off.
**Back:** *"29th = Kerala's two land acts in the Ninth."*

### Flashcard Amend-030 — 30th Amendment (1972)
**Front:** 30th Amendment (1972) — appeal to Supreme Court in civil cases (Art 133)
**Provision:** Enacted 1972; commenced **9 June 1972**. Changed **Art 133** — civil appeal to the Supreme Court from a High Court lies only where the case involves a **substantial question of law of general importance** (the old money valuation test deleted; the Rs 20,000 figure is STILL-UNVERIFIED ⚠️; the 2020 Wikisource text of Art 133(1) has no money test, ✅).
**Learn:**
- Trigger: SC docket and backlog.
- Effect: appellate jurisdiction becomes law-centric, not valuation-based.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 133; Art 134; 42nd (Art 131A) — not directly.
**Trap:** 30th (civil appeal) vs 42nd (SC supremacy); the valuation test (figure given as Rs 20,000, ⚠️ unverified) existed before the amendment.
**Back:** *"Thirty: only substantial law, not money."*

### Flashcard Amend-031 — 31st Amendment (1973)
**Front:** 31st Amendment (1973) — Lok Sabha strength raised
**Provision:** Enacted 1973; commenced **17 October 1973**. Changed **Art 81, 330, 332** — raised the LS strength from 525 to 545 (✅ Wikipedia 31st Amdt; the 525 + 20 UT-seat split is the standard breakdown, not stated on that page).
**Learn:**
- Trigger: population growth and delimitation.
- Subsequent freeze: 42nd (1976) froze LS seats until 2001; 84th (2001) extended the freeze to 2026 census.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 81; 42nd (1976); 84th (2001); Art 82.
**Trap:** 31st (1973) raised the LS cap; 42nd (1976) froze it; do not mix the freeze with the increase.
**Back:** *"31st = 525 → 545 (LS ceiling), freeze came later."*

### Flashcard Amend-032 — 32nd Amendment (1973)
**Front:** 32nd Amendment (1973) — Telangana and Andhra special provisions (Arts 371D, 371E)
**Provision:** Enacted 1973; commenced **1 July 1974**. Changed **Art 371** and **Sch 7**; inserted **Art 371D** (equitable opportunities for Telangana and Andhra regions in public employment and education) and **Art 371E** (Central University in Andhra Pradesh; Administrative Tribunal).
**Learn:**
- Trigger: Telangana agitation (1969) and the Gentlemen's Agreement (1956).
- Art 371D: Presidential orders for local cadre; 371D(4) for Telangana.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 371D, 371E; 2014 AP Reorganisation Act; 371D's Administrative Tribunal (Art 371D(3)); Central University of AP (2014 Act).
**Trap:** 32nd (1974) vs 36th (Sikkim, 1975); 371D/E are for AP/Telangana (not 371F).
**Back:** *"Thirty-two = Telangana safeguards (371D) and the Andhra university (371E)."*

### Flashcard Amend-033 — 33rd Amendment (1974)
**Front:** 33rd Amendment (1974) — genuine resignation of MPs/MLAs
**Provision:** Enacted 1974; commenced **19 May 1974**. Changed **Art 101(3)(b)** and **Art 190(3)(b)** — the Speaker/Chairman may accept resignation of an MP/MLA only if voluntary and genuine.
**Learn:**
- Trigger: mass resignations by legislators to force elections or political shifts.
- Effect: Speaker must be satisfied that resignation is genuine.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 101(3)(b), 190(3)(b); 52nd (1985) (Tenth Schedule — separate anti-defection).
**Trap:** 33rd (1974) = resignation genuineness; 52nd (1985) = defection disqualification (Tenth Schedule). Do not mix them.
**Back:** *"33rd = Resign? Only if genuine (Speaker must be satisfied)."*

### Flashcard Amend-034 — 34th Amendment (1974)
**Front:** 34th Amendment (1974) — 20 land tenure Acts into the Ninth Schedule
**Provision:** Enacted 1974; commenced **7 September 1974** (✅ Wikipedia list). Changed **Ninth Schedule** — added land tenure and land reform Acts (count of 20 reported; STILL-UNVERIFIED ⚠️, no count on Wikipedia).
**Learn:**
- Trigger: land tenure and tenancy laws across States (Kerala, UP, Punjab, etc.).
- Effect: Art 31B protection.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 31B; Ninth Schedule; 17th (1964, 44 Acts ⚠️); 29th (1972, Kerala); *I.R. Coelho* (2007) (post-24 April 1973 laws reviewable; the 34th (1974) laws are after 24 April 1973 — review open).
**Trap:** 34th (1974) laws were added after 24 April 1973, so under Coelho they are open to review (not automatic immunity). The Ninth count should be verified.
**Back:** *"34th = 20 land Acts: placed after 24 April 1973, so Coelho lets courts test them."*

### Flashcard Amend-035 — 35th Amendment (1975)
**Front:** 35th Amendment (1975) — Sikkim as an Associate State (Art 2A, Tenth Schedule)
**Provision:** Enacted 1974; commenced **1 March 1975**. Changed **Art 80, 81**; inserted **Art 2A** (Sikkim associate State) and added the original **Tenth Schedule** (terms of association for Sikkim).
**Learn:**
- Trigger: Sikkim's 1974 referendum/Chogyal's rule and the Sikkim Assembly's request.
- Sikkim became a full State by the 36th (1975).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 2A (omitted 36th); original Tenth Schedule (omitted 36th); Art 371F (36th); 52nd (1985) — the later Tenth Schedule is anti-defection (different).
**Trap:** 35th's "Tenth Schedule" was the Sikkim Schedule; the anti-defection Tenth Schedule came in 1985 (52nd). Confuse at your peril.
**Back:** *"35th = Sikkim associate state (Art 2A + original Tenth Schedule)."*

### Flashcard Amend-036 — 36th Amendment (1975)
**Front:** 36th Amendment (1975) — Sikkim becomes the 22nd State
**Provision:** Enacted 1975; commenced **26 April 1975** (✅ Wikipedia 36th Amdt). Sikkim's statehood date is **16 May 1975**, a different date (✅ Wikipedia Sikkim article). Changed **Art 80, 81** and **First & Fourth Schedules**; inserted **Art 371F** (special provisions for Sikkim); **omitted Art 2A and the original Tenth Schedule**.
**Learn:**
- Trigger: Sikkim's merger/ statehood after the 1975 referendum.
- Sikkim is the 22nd State (✅ Wikipedia Sikkim article).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 371F; Art 2A & original Tenth (omitted); 35th (1975).
**Trap:** 36th made Sikkim a full State (22nd); 22nd Amdt (1969) is not the 22nd State. Sikkim's statehood date vs commencement date differ.
**Back:** *"36th = Sikkim full State (22nd), Art 2A and original Tenth gone, Art 371F in."*

### Flashcard Amend-037 — 37th Amendment (1975)
**Front:** 37th Amendment (1975) — legislative assembly for Arunachal Pradesh (UT)
**Provision:** Enacted 1975; commenced **3 May 1975**. Changed **Art 239A** and **Art 240** — provides a legislative assembly and council of ministers for the UT of Arunachal Pradesh.
**Learn:**
- Arunachal (NEFA) was a UT from 1972 (NEAR Act 1971) and got an assembly under this amendment.
- Arunachal became a State in 1987 (✅ Wikipedia 55th Amdt, commenced 20 Feb 1987, refers to "formation of the state of Arunachal Pradesh"; exact statehood date not on that page).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 239A; Art 240; 27th (1971); 55th (1986, Art 371H Arunachal special).
**Trap:** 37th (1975) gave Arunachal an assembly while it was still a UT; statehood came in 1987; the 55th (1986) added Art 371H. Keep 27th (UT creation via NEAR Act) and 37th (assembly) distinct.
**Back:** *"37th = Arunachal gets an Assembly (UT, Art 239A)."*

### Flashcard Amend-038 — 38th Amendment (1975)
**Front:** 38th Amendment (1975) — satisfaction of President/Governor non-justiciable (Emergency, ordinances)
**Provision:** Enacted 1975; commenced **1 August 1975**. Changed **Art 123, 213, 239B, 352, 356, 359, 360** — the President's/Governor's satisfaction for ordinances and emergencies made final and not justiciable; multiple emergencies may be proclaimed at once (352).
**Learn:**
- Trigger: Emergency of June 1975 (proclaimed 25 June 1975) and fear of judicial review.
- Effect: the satisfaction of the President/Governor cannot be questioned; this was reversed by the 44th (1978).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** *ADM Jabalpur v Shivakant Shukla* (1976) (habeas corpus suspended during Emergency — later overruled by *K.S. Puttaswamy* (2017)); 39th (1975); 42nd (1976); 44th (1978) (reversed the 38th's exclusion).
**Trap:** 38th (1975) excluded judicial review of emergency satisfaction; 44th (1978) restored it; 42nd (1976) also changed Art 352.
**Back:** *"38th = Judges shut out of Emergency calls (later reversed by 44th)."*

### Flashcard Amend-039 — 39th Amendment (1975)
**Front:** 39th Amendment (1975) — election of PM, President, VP, Speaker shielded (Art 329A)
**Provision:** Enacted 1975; commenced **10 August 1975**. Changed **Art 71, 329**, **Ninth Schedule**; inserted **Art 329A** (election disputes of PM and Speaker to be decided by a body designated by Parliament, not courts; also shielded election of President, VP, PM and Speaker from judicial review).
**Learn:**
- Trigger: *State of UP v Raj Narain* (Allahabad HC, 12 June 1975) set aside Indira Gandhi's election.
- *Indira Nehru Gandhi v Raj Narain* (1975): SC struck the 39th's ouster of judicial review as violating free & fair elections, rule of law and judicial review (basic structure); the Ninth additions (laws) were not struck as such.
- 44th Amdt (1978) omitted Art 329A.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** *Indira Nehru Gandhi v Raj Narain* (1975); *Kesavananda* (1973); 44th (1978).
**Trap:** 39th (1975) and Art 329A vs 44th (1978) (omission); the 39th's Ninth Schedule additions were not voided in the same way. Art 329A is 'omitted' not 'live'.
**Back:** *"39th = PM's election shielded; SC said no (basic structure, 1975)."*

### Flashcard Amend-040 — 40th Amendment (1976)
**Front:** 40th Amendment (1976) — offshore resources vest in the Union (Art 297)
**Provision:** Enacted 1976; commenced **27 May 1976** (✅ Wikipedia list). Changed **Art 297** — things of value in territorial waters, continental shelf and EEZ vest in the Union (✅ Wikisource Part XII); added Acts to **Ninth Schedule** (count STILL-UNVERIFIED ⚠️).
**Learn:**
- Trigger: Maritime and offshore resources (oil and gas) and the law of the sea development (EEZ).
- Effect: resources of territorial waters, continental shelf & EEZ belong to Union (not States).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 297; Territorial Waters, Continental Shelf, EEZ and Other Maritime Zones Act 1976; UNCLOS (1982, ⚠️ India ratified 1995).
**Trap:** 40th (1976) is about maritime resources (Art 297), not Art 297 general property. The Ninth additions count is ⚠️.
**Back:** *"40th = Sea resources to Union (Art 297)."*

### Flashcard Amend-041 — 41st Amendment (1976)
**Front:** 41st Amendment (1976) — retirement age of State Public Service Commission members
**Provision:** Enacted 1976; commenced 7 Sep 1976. Amends **Art 316** so that the chairman and members of a **State** Public Service Commission retire at **62** (previously 60). The UPSC's own age limit stays at **65** under Art 316(1).
**Learn:**
- Service-condition change only; no change to Part XIV's structure or to the functions of Art 320.
- The age limit protects the tenure of State PSC chairpersons, a point linked to Art 317 (removal only by the Governor/President on the stated grounds and procedure).
- Passed during the Emergency (1975–77) with other Parliament-only amendments; Art 316 is not on the Art 368(2) federal list, so no State ratification was needed.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 315 (Public Service Commissions), Art 316 (terms), Art 317 (removal), Art 320 (functions). No landmark case.
**Trap:** Do not extend the 62-year limit to UPSC members (still 65). Do not confuse with the 45th/62nd, which are about legislative reservation (Art 334).
**Back:** *"Forty-one: State commissioners stay to sixty-two; the Union's sixty-five stays."*

### Flashcard Amend-042 — 42nd Amendment (1976)
**Front:** 42nd Amendment (1976) — the "mini-Constitution"
**Provision:** Enacted 1976 (Lok Sabha passed 2 Nov 1976; Rajya Sabha 10–11 Nov 1976; assent 18 Dec 1976). Commencement: 3 Jan 1977 (most sections), 1 Feb 1977, 1 Apr 1977 (s.27) (✅ Wikipedia 42nd Amdt). Passed during the Emergency (25 Jun 1975 – 21 Mar 1977). The widest amendment in the Constitution's history.
**Learn:**
- **Complete change list (grouped):**
  - *Preamble:* "Socialist" and "Secular" inserted; "unity of the Nation" became "unity and integrity of the Nation".
  - *Part IVA:* **Art 51A** — ten Fundamental Duties (the eleventh, Art 51A(k), came with the 86th Amendment in 2002).
  - *Part XIVA:* **Art 323A** (administrative tribunals) and **Art 323B** (tribunals for other matters).
  - *DPSPs:* **Art 39A** (equal justice and free legal aid), **Art 43A** (workers' participation in management), **Art 48A** (protect environment, forests and wildlife).
  - *Art 31C:* protection extended from Art 39(b)–(c) to **all** DPSPs; laws implementing any DPSP shielded from challenge under Arts 14, 19 and 31; the bar on judicial review extended by the 42nd (struck down in *Minerva Mills*, 1980).
  - *New Articles inserted:* 31D (anti-national activities), 32A, 131A (SC's exclusive jurisdiction over Centre–State disputes), 139A, 144A, 226A, 228A (HC's power over State laws), 257A (Central forces in States).
  - *Articles substituted or amended:* 103, 150, 192, 226 (substituted); 31, 39, 55, 74, 77, 81, 82, 83, 100, 102, 105, 118, 145, 166, 170, 172, 189, 191, 194, 208, 217, 225, 227, 228, 311, 312 (All-India Judicial Service), 330, 352, 353, 356–359, 366 (new clauses defining "Central law" and "State law"), 368, 371F.
  - *Art 74:* President bound by Council of Ministers' aid and advice (Governors not covered).
  - *Terms:* Lok Sabha (Art 83(2)) and State Assemblies (Art 172) moved from 5 to **6** years; delimitation and seat allocation frozen until the **2001 Census** (Arts 81, 82, 170).
  - *Emergency:* President's satisfaction under Art 352 made final and non-justiciable; Art 356 Parliament-approval gap extended from six months to one year; Art 357 continuation of laws during President's Rule; Arts 358–359 widened (suspension of Art 19 and of enforcement of rights).
  - *Art 368 (4)–(5):* no amendment "shall be called in question in any court on any ground"; power declared unlimited.
  - *Seventh Schedule:* subjects moved from State to **Concurrent List** — Education, Forests, Protection of wild animals and birds, Weights and measures, and Administration of Justice (other than SC/HC) (entry numbers ⚠️ verify); population control and family planning added (Entry 20A). Art 105/194 "evolve" privileges clause.
  - *Art 366 and 371F:* consequential definitions and special-status changes.
- **Reversals:** 43rd (1977–78) removed 31D, 32A, 131A, 144A, 226A, 228A; 44th (1978–79) omitted 257A and restored most of the Emergency and Art 74 features; the six-year term was reversed by the 44th.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** *Minerva Mills v. Union of India* (1980): struck the 31C extension (s.4) and the unlimited-power clauses (s.55); "the donee of a limited power cannot … convert the limited power into an unlimited one" (Chandrachud CJ). Basic-structure doctrine reaffirmed. *Kesavananda Bharati* (1973) was the doctrine the 42nd tried to negate. Art 51A and Art 48A link to the DPSP–FR balance debate.
**Trap:** Duties are non-justiciable (Art 51A); no enforcement mechanism. The "Socialist Secular" words are in the Preamble, not in Part III. Minerva Mills struck the clauses, not the whole Act. Do not say the 42nd reduced the Lok Sabha term to 5 years (it raised it to 6). PYQ route: "which amendment added Fundamental Duties / six-year term / unlimited amending power" — answer 42nd, with the reversal by the 43rd/44th as the qualifier.
**Back:** *"Forty-two: Socialist-Secular Preamble, ten Duties, six-year House, and an amending power Minerva Mills called limited."*

### Flashcard Amend-043 — 43rd Amendment (1977)
**Front:** 43rd Amendment — restoration of judicial review (post-Emergency)
**Provision:** Enacted 1977; commenced 13 Apr 1978. **Repeals** Arts 31D, 32A, 131A, 144A, 226A and 228A (all inserted by the 42nd); **amends** Arts 145, 226, 228 and 366. The result: the Supreme Court and High Courts regain jurisdiction over validity challenges that the 42nd had curtailed.
**Learn:**
- Enacted by the Janata government after the 1977 election; part of the post-Emergency repair programme alongside the 44th.
- 31D (anti-national activities) and 32A (no SC review of State laws under FR) are the most important repeals.
- 228A restored the High Court's power to decide State-law constitutional questions in the normal way; 131A removed the SC's exclusive original jurisdiction created by the 42nd.
- Repeal means the pre-1976 jurisdiction is back; it does not undo the 42nd's Preamble, duties or tribunals.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 131 (original jurisdiction), Art 226 (HC writs), Art 145 (SC rules), Art 366 (definitions). 42nd (1976) and 44th (1978) are the linked amendments.
**Trap:** The 43rd did **not** remove Part IVA (Fundamental Duties), Arts 39A, 43A, 48A or the Tribunals (Part XIVA); it removed the court-curbing clauses. Do not write "43rd repealed the 42nd entirely". Another trap: the 43rd is sometimes confused with the 44th's reversal of property FR.
**Back:** *"Forty-three: the courts get their keys back (31D, 32A, 131A, 144A, 226A, 228A out)."*

### Flashcard Amend-044 — 44th Amendment (1978)
**Front:** 44th Amendment (1978) — property removed from Fundamental Rights; Emergency safeguards
**Provision:** Title "1978" (Parliament passed it in 1978; assent by President Neelam Sanjiva Reddy on **30 Apr 1979**). Commencement per the Act's own sections (✅ Wikipedia 44th Amdt): **30 Apr 1979** (most sections, incl. the assent/Gazette date), **1 Aug 1979** (ss. 17–21, 30), **6 Sep 1979** (ss. 24, 43); the omission of Art 329A took effect **20 Jun 1979** (✅ Wikisource Part XV footnote; the Wikipedia list table also gives 20 Jun 1979). Lok Sabha passed 23 Aug 1978; Rajya Sabha passed with amendments 31 Aug 1978; Lok Sabha accepted those amendments 7 Dec 1978. Ratified by 17 State legislatures.
**Learn:**
- **Complete change list (grouped):**
  - *Property:* Art 19(1)(f) (right to acquire, hold and dispose of property) and **Art 31** (compensation for compulsory acquisition) omitted; **Art 300A** inserted in Part XII — "No person shall be deprived of his property save by authority of law." Property is now a constitutional (not fundamental) right.
  - *Emergency — Art 352:* "internal disturbance" replaced by **"armed rebellion"**; proclamation only on the **written advice of the Union Cabinet**; Parliament must approve within **one month** by a **special majority** (majority of total membership and two-thirds present and voting); Art 352(7) revocation procedure.
  - *Art 358:* Art 19 suspended only on a proclamation on grounds of **war or external aggression**, not armed rebellion.
  - *Art 359:* Art 20 (protection in respect of conviction) and **Art 21** (life and personal liberty) cannot be suspended during an Emergency.
  - *Art 356:* Parliament-approval period restored to **six months** (the 42nd's one year reversed).
  - *Art 22:* preventive detention — the maximum period without an Advisory Board report reduced from **three to two months**.
  - *Art 74:* President may require the Council of Ministers to reconsider advice **once**; reconsidered advice binding.
  - *Art 83(2) and Art 172:* House terms restored to **five years**.
  - *Arts 71, 103, 192:* substituted; election disputes of President/VP and disqualification procedure changed (the EC's opinion for disqualification under Arts 103/192).
  - *Arts 105, 194:* parliamentary and legislative privileges restored (the 42nd's "evolve" wording reversed).
  - *New Art 134A* (certificate for appeal to SC) and **Art 361A** (protection for fair and accurate press reports of Parliament and State proceedings).
  - *Omitted:* Art 31, Art 257A (Central forces in States), Art 329A (election of PM/Speaker), Part XIVA (Tribunals) was marked for omission but the Rajya Sabha did not support it (✅ corrected: the omission of Part XIVA was blocked; Wikipedia 44th Amdt).
  - *Blocked in the Rajya Sabha:* clauses amending **Art 31C**, **Art 366** and **Art 368** did not get the two-thirds vote; the 42nd's Art 31C and 368 changes therefore largely survived until *Minerva Mills*.
  - *Also amended:* Arts 19, 30, 31A, 38 (new cl. (2) on minimising income inequalities), 77, 123, 132–134, 139A, 150, 166, 213, 217, 225–229, 239B, 329, 356, 358–360, 371F; Ninth Schedule updated.
- **Why it was needed:** the Janata government sought to undo Emergency excesses; property was removed from FR to end the constitutional blockade on land reform (the 4th, 17th, 25th and 31C debates).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** *Kesavananda Bharati* (1973) — background for property debates; *Minerva Mills* (1980) — 42nd's excesses; *Maneka Gandhi* (1978) — Art 21 read with Arts 14 and 19 (decided 25 Jan 1978, before the 44th took effect) ✓. 44th also made Art 21 unsuspendable, which makes the ADM Jabalpur (1976) ruling's logic harder to repeat in an Emergency.
**Trap:** Property is a legal right under Art 300A, not an FR under Part III; a suit under Art 32 is not available for property. The Emergency safeguards (Arts 20, 21 not suspendable) are the **44th**, not the 42nd. "Armed rebellion" is the 44th's wording (the 1950 Constitution said "internal disturbance"). Art 19(1)(f) was removed, so "property" claims cannot be filed under Art 19. ✅ corrected: 20 Jun 1979 is the effective date of the Art 329A omission (Wikisource Part XV footnote), not a general commencement date; the Wikipedia 44th page's section list gives 30 Apr, 1 Aug and 6 Sep 1979. Frequent PYQ: 44th removed property from FR (2019 Q45 route, paired with the 99th).
**Back:** *"Forty-four: Property to Article 300A; Emergency with armed rebellion, Cabinet advice and two Houses; 21 never suspended."*

### Flashcard Amend-045 — 45th Amendment (1980)
**Front:** 45th Amendment (1980) — extension of legislative reservation to 1990
**Provision:** Enacted 1980; commenced 25 Jan 1980. Amends **Art 334**: reservation of seats for SCs and STs in the Lok Sabha and State Assemblies, and nomination of Anglo-Indians, extended from 1980 to **1990**.
**Learn:**
- Part of a series of ten-year extensions (23rd 1969 → 1980; 45th → 1990; 62nd → 2000; 79th → 2010; 95th → 2020; 104th → 2030).
- The sunset clause in Art 334 is a constitutional time limit (the framers' 10-year design), and each extension needed a constitutional amendment.
- The principle: reservation in legislatures is a time-bound affirmative measure; by contrast, reservation in education and jobs (Arts 15(4), 16(4)) has no sunset.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 330 (Lok Sabha ST seats), Art 332 (Assembly ST seats), Art 334 (sunset), 23rd, 62nd, 79th, 95th, 104th (later extensions). No landmark case.
**Trap:** Do not confuse the sunset for SC/ST reservation in legislatures (Art 334) with reservation in services (Art 16(4)), which carries no sunset. The 104th (2019) extended to 2030 and ended Anglo-Indian nomination (Arts 331, 333) — do not attribute the Anglo-Indian end to the 45th.
**Back:** *"Forty-five: the legislative quota gets a decade more (to 1990)."*

### Flashcard Amend-046 — 46th Amendment (1982)
**Front:** 46th Amendment (1982) — sales tax on works contracts and hire-purchase
**Provision:** Enacted 1982; commenced 2 Feb 1983. Inserts **Art 366(29A)**, defining "tax on the sale or purchase of goods" to include transfer of property in goods involved in works contracts, hire-purchase, leasing, etc.; amends **Arts 269 and 286** and **Seventh Schedule** (State List entry on sales tax, Entry 54).
**Learn:**
- Trigger: the Supreme Court's rule that a works contract was indivisible and not a sale of goods, so the States could not levy sales tax on the goods element.
- The amendment lets States tax the "deemed sale" element of works contracts, hire-purchase and leasing.
- It is the constitutional fix for a tax-base gap; the GST (101st) later replaced the State sales tax architecture.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** *Gannon Dunkerley & Co. v. State of Rajasthan* (1958) — indivisible works contract (the trigger). Art 269 (Union-collected taxes assigned to States), Art 286 (restriction on taxation of inter-State and export sales), Art 366(29A), 101st (GST, 2016).
**Trap:** The 46th does not create a "GST" — it is the State sales-tax framework (pre-GST). The 6th Amendment (1956) created Union List Entry 92A and the inter-State sales-tax framework; do not conflate the two.
**Back:** *"Forty-six: works contracts are sales too, for tax."*

### Flashcard Amend-047 — 47th Amendment (1984)
**Front:** 47th Amendment (1984) — land reform laws in the Ninth Schedule
**Provision:** Enacted 1984; commenced 26 Aug 1984 (✅ Wikipedia list). Adds State land-reform Acts to the **Ninth Schedule** (the count of 14 is STILL-UNVERIFIED ⚠️; Wikipedia gives no count); the Ninth Schedule was thus used to protect land laws from judicial review under Arts 14, 19 and 31.
**Learn:**
- The Ninth Schedule (1st Amendment, 1951, Art 31B) shields listed laws from challenge on FR grounds.
- Post-1973, the courts held that Ninth-Schedule laws are open to review if they violate the basic structure (*I.R. Coelho*, 2007), and that laws added after **24 Apr 1973** are tested that way (*Waman Rao*, 1981 — cut-off date).
- Common Prelims line: the Ninth Schedule's protection is not absolute.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 31B (Ninth Schedule), Art 31A (land-reform protection), *I.R. Coelho v. State of Tamil Nadu* (2007), *Waman Rao v. Union of India* (1981), *Kesavananda Bharati* (1973), 1st/4th/17th/29th/34th/66th/78th Amendments (other land-law additions).
**Trap:** Adding a law to the Ninth Schedule does not stop basic-structure review after 24 Apr 1973 (*Coelho*). Do not say the Ninth Schedule's protection is unlimited, and do not confuse the 47th with the 34th, 66th or 78th by Act count: the counts 14, 20, 55 and 27 are all STILL-UNVERIFIED ⚠️.
**Back:** *"Forty-seven: fourteen land laws in the shield, but the shield has a basic-structure check."*

### Flashcard Amend-048 — 48th Amendment (1984)
**Front:** 48th Amendment (1984) — President's Rule in Punjab up to two years
**Provision:** Enacted 1984; commenced 1 Apr 1984. Amends the proviso to **Art 356(5)** to allow the President's Rule in **Punjab** to be extended beyond one year, up to **two years**, subject to Election Commission certification of the conditions.
**Learn:**
- Trigger: the Punjab insurgency; the President's Rule imposed in Punjab in 1983 was approaching the one-year cap that Art 356 then allowed.
- Art 356 approval: Parliament must approve a proclamation within two months and renew it every six months; the 48th changed only the duration proviso for Punjab.
- The 59th, 63rd, 64th, 67th and 68th later extended or regulated Punjab's period; the 63rd repealed the 59th's special Punjab emergency provisions.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 356 (President's Rule), Art 357, 59th (1988), 63rd (1989), 64th (1990), 67th (1990), 68th (1991). *S.R. Bommai v. Union of India* (1994): Art 356 is subject to judicial review and the floor-test rule.
**Trap:** The 48th is about the **duration** of President's Rule in Punjab, not the Art 352 Emergency. Do not confuse "two years" with the later "five years" (68th). Bommai (1994) is the case that restricted misuse of Art 356 generally.
**Back:** *"Forty-eight: Punjab's Rule runs to two years, not one."*

### Flashcard Amend-049 — 49th Amendment (1984)
**Front:** 49th Amendment (1984) — Tripura Tribal Areas Autonomous District Council
**Provision:** Enacted 1984; commenced 11 Sep 1984. Brings the **Tripura Tribal Areas Autonomous District Council** under the **Sixth Schedule** (with the Art 244(2) framework); amends Art 244 and Schedules 5 and 6.
**Learn:**
- The Sixth Schedule governs tribal areas of Assam, Meghalaya, Tripura and Mizoram (not Schedule V).
- Sixth Schedule councils have legislative, executive and judicial powers within the area, and the Governor can exclude or modify Acts.
- Schedule V (Art 244(1)) covers Scheduled Areas in other States; Schedule VI (Art 244(2)) covers the four NE tribal-area States.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 244 (scheduled and tribal areas), Art 244A (autonomous State in Assam, 22nd Amendment), Sixth Schedule (paras 1–20), 22nd Amendment (Meghalaya).
**Trap:** The Sixth Schedule is for the four NE States; Schedule V is for Scheduled Areas elsewhere. The 49th does not create a State; Tripura is a State since 1972.
**Back:** *"Forty-nine: Tripura's tribal councils join the Sixth."*

### Flashcard Amend-050 — 50th Amendment (1984)
**Front:** 50th Amendment (1984) — Art 33 extended to intelligence and communication forces
**Provision:** Enacted 1984; commenced 11 Sep 1984. Amends **Art 33** to empower Parliament to restrict or abrogate, by law, the Fundamental Rights of members of the **armed forces, police and paramilitary, intelligence and communication** organisations and of persons engaged in securing public order in relation to such forces and bodies.
**Learn:**
- Art 33 has existed since 1950 for forces; the 50th widened it to intelligence and communication personnel and to those guarding property and communications of such organisations.
- Restriction is by **Parliament** (Art 33 is a law-making power, not an executive order).
- Linked to Art 19 and Art 21 limits; restrictions must be reasonable and tied to the purpose of discipline.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 33, Art 19, Art 21, Art 34 (martial law). No landmark case cited; verify any case before use.
**Trap:** Art 33 is a law-making power for Parliament; it is not an Emergency power (that is Art 352/358/359). Do not say the 50th suspended FR for armed forces; it authorises restriction by law only.
**Back:** *"Fifty: Parliament may trim the rights of the forces and intelligence, by law."*

### Flashcard Amend-051 — 51st Amendment (1984)
**Front:** 51st Amendment (1984) — Scheduled Tribe seats for North-Eastern States
**Provision:** Enacted 1984; commenced 16 Jun 1984. Amends **Arts 330 and 332** to provide reservation of seats for **Scheduled Tribes** in the Lok Sabha and State Assemblies of the North-Eastern States: Lok Sabha seats for Nagaland, Meghalaya, Mizoram and Arunachal Pradesh; Assembly seats for Meghalaya and Arunachal Pradesh only (✅ corrected: the card listed all four for both Houses; Wikipedia 51st Amdt, commenced 16 Jun 1984; Nagaland and Mizoram Assembly seats came by the 57th, 1987).
**Learn:**
- Part of the NE reorganisation sequence: 27th (1971), 36th (Sikkim, 1975), 53rd, 55th, 56th, 57th (1987).
- Art 330 is the Lok Sabha ST-seat provision; Art 332 the Assembly ST-seat provision; both require delimitation rules to be applied.
- Reservation of ST seats in NE States is a tool to reconcile tribal identity with election-based representation.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 330, Art 332, Art 334 (sunset), 57th (1987, assembly seats), 90th (2003, Bodoland, Art 332(6)).
**Trap:** The 51st is about **ST** seats; the 57th is about **Assembly** ST seats; do not attribute Art 332 changes solely to one amendment. State list verified (✅ Wikipedia 51st Amdt).
**Back:** *"Fifty-one: tribal seats in the North-East for Lok Sabha and Assemblies."*

### Flashcard Amend-052 — 52nd Amendment (1985)
**Front:** 52nd Amendment (1985) — Anti-defection law (Tenth Schedule)
**Provision:** Enacted 1985; commenced 1 Mar 1985. Inserts the **Tenth Schedule** and amends **Arts 101(3), 102(2), 190(3), 191(2)**: an MP or MLA is disqualified if they voluntarily give up membership of their party or defy the party whip in the House without permission, subject to exceptions. The **Speaker** (or Chairman) is the adjudicating authority.
**Learn:**
- Exceptions (original): a **split** of one-third of the legislature party (para 3) and a **merger** with another party of two-thirds (para 4). Para 3 (split) was deleted by the **91st** (2003); the **merger** exception remains (para 4).
- Grounds: (a) voluntarily giving up party membership; (b) voting or abstaining contrary to the whip. Independents lose membership if they join a party.
- Disqualification is decided by the **Presiding Officer**, not by the President or the Governor.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** *Kihoto Hollohan v. Zachillhu* (1992): Tenth Schedule upheld; Speaker's decision is subject to judicial review (limited), and the Speaker's action is not immune. *Rajendra Singh Rana v. Swami Prasad Maurya* (2007): Speaker's power over merger. 91st (2003). Art 361B (91st): disqualified legislators barred from remunerative political posts.
**Trap:** The 52nd does not give the President the disqualification decision (for State MLAs, the Governor decides office-of-profit disqualification after Election Commission opinion, under Art 192 as restored by the 44th). The 91st removed the split exception; a 1/3 split no longer protects a defection. Do not say the 52nd applies to the Rajya Sabha Chairman's decision as final.
**Back:** *"Fifty-two: Tenth Schedule — defect and you lose the seat; only a merger saves you."*

### Flashcard Amend-053 — 53rd Amendment (1986)
**Front:** 53rd Amendment (1986) — special provision for Mizoram
**Provision:** Enacted 1986; commenced **20 Feb 1986** (✅ Wikipedia 53rd Amdt). Mizoram's statehood date of **20 Feb 1987** is not on that page and is STILL-UNVERIFIED ⚠️. Inserts **Art 371G** (✅ Wikisource Part XXI: "Notwithstanding anything in this Constitution"; clause (a) protects Mizo social/religious practice, customary law and land): no Act of Parliament relating to Mizo customary law and social practice, Mizo religious and social practices, ownership and transfer of land, or criminal and civil justice involving Mizo customary law applies to Mizoram unless the Legislative Assembly decides by resolution.
**Learn:**
- Trigger: the Mizo Accord (1986), leading to Mizoram's statehood as the 23rd State.
- Art 371G is a "non-obstante" shield for tribal customary law, similar to the Nagaland (Art 371A, 13th) model.
