- The protection operates through a legislative-assembly resolution; it is not a Governor's veto.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 371A (Nagaland, 13th Amendment, 1962), Art 371H (Arunachal, 55th), Art 371J (Karnataka, 98th). Mizo Accord (1986).
**Trap:** Art 371G is a State-specific protection, not an Art 244 Scheduled Area. Do not confuse Mizoram's Art 371G with Art 371A (Nagaland) — the 371A list is wider.
**Back:** *"Fifty-three: Mizo custom gets its own shield, Article 371G."*

### Flashcard Amend-054 — 54th Amendment (1986)
**Front:** 54th Amendment (1986) — salaries of Supreme Court and High Court judges
**Provision:** Enacted 1986; commenced 1 Apr 1986. Amends **Arts 125 and 221** and the **Second Schedule**; the salaries of SC and HC judges are now to be determined by **Parliament by law** (and may be changed by law), replacing the fixed schedule amount.
**Learn:**
- Salary of judges is charged on the Consolidated Fund (Art 112(3)(d) and 125/221) and cannot be varied to the disadvantage of a judge after appointment (Art 125(2), 221(2) — as amended).
- Art 360(4)(b) (financial emergency) runs the other way: during a financial emergency the President may issue directions for the reduction of salaries and allowances of persons serving the Union, expressly including the Judges of the Supreme Court and the High Courts (✅ corrected: was "protects judges' salaries from reduction"; Wikisource Const. 2020, Part XVIII, Art 360(4)(b)).
- The change let Parliament revise pay without a fresh constitutional amendment.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 125(1)–(2) (SC judges' salary and no-disadvantage rule; Second Schedule until Parliament acts) (✅ corrected: was "Art 124(7) (SC judges' salary per Second Schedule)"; Art 124(7) is the bar on pleading or acting after retirement, Wikisource Const. 2020, Part V), Art 221 (HC judges), Art 360(4), Second Schedule, 54th-linked pay revisions.
**Trap:** The 54th changed the mechanics of salary determination; it did not make judges' salaries subject to executive approval. Do not conflate with the judges' tenure and removal under Art 124(4)–(5) (impeachment).
**Back:** *"Fifty-four: judges' pay moves from the schedule to Parliament's law."*

### Flashcard Amend-055 — 55th Amendment (1986)
**Front:** 55th Amendment (1986) — special responsibility of the Governor, Arunachal Pradesh
**Provision:** Enacted 1986; commencement 20 Feb 1987 (the day Arunachal Pradesh became the 24th State). Inserts **Art 371H**: the Governor of Arunachal Pradesh has special responsibility regarding law and order, exercised by his individual judgment after consulting the Council of Ministers (✅ Wikisource Const. 2020, Part XXI, Art 371H(a): "the Governor shall, after consulting the Council of Ministers, exercise his individual judgment as to the action to be taken"; was "in his discretion").
**Learn:**
- Art 371H is the special-status clause for Arunachal, created when it became a State (from the NEFA/UT background).
- The Governor's special responsibility is exercised by individual judgment, but only after consulting the Council of Ministers, so it is not outside aid and advice altogether (✅ corrected: was "sits outside the normal aid and advice rule").
- Arunachal's special status is a counterpart of the 371A (Nagaland) and 371G (Mizoram) provisions.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 371H, Art 371A, Art 371G, 24th State (1987), 37th Amendment (1975, Arunachal UT Assembly), Art 163 (Governor's discretion).
**Trap:** The 37th (1975) created the Arunachal UT's legislature; the 55th (1986) gave the Governor's special responsibility and statehood followed in 1987. Do not write 55th = statehood.
**Back:** *"Fifty-five: Arunachal's Governor keeps law and order in his own hands."*

### Flashcard Amend-056 — 56th Amendment (1987)
**Front:** 56th Amendment (1987) — Goa's statehood transition
**Provision:** Enacted 1987; commenced 30 May 1987 (Goa became the 25th State the same day). Inserts **Art 371I**: the Legislative Assembly of Goa shall have **not less than 30 members** (transitional provision for the new State) (✅ Wikisource Const. 2020, Part XXI, Art 371I).
**Learn:**
- Goa was a Union Territory from 1961 (12th Amendment, 1962); the 56th converted it into a State with a transition clause.
- Art 371I is a State-specific protection for the Assembly's strength; it is not the same as Art 332 (ST seats).
- Pattern: the 1986–87 package (53rd, 55th, 56th, 57th) created statehood for Mizoram, Arunachal and Goa in 1987.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 371I, Art 2 and 3 (formation of States), 12th Amendment (1962, Goa UT), 53rd, 55th, 57th (1987). No landmark case.
**Trap:** The 56th does not set Goa's Lok Sabha seats or ST seats; those come from Arts 81 and 332 (57th for Assembly ST seats in some NE States). Do not attribute Goa's ST seats to the 56th.
**Back:** *"Fifty-six: Goa becomes a State with at least thirty MLAs."*

### Flashcard Amend-057 — 57th Amendment (1987)
**Front:** 57th Amendment (1987) — ST seats in Assemblies of certain North-Eastern States
**Provision:** Enacted 1987; commenced 21 Sep 1987. Amends **Art 332** to provide seat reservation for **Scheduled Tribes** in the Legislative Assemblies of **Nagaland, Meghalaya, Mizoram and Arunachal Pradesh** (✅ Wikisource Const. 2020, Part XVI, Art 332(3A) names Nagaland, Meghalaya, Mizoram and Arunachal Pradesh).
**Learn:**
- Complements the 51st (Lok Sabha ST seats); together they set tribal representation in NE Assemblies and the Lok Sabha.
- Art 332 counts ST seats based on the population proportion; a delimitation step (Art 82/170) is needed for allocation changes, which is why the 84th and 87th froze and then reset the basis.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 332, Art 330, 51st (1984), 84th (2001) and 87th (2003) freezing/census bases, 90th (2003, Bodoland).
**Trap:** The 57th is the Assembly provision, the 51st the Lok Sabha/Assembly provision for other NE States — check the State list. Do not extend the 57th to Goa (Goa's ST reservation is not the point).
**Back:** *"Fifty-seven: tribes in the NE Assemblies, four States named."*

### Flashcard Amend-058 — 58th Amendment (1987)
**Front:** 58th Amendment (1987) — authoritative Hindi text of the Constitution
**Provision:** Enacted 1987; commenced 9 Dec 1987. Inserts **Art 394A** (Part XXII) (✅ Wikisource Const. 2020, Part XXII, Art 394A(1), (3)): the President shall cause an authoritative translation of the Constitution in the Hindi language to be published, and it is authoritative alongside the English text.
**Learn:**
- Art 348 (English as the language of SC, HC and Acts) and Art 343 (Official language is Hindi in Devanagari, with English for 15 years) are the official-language context; Art 394A fixes an authoritative Hindi text.
- The English text remains the default for interpretation unless Hindi is expressly used, a point that matters in Mains answers on language.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 343, Art 344, Art 348, Art 351 (promotion of Hindi), Part XVII, 7th Amendment (Art 350A, 350B) for linguistic minorities, 21st/71st/92nd/96th (Eighth Schedule changes).
**Trap:** 58th = Hindi text; Eighth Schedule languages = other amendments (21st Sindhi, 71st, 92nd, 96th Odia). Do not write that the 58th made Hindi the national language; the Constitution has no national language clause.
**Back:** *"Fifty-eight: the Hindi Constitution becomes authoritative, Article 394A."*

### Flashcard Amend-059 — 59th Amendment (1988)
**Front:** 59th Amendment (1988) — Punjab "special" Emergency provisions (repealed by 63rd)
**Provision:** Enacted 1988; commenced 30 Mar 1988. Inserts **Art 359A** (special provisions for Punjab) and amends **Art 356**: the President's Rule in Punjab permitted up to **three years** (⚠️ 59th-era figure unverified; the current Art 356(4) Punjab proviso reads "five years", ✅ Wikisource Const. 2020, Part XVIII). ✅ Art 359A was "Omitted by the Constitution (Sixty-third Amendment) Act, 1989, s. 3 (w.e.f. 6-1-1990)" (Wikisource footnote). The Governor/Administrator and rights-suspension wording remains ⚠️ unverified.
**Learn:**
- Trigger: the Punjab militancy and the failure of the 1985 Accord-era arrangements; the Punjab emergency was treated as a special Art 356-type regime, not an Art 352 Emergency.
- The 63rd Amendment (1989) repealed the Punjab provisions; the 64th (1990), 67th (1990) and 68th (1991) extended President's Rule to 3.5, 4 and 5 years respectively.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 356, Art 359A (inserted and then repealed), 48th (1984), 63rd (1989), 64th, 67th, 68th. *S.R. Bommai v. Union of India* (1994) constrained Art 356.
**Trap:** The 59th is not a national Emergency. Do not say it "suspended all FR in Punjab" unless the Act text is checked (⚠️). Do not confuse Art 356 with Art 352.
**Back:** *"Fifty-nine: Punjab's special rule, three years, later struck by Sixty-three."*

### Flashcard Amend-060 — 60th Amendment (1988)
**Front:** 60th Amendment (1988) — profession tax ceiling
**Provision:** Enacted 1988; commenced 20 Dec 1988. Amends **Art 276(2)**: the maximum profession tax (which a State may levy) raised from **Rs 250** to **Rs 2,500** a year per person.
**Learn:**
- Profession tax is a State levy under Art 276 (a State List entry, Entry 60), with a ceiling fixed by the Constitution.
- The 60th raised the ceiling; it did not create a tax on incomes (income tax is Union, Entry 82).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 276, Art 265 (no tax without law), State List Entry 60, Entry 82 (Union income tax), 80th (2000 tax-pooling).
**Trap:** Profession tax (State, Art 276) is not income tax (Union, Art 265/Entry 82). The figures are Rs 250 and Rs 2,500 ceiling — do not invert them.
**Back:** *"Sixty: profession tax ceiling 250 → 2,500."*

### Flashcard Amend-061 — 61st Amendment (1988–89)
**Front:** 61st Amendment — voting age reduced from 21 to 18
**Provision:** Titled 1988 (✅ Wikisource Const. 2020, Part XV, Art 326 fn. 3: "Constitution (Sixty-first Amendment) Act, 1988, s. 2", w.e.f. 28-3-1989); in force **28 Mar 1989**. Amends **Art 326**: elections to the Lok Sabha and State Assemblies are by adult suffrage for citizens **18 years and above** (previously 21).
**Learn:**
- Trigger: the demand to align voting age with the age of majority and the 18–21 youth vote; it was a Parliament-only change to Art 326 and did not need State ratification.
- Voting age and candidature age are separate: Lok Sabha candidate age is 25 and Rajya Sabha 30, both in Art 84(b) (✅ corrected: was "Lok Sabha 25 (Art 84(a))"; Art 84(a) is the citizenship clause, Wikisource Const. 2020, Part V); State Assembly 25 (Art 173(b), not checked).
- Adult suffrage is a basic feature (equality and democracy), but the age is a legislative detail.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 326, Art 84 (qualifications for Parliament), Art 173 (State Assembly candidates), Art 19(1)(a)-related voter rights (no case cited).
**Trap:** The 61st changed **voting** age, not candidature age. Do not write that 18-year-olds may contest Lok Sabha or Assembly elections; the qualification age remains 25 (Lok Sabha/Assembly) and 30 (Rajya Sabha).
**Back:** *"Sixty-one: vote at eighteen; stand at twenty-five."*

### Flashcard Amend-062 — 62nd Amendment (1989)
**Front:** 62nd Amendment (1989) — SC/ST and Anglo-Indian reservation to 2000
**Provision:** Titled 1989; commenced 25 Jan 1990. Amends **Art 334** to extend SC/ST reservation in the Lok Sabha and State Assemblies, and Anglo-Indian nomination, from 1990 to **2000**.
**Learn:**
- Part of the decade-by-decade sunset cycle: 45th (to 1990), 62nd (to 2000), 79th (to 2010), 95th (to 2020), 104th (to 2030).
- The extension is for legislative seats only, not for services.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 334, Art 330, Art 332, Art 16(4) (services — no sunset), 45th, 79th, 95th, 104th.
**Trap:** The 62nd is about legislatures. Do not confuse with Art 335 (services relaxation, 82nd) or Art 16(4A) (promotion, 77th).
**Back:** *"Sixty-two: the decade quota runs to two thousand."*

### Flashcard Amend-063 — 63rd Amendment (1989–90)
**Front:** 63rd Amendment (1990) — repeal of the Punjab special provisions
**Provision:** Titled 1989 (✅ corrected: was 1990; Wikisource Const. 2020, Part XVIII, Art 359A footnote: "Constitution (Sixty-third Amendment) Act, 1989, s. 3 (w.e.f. 6-1-1990)"); commenced 6 Jan 1990 (✅ Wikipedia list). **Repeals** Art 359A (inserted by the 59th) and restores the earlier position in **Art 356**.
**Learn:**
- The 59th's special Punjab regime was repealed; President's Rule in Punjab then ran under the general Art 356 framework.
- The 64th, 67th and 68th later re-extended Punjab's President's Rule — the 63rd does not block extensions, only repeals the special provisions.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** 59th (1988), 64th (1990), 67th (1990), 68th (1991), Art 356, *S.R. Bommai* (1994).
**Trap:** The 63rd repealed the 59th; it did not repeal the 48th (Punjab up to 2 years). Do not write that the 63rd ended President's Rule in Punjab.
**Back:** *"Sixty-three: the Fifty-ninth's Punjab special regime is undone."*

### Flashcard Amend-064 — 64th Amendment (1990)
**Front:** 64th Amendment (1990) — Punjab President's Rule up to 3½ years
**Provision:** Titled 1990; commenced 16 Apr 1990. Amends **Art 356(5)** proviso to allow President's Rule in Punjab to run up to **three and a half years** (⚠️ verify the exact statutory wording).
**Learn:**
- The extension was a response to continuing insurgency in Punjab during the period the State Assembly was suspended.
- Each extension was done by constitutional amendment rather than by ordinary law, because Art 356's period was in the Constitution.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 356, 48th (1984), 59th (1988), 63rd (1989), 67th (1990), 68th (1991), *S.R. Bommai* (1994).
**Trap:** The 64th is about Punjab only; do not extend to all States. The 67th and 68th follow, so the cap is five years in total, not 3.5.
**Back:** *"Sixty-four: Punjab gets three and a half years."*

### Flashcard Amend-065 — 65th Amendment (1990)
**Front:** 65th Amendment (1990) — National Commission for SCs and STs (Art 338)
**Provision:** Titled 1990; commenced 12 Mar 1992. Amends **Art 338** to replace the single Special Officer for SCs and STs (Art 338, original) with a **multi-member National Commission for Scheduled Castes and Scheduled Tribes**.
**Learn:**
- The Commission investigates and monitors safeguards and advises the President/Parliament; the body had a constitutional base from 1992.
- The 89th (2003) later split it into two bodies: NCSC (Art 338) and NCST (Art 338A).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 338, Art 338A (89th, 2003), Art 338B (102nd, NCBC), Art 15–16 safeguards, Art 46 (DPSP for weaker sections).
**Trap:** Do not say the 65th created the NCST; the joint Commission was split by the 89th. Note the year gap: Act 1990, commencement 1992.
**Back:** *"Sixty-five: the SC/ST officer becomes a Commission."*

### Flashcard Amend-066 — 66th Amendment (1990)
**Front:** 66th Amendment (1990) — land-reform laws in the Ninth Schedule
**Provision:** Titled 1990; commenced 7 Jun 1990. Adds **55 State land-reform Acts** to the **Ninth Schedule**.
**Learn:**
- Same device as the 17th, 29th, 34th, 47th and 78th Amendments: land-reform laws placed under Art 31B to avoid challenge under Arts 14, 19 and 31.
- The protection is subject to basic-structure review for laws added after 24 Apr 1973 (*Waman Rao*, 1981; *I.R. Coelho*, 2007).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 31B, Art 31A, *Waman Rao* (1981), *I.R. Coelho* (2007), 17th (1964), 29th (1972), 34th (1974), 47th (1984), 78th (1995).
**Trap:** 55 is the 66th's number, not the 66th's year. Do not claim the 66th's additions are beyond review.
**Back:** *"Sixty-six: fifty-five land Acts join the shield."*

### Flashcard Amend-067 — 67th Amendment (1990)
**Front:** 67th Amendment (1990) — Punjab President's Rule up to 4 years
**Provision:** Titled 1990; commenced 4 Oct 1990. Amends the **Art 356** proviso so that President's Rule in Punjab can run up to **four years** (⚠️ verify wording).
**Learn:**
- A further extension to the Punjab regime (after 64th's 3.5 years).
- Each step needed a fresh constitutional amendment, which is why the sequence is visible in the Constitution's amendment list.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 356, 64th (1990), 68th (1991), *S.R. Bommai* (1994).
**Trap:** The 67th is 4 years; the 68th is 5 years (maximum). Do not reverse them.
**Back:** *"Sixty-seven: four years, Punjab."*

### Flashcard Amend-068 — 68th Amendment (1991)
**Front:** 68th Amendment (1991) — Punjab President's Rule up to 5 years
**Provision:** Titled 1991; commenced 12 Mar 1991. Amends **Art 356** proviso to extend Punjab President's Rule to **five years** (the maximum allowed under this series) (✅ Wikisource Const. 2020, Part XVIII, Art 356(4) Punjab proviso: "five years").
**Learn:**
- The fifth and last extension in the 1988–91 Punjab sequence (59th, 63rd, 64th, 67th, 68th).
- Art 356 as it now stands prohibits continuation beyond three years unless certain conditions are met (✅ Wikisource Const. 2020, Part XVIII, Art 356(4) first proviso: "no such Proclamation shall in any case remain in force for more than three years", subject to the resolution-based continuance; the 44th/1988–91 interaction remains ⚠️).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 356, *S.R. Bommai v. Union of India* (1994), 48th, 59th, 63rd, 64th, 67th.
**Trap:** Five years is the Punjab-specific ceiling in that series. Do not say five years is the current general ceiling.
**Back:** *"Sixty-eight: Punjab's rule reaches its five-year maximum."*

### Flashcard Amend-069 — 69th Amendment (1991)
**Front:** 69th Amendment (1991) — Delhi as National Capital Territory
**Provision:** Titled 1991; commenced 1 Feb 1992. Inserts **Art 239AA** (National Capital Territory of Delhi — Legislative Assembly; Council of Ministers; Lt Governor/Administrator) and **Art 239AB** (failure of constitutional machinery in Delhi).
**Learn:**
- Delhi is a Union Territory with a legislature and a Council of Ministers under Art 239AA, with Parliament's power intact.
- Art 239AA(4): the Council of Ministers (not more than ten per cent of the Assembly's members) aids and advises the Lt Governor, except where a law requires him to act in his discretion; a difference of opinion goes to the President (✅ Wikisource Const. 2020, Part VIII; Art 239AB confirmed).
- Art 239AB permits suspension of the Assembly, akin to President's Rule in a UT.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** *Government of NCT of Delhi v. Union of India* (2018, Constitution Bench): Lt Governor is bound by Council of Ministers' aid and advice except on land, police and public order; Delhi's elected government has primacy in other matters (verify); 69th–70th; 106th (women's quota for Delhi Assembly, Art 239AA changed).
**Trap:** Delhi is an NCT, not a State, though it has a legislature. Art 239AA is not Art 2 or 3. Do not say the Delhi Assembly has police or land powers (reserved to the Union/LG under 2018 judgment).
**Back:** *"Sixty-nine: Delhi gets an Assembly, not statehood."*

### Flashcard Amend-070 — 70th Amendment (1991/1992)
**Front:** 70th Amendment — Delhi and Puducherry in the presidential electoral college
**Provision:** Commenced 21 Dec 1991 (title 1992 ⚠️ verify). Amends **Arts 54 and 55**: the elected members of the Legislative Assemblies of **Delhi** and **Puducherry** join the electoral college for the President and the Union Territories' legislatures (✅ Wikisource Const. 2020, Part V, Art 54 explanation: "State" includes the NCT of Delhi and the UT of Pondicherry).
**Learn:**
- The electoral college for the President: elected MPs and MLAs of States; Delhi and Puducherry's MLAs joined it after the 69th created Delhi's Assembly and the 14th created Puducherry's.
- The 70th does not give Delhi or Puducherry statehood; the vote is a representational add-on.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 54, Art 55 (method of election), 69th (1991), 14th (1962 Puducherry), 70th.
**Trap:** Election of President: elected MPs + elected MLAs of the States and Delhi/Puducherry Assemblies; nominated members and MLCs do not vote (Art 54). Do not write that Council members vote.
**Back:** *"Seventy: Delhi and Puducherry's MLAs elect the President."*

### Flashcard Amend-071 — 71st Amendment (1992)
**Front:** 71st Amendment (1992) — Konkani, Manipuri and Nepali in the Eighth Schedule
**Provision:** Enacted 1992; commenced 31 Aug 1992. Adds **Konkani, Manipuri and Nepali** to the **Eighth Schedule**, taking the total to **18** languages.
**Learn:**
- The Eighth Schedule lists official languages recognised for Art 344(1) and Art 351 (Hindi development); being listed gives the language a status in the Commission and the Sahitya Akademi-type schemes.
- Subsequent additions: 92nd (2003: Bodo, Dogri, Maithili, Santali), 96th (2011: Odia).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 344(1) (Official Language Commission), Art 351, Eighth Schedule, 21st (1967, Sindhi), 92nd (2003, total 22), 96th (2011).
**Trap:** Eighth Schedule count: 14 (original) → 15 (Sindhi, 21st) → 18 (71st) → 22 (92nd). Do not write 18 as the current total.
**Back:** *"Seventy-one: K-M-N makes it eighteen."*

### Flashcard Amend-072 — 72nd Amendment (1992)
**Front:** 72nd Amendment (1992) — Scheduled Tribe seats in the Tripura Assembly
**Provision:** Enacted 1992; commenced 5 Dec 1992. Amends **Art 332** to reserve ST seats in the **Tripura** Legislative Assembly (in line with the Tripura Tribal Areas arrangement).
**Learn:**
- Tripura's ST population and the Tripura Tribal Areas Autonomous District Council (49th) are linked to this change.
- Art 332 assembly seat reservation is decided by population ratio and delimitation.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 332, 49th (1984, Sixth Schedule), 51st, 57th.
**Trap:** The 72nd is the Tripura Assembly provision, not the Sixth Schedule (49th). Do not say it created the Tripura Tribal Areas Council.
**Back:** *"Seventy-two: Tripura's tribal seats in the Assembly."*

### Flashcard Amend-073 — 73rd Amendment (1992)
**Front:** 73rd Amendment (1992) — Panchayati Raj (Part IX, Eleventh Schedule)
**Provision:** Enacted 1992; commenced **24 Apr 1993** (National Panchayati Raj Day). Inserts **Part IX (Arts 243–243O)** and the **Eleventh Schedule** (29 subjects; ✅ Wikisource Const. 2020, Part IX checked for Arts 243B, 243D(1)/(3), 243I, 243K, 243M, 243O; Eleventh Schedule count not checked). Key rules:
- Gram Sabha (Art 243A); three-tier system at village, intermediate and district levels (Art 243B).
- Direct elections; term of **five years** (Art 243E); fresh election within six months of dissolution.
- Reservation for SCs/STs in proportion to population (Art 243D(1)); **one-third** of seats reserved for women (Art 243D(3)), and one-third of offices of chairpersons.
- State Election Commission (Art 243K) and State Finance Commission (Art 243I).
- Bar on courts interfering in electoral matters (Art 243O).
**Learn:**
- Panchayats are not optional: a State must hold elections within the constitutional timetable.
- Economic development and social justice (Eleventh Schedule) link to Art 40 (DPSP) and the 7th Schedule.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** *Rajbala v. State of Haryana* (decided 10 Dec 2015; Chelameswar and Sapre JJ.; (2016) 1 SCC 463) upheld Haryana's educational qualifications for Panchayat candidates (✅ Wikipedia; the page is a stub and records only the Art 14 challenge as the basis). *K. Krishna Murthy v. Union of India* (2010): triple test for OBC reservation in local bodies. Art 40 (DPSP), Art 243ZA (Panchayat electoral rolls).
**Trap:** Panchayats are a State subject (State List Entry 5, local government); the Union List has no panchayat entry. The 73rd does not apply to Scheduled Areas in full (Art 243M — PESA 1996 override). Do not write "one-third of seats" for SCs/STs (that is in proportion to population, plus 1/3 women).
**Back:** *"Seventy-three: Panchayats, twenty-fourth April, three tiers, five years."*

### Flashcard Amend-074 — 74th Amendment (1992)
**Front:** 74th Amendment (1992) — Municipalities (Part IXA, Twelfth Schedule)
**Provision:** Enacted 1992; commenced **1 Jun 1993**. Inserts **Part IXA (Arts 243P–243ZG)** and the **Twelfth Schedule** (18 subjects); amends Art 280. Municipal bodies: **Nagar Panchayat, Municipal Council, Municipal Corporation** (Art 243Q). Ward-level direct election; five-year term; one-third women reservation (Art 243T); State Election Commission and State Finance Commission for municipalities.
**Learn:**
- Parallel to the 73rd: same framework of periodic elections and independent finance and election bodies.
- Art 243W (powers to municipalities) and Art 243X (taxes, duties and fees) are the financial core.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 243P–243ZG, Art 243W, Art 243X, Twelfth Schedule, Art 280 (Finance Commission to consider municipalities), 73rd (1992).
**Trap:** Do not confuse the 74th's 18 subjects with the 73rd's 29 subjects; the 74th's Twelfth Schedule is for urban local bodies. Do not write that the 74th covers Scheduled Areas.
**Back:** *"Seventy-four: Municipalities, June first, eighteen subjects, Twelfth Schedule."*

### Flashcard Amend-075 — 75th Amendment (1994)
**Front:** 75th Amendment (1994) — Rent Control Tribunals (Art 323B)
**Provision:** Enacted 1993 (✅ corrected: was 1994; Wikisource Const. 2020, Part XIVA, Art 323B(2)(h) note: "inserted by the 75th Amendment (1993)"); commencement **15 May 1994** (✅ Wikipedia list). Amends **Art 323B** to allow Parliament or State legislatures to set up **Rent Tribunals** for disputes on **rent, its regulation and control, and tenancy issues** (✅ Art 323B(1), (2)(h); "eviction" is not a separately listed matter, so that word is corrected out).
**Learn:**
- Art 323B enables tribunals for specified matters by law (taxation, land reforms, elections, etc.); the 75th adds rent control.
- Tribunals are an alternative to the High Court's writ jurisdiction for specified matters, subject to *L. Chandra Kumar*-type review of their jurisdiction.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 323A (administrative tribunals), Art 323B, 42nd (1976, Part XIVA), *L. Chandra Kumar v. Union of India* (1997): tribunals' decisions remain subject to High Court and Supreme Court writ jurisdiction (Art 226/32 cannot be ousted).
**Trap:** Tribunals under Art 323A (service) and 323B (other matters) are different; do not conflate. *L. Chandra Kumar* (1997) is a limit on ouster.
**Back:** *"Seventy-five: rent tribunals, under Article 323B."*

### Flashcard Amend-076 — 76th Amendment (1994)
**Front:** 76th Amendment (1994) — Tamil Nadu 69% reservation placed in the Ninth Schedule
**Provision:** Enacted 1994; commenced 31 Aug 1994. Places the **Tamil Nadu Backward Classes, Scheduled Castes and Scheduled Tribes (Reservation of Seats in Educational Institutions and of Appointments or Posts in the Services) Act, 1993** (69% total reservation) in the **Ninth Schedule**.
**Learn:**
- Trigger: *Indra Sawhney* (1992) capped reservation at **50%**; Tamil Nadu's 69% scheme was protected by the Ninth Schedule.
- The protection is not absolute: post-2007 review applies to Ninth-Schedule laws that breach the basic structure (*I.R. Coelho*), and the 69% quota remains a live constitutional question (⚠️ check current status before quoting).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** *Indra Sawhney v. Union of India* (1992): 50% ceiling; *I.R. Coelho* (2007); Art 31B; Art 15(4) and 16(4).
**Trap:** The 76th did not raise the cap to 69% for everyone; it applied to Tamil Nadu's law. Do not treat 69% as a constitutional general ceiling.
**Back:** *"Seventy-six: Tamil Nadu's 69% shielded in the Ninth."*

### Flashcard Amend-077 — 77th Amendment (1995)
**Front:** 77th Amendment (1995) — reservation in promotion for SCs and STs (Art 16(4A))
**Provision:** Enacted 1995; commenced with retrospective effect from **17 Jun 1995**. Inserts **Art 16(4A)**: the State may make provision for reservation in **promotion** in favour of SCs and STs if, in its opinion, they are not adequately represented in State services (✅ Wikisource Const. 2020, Part III, Art 16(4A): "in matters of promotion, with consequential seniority").
**Learn:**
- Trigger: *Indra Sawhney* (1992) held that reservation in promotion was not permitted under Art 16(4) (⚠️ the Court did not authorise promotion quotas; the 77th responded).
- Subsequent: 81st (backlog carry-forward), 82nd (lower qualifying marks), 85th (consequential seniority from 1995).
- Requirement: State's opinion of inadequate representation (quantifiable data, *Nagaraj*).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** *Indra Sawhney* (1992), *M. Nagaraj v. Union of India* (2006): upheld 77th, 81st, 82nd, 85th subject to quantifiable data, backwardness and efficiency (creamy layer for SC/ST also tested); *Jarnail Singh v. Lachhmi Narain Gupta* (2018) — no creamy layer for SC/ST promotions (⚠️ verify wording).
**Trap:** Art 16(4A) is about promotion, not admission (Art 15(4)/(5)). The 85th (2001) is the seniority amendment; do not attribute retrospective seniority to the 77th alone.
**Back:** *"Seventy-seven: promotions, not just jobs, for SC and ST."*

### Flashcard Amend-078 — 78th Amendment (1995)
**Front:** 78th Amendment (1995) — 27 land-reform laws in the Ninth Schedule
**Provision:** Enacted 1995; commenced 30 Aug 1995. Adds **27 State land-reform Acts** to the **Ninth Schedule** (cumulative total cited as 284).
**Learn:**
- Continues the land-reform Ninth Schedule series (17th, 29th, 34th, 47th, 66th).
- Post-1973 basic-structure rule applies to these laws too (*Waman Rao*, *Coelho*).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 31B, *I.R. Coelho* (2007), *Waman Rao* (1981), 66th (1990).
**Trap:** The "284" total is a commonly cited figure (⚠️ verify exact current count). Do not treat the Ninth Schedule as immune from review.
**Back:** *"Seventy-eight: twenty-seven land laws; the shield now holds 284."*

### Flashcard Amend-079 — 79th Amendment (1999)
**Front:** 79th Amendment (1999) — SC/ST and Anglo-Indian reservation extended to 2010
**Provision:** Enacted 1999; commenced 25 Jan 2000. Amends **Art 334** to extend legislative reservation for SCs and STs, and the Anglo-Indian nomination, from 2000 to **2010**.
**Learn:**
- Part of the decade cycle (45th, 62nd, 79th, 95th, 104th).
- The 79th came at a time when the 2001 census was approaching; the 84th and 87th then changed the census base.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 334, 62nd, 95th, 104th; 84th (2001) and 87th (2003) on census.
**Trap:** Do not confuse 79th (2010) with 95th (2020) and 104th (2030).
**Back:** *"Seventy-nine: legislative quota to two thousand ten."*

### Flashcard Amend-080 — 80th Amendment (2000)
**Front:** 80th Amendment (2000) — alternative scheme of tax devolution (Tenth Finance Commission)
**Provision:** Enacted 2000; commenced 9 Jun 2000. Amends **Arts 269 and 270**; **omits Art 272** (✅ Wikisource Const. 2020, Part XII: Art 272 "Omitted by the Constitution (Eightieth Amendment) Act, 2000, s. 4 (w.e.f. 9-6-2000)"). Union taxes now share a common pool: the net proceeds of all Union taxes (except surcharges and cess) are distributed between the Union and the States **as recommended by the Finance Commission**. The Tenth FC recommended **29%** to the States.
**Learn:**
- Trigger: the old Art 272 system (Union duties distributed under Art 272) and the 1999–2000 fiscal debate; the 80th created a single pool based on the FC.
- Art 270 is the pooling provision; Art 269 keeps assigned taxes (e.g. stamp duty) with the Union's collection and State appropriation.
- Finance Commissions (Art 280) make the recommendation every five years.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 268, 269, 270, 272 (omitted), 280 (Finance Commission), 88th (2003, service tax), 101st (2016, GST), 29 Finance Commission (owner file).
**Trap:** 29% is the Tenth FC's recommendation, not a constitutional share. Do not say 29% is in the Constitution. Do not confuse Art 270 (divisible pool) with Art 269 (assigned taxes).
**Back:** *"Eighty: one pool, FC decides the share, twenty-nine per cent in the Tenth Finance Commission."*

### Flashcard Amend-081 — 81st Amendment (2000)
**Front:** 81st Amendment (2000) — backlog reserved vacancies carried forward (Art 16(4B))
**Provision:** Enacted 2000; commenced 9 Jun 2000. Inserts **Art 16(4B)**: unfilled reserved vacancies for SCs/STs in a year may be carried forward to the next year and are not counted in the 50% ceiling of that year.
**Learn:**
- Trigger: *Indra Sawhney* (1992) capped reservation at 50% per year, so unfilled vacancies could not be carried forward.
- The 81st created the carry-forward ("backlog") mechanism for promotions.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** *Indra Sawhney* (1992), *M. Nagaraj* (2006), 77th (1995), 82nd (2000), 85th (2001).
**Trap:** Carry-forward is a separate category from the 50% ceiling; do not describe it as an extra percentage of seats. It applies to vacancies, not to fresh appointment quotas.
**Back:** *"Eighty-one: the backlog carries forward, outside the fifty."*

### Flashcard Amend-082 — 82nd Amendment (2000)
**Front:** 82nd Amendment (2000) — relaxation of standards for SC/ST promotion (Art 335 proviso)
**Provision:** Enacted 2000; commenced 8 Sep 2000. Adds a **proviso to Art 335** (✅ Wikisource Const. 2020, Part XVI): nothing prevents the State from making relaxation in qualifying marks in examinations, or lowering evaluation standards, for SC/ST members in reserved posts and promotions.
**Learn:**
- Art 335 is the "efficiency" clause (claims of SC/ST must be consistent with the maintenance of efficiency of administration).
- The proviso lets the State relax the minimum qualifying marks for SC/ST promotions.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 335, *M. Nagaraj* (2006), 77th, 81st, 85th.
**Trap:** Relaxation is for SC/ST, not OBC; the 82nd does not allow relaxing the merit threshold for general category. ✅ corrected: the Art 335 proviso does itself refer to "reservation ... to any class or classes of services or posts" in matters of promotion (Wikisource Part XVI), but it only permits relaxing marks or evaluation standards; the general promotion-reservation power is Art 16(4A).
**Back:** *"Eighty-two: SC/ST promotion marks may be relaxed."*

### Flashcard Amend-083 — 83rd Amendment (2000)
**Front:** 83rd Amendment (2000) — Arunachal Pradesh exempted from SC reservation in Panchayats
**Provision:** Enacted 2000; commenced 8 Sep 2000. Inserts **Art 243M(3A)**: Art 243D's reservation of seats for Scheduled Castes does not apply to Arunachal Pradesh, which has no Scheduled Castes (✅ corrected: was "Amends Art 243M(4)(b): Part IX does not apply to the Scheduled Areas of Arunachal Pradesh"; Wikisource Const. 2020, Part IX, Art 243M(3A): "Nothing in article 243D, relating to reservation of seats for the Scheduled Castes ... shall apply to the State of Arunachal Pradesh"; 243M(4)(b) is the Parliament-extension clause).
**Learn:**
- Art 243M is the exemptions clause for Part IX: Scheduled Areas (243M(1)), Nagaland, Meghalaya and Mizoram plus Manipur's hill areas (243M(2)), and the 83rd's Arunachal SC-reservation clause (243M(3A)) (✅ Wikisource Part IX).
- The 83rd kept the 73rd's basic framework and removed a reservation requirement with no beneficiaries.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 243M, Art 243D, 73rd (1992), Fifth and Sixth Schedules, Art 244.
**Trap:** The 83rd does not exempt Arunachal from Panchayats altogether; it excludes the SC reservation. Do not write that Part IX does not apply to Arunachal at all.
**Back:** *"Eighty-three: Arunachal has no SC seats to reserve."*

### Flashcard Amend-084 — 84th Amendment (2001)
**Front:** 84th Amendment (2001) — freeze on delimitation until the first census after 2026
**Provision:** Enacted 2001; commenced 21 Feb 2002. Amends **Arts 55, 81, 82, 170, 330 and 332**: the freeze on readjustment of the number of seats and territorial boundaries (based on the 1971 census) is extended until the **first census after 2026** (✅ Art 332(3A)–(3B) wording "first census ... after the year 2026", Wikisource Part XVI). Delimitation for seat allocation continues on the 1971 base.
**Learn:**
- The freeze was an earlier decision (42nd, 1976 to 2001) extended to 2026 to deter population-control-related seat losses for States with good family-planning records.
- The 87th (2003) then changed the basis for delimitation to the **2001 census** while keeping the freeze on seats.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 81, 82, 170, 330, 332; 42nd (1976); 87th (2003); 106th (2023) (women's quota linked to delimitation).
**Trap:** The freeze was on seat numbers and boundaries until after 2026; it is not the 2001 census. Do not write that the 84th used the 2001 census (it was the 87th).
**Back:** *"Eighty-four: seats frozen on the 1971 base until after 2026."*

### Flashcard Amend-085 — 85th Amendment (2001)
**Front:** 85th Amendment (2001) — consequential seniority in promotions for SC/ST
**Provision:** Enacted 2001; commenced 4 Jan 2002 (✅ Wikipedia list). Amends **Art 16(4A)**: consequential seniority for SC/ST officers promoted by reservation, applicable with retrospective effect from **17 Jun 1995** (the 77th's date).
**Learn:**
- Trigger: *Ajit Singh* (1999) type disputes, where seniority of promoted SC/ST officers was contested.
- The 85th pairs with the 77th: promotion quota plus seniority.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 16(4A), 77th (1995), *M. Nagaraj* (2006), *Ajit Singh* (1999) (⚠️ verify).
**Trap:** Seniority (85th) is separate from promotion (77th) and from carry-forward (81st). Do not attribute the retrospective date to the 85th alone.
**Back:** *"Eighty-five: seniority follows promotion, back to 1995."*

### Flashcard Amend-086 — 86th Amendment (2002)
**Front:** 86th Amendment (2002) — Right to free and compulsory education (Art 21A)
**Provision:** Enacted 2002; commenced 12 Dec 2002. Inserts **Art 21A**: the State shall provide **free and compulsory education to all children of the age of six to fourteen** years in a manner the State may by law determine (✅ Wikisource Const. 2020, Part III, Art 21A). Amends **Art 45** (State to provide early childhood care and education for all children **until age six**; ✅ Wikisource Part IV, Art 45) and inserts **Art 51A(k)** (parent or guardian to provide opportunities for education to children between six and fourteen).
**Learn:**
- Trigger: *Mohini Jain* (1992) and *Unni Krishnan* (1993) read the right to education into Art 21; the 86th converted it into an express right.
- Enabling law: **Right of Children to Free and Compulsory Education Act, 2009** (in force **1 Apr 2010**).
- Art 21A is a Fundamental Right (Part III); Art 45 (DPSP) covers early childhood.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** *Mohini Jain v. State of Karnataka* (1992); *Unni Krishnan v. State of AP* (1993); RTE Act 2009; Art 21A, Art 45, Art 51A(k); 42nd (Art 51A).
**Trap:** Art 21A covers 6–14 (not 0–14); early childhood under six is in Art 45 (DPSP), not in Art 21A. Art 51A(k) duty is on parents/guardians. Do not confuse with Art 21.
**Back:** *"Eighty-six: six to fourteen, free and compulsory, Article 21A."*

### Flashcard Amend-087 — 87th Amendment (2003)
**Front:** 87th Amendment (2003) — delimitation on the 2001 census
**Provision:** Enacted 2003; commenced 22 Jun 2003. Amends **Arts 81, 82, 170, 330 and 332**: the basis for delimitation of Lok Sabha and Assembly constituencies is the **2001 census** instead of 1991 (the 84th's 1971 freeze stays on seat numbers).
**Learn:**
- Delimitation Commission (Art 82 and 170) uses the census figures; the 87th made the 2001 census the base.
- The freeze on the **number** of seats (84th, 2001) remains until after 2026 — so the 87th changed boundaries, not the total seats.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 82, 170, 84th (2001), 42nd (1976), 106th (2023) (women's quota via delimitation).
**Trap:** Keep 84th (freeze to 2026, 1971 base for seat totals) and 87th (2001 census for boundaries) separate.
**Back:** *"Eighty-seven: boundaries on 2001, numbers still frozen."*

### Flashcard Amend-088 — 88th Amendment (2003)
**Front:** 88th Amendment (2003) — service tax (Art 268A)
**Provision:** Enacted 2003; commenced 15 Jan 2004. Inserts **Art 268A**: service tax levied by the Union, collected and appropriated by the Union and the States in the manner Parliament prescribes; amends **Art 270** and **Seventh Schedule**.
**Learn:**
- Trigger: services were outside the Union's tax power at the time; the 88th added Art 268A to address that. ✅ corrected: Wikisource Const. 2020, Part XII footnote says Art 268A was "never brought into force", so the claim that Art 268A itself added services to the Union's tax base is withdrawn; how service tax was actually levied is ⚠️ unverified.
- Repealed in effect by the 101st (2016) in the GST design (Art 268A omitted; ✅ Wikisource Part XII: "Omitted by the Constitution (One Hundred and First Amendment) Act, 2016, s. 7 (w.e.f. 16-9-2016)").
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 268A (omitted by 101st), Art 270, Entry 92C (Union list services tax), 101st (2016), Art 246A (GST).
**Trap:** Service tax is not a State tax; it was Union-levied, shared. Do not claim Art 268A is still in force.
**Back:** *"Eighty-eight: services taxed, later absorbed into GST."*

### Flashcard Amend-089 — 89th Amendment (2003)
**Front:** 89th Amendment (2003) — separate National Commission for Scheduled Tribes (Art 338A)
**Provision:** Enacted 2003; commencement recorded 28 Sep 2003 in the Wikipedia list (✅); NCST operations are generally dated 19 Feb 2004 (⚠️ unverified). Art 338A confirmed present (✅ Wikisource Part XVI). Amends **Art 338** (National Commission for SCs) and inserts **Art 338A** (National Commission for STs).
**Learn:**
- Splits the joint Commission created by the 65th (1990) into two Commissions: SC (Art 338) and ST (Art 338A).
- Functions: safeguards, investigations, reporting to the President.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 338, 338A, 65th (1990), 102nd (2018, NCBC Art 338B), 31 National Commissions-SC-ST-BC (owner file).
**Trap:** Art 338A is ST, Art 338B is OBC (102nd, 2018) — the three commissions are separate. Do not say the 89th created the SC Commission.
**Back:** *"Eighty-nine: SC and ST commissions split in two."*

### Flashcard Amend-090 — 90th Amendment (2003)
**Front:** 90th Amendment (2003) — Bodoland seats in the Assam Assembly
**Provision:** Enacted 2003; commenced 28 Sep 2003 (per Wikipedia list). Amends **Art 332** (new clause (6); ✅ Wikisource Const. 2020, Part XVI, Art 332(6) and Bodoland proviso) to maintain the proportion of ST and non-ST seats in the Assam Assembly, including the **Bodoland Territorial Areas District**.
**Learn:**
- Trigger: the Bodo Accord (2003) and the creation of the Bodoland Territorial Council; the 90th protects the ST share of Assembly seats when delimitation changes boundaries.
- Art 332(6) is the proportionality clause for Assam.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 332, Art 244 (Sixth Schedule — BTC under the 6th Schedule), 51st (1984), 57th (1987), 92nd (2003, Bodo language).
**Trap:** The Bodo Territorial Council is under the Sixth Schedule (not Art 332 alone); the 90th concerns Assembly seat proportion only. Do not conflate with the 92nd, which listed Bodo as an Eighth Schedule language.
**Back:** *"Ninety: Bodoland's share of Assam's Assembly seats protected."*

### Flashcard Amend-091 — 91st Amendment (2003)
**Front:** 91st Amendment (2003) — 15% cap on ministers and anti-defection tightening
**Provision:** Enacted 2003; commenced 1 Jan 2004. Amends:
- **Art 75(1A)**: total Council of Ministers (including PM) must not exceed **15%** of the House's strength; **Art 164(1A)** for States (with a minimum of **12** ministers in a State; ✅ Wikisource Const. 2020, Part VI, Art 164(1A) first proviso; Art 75(1A) 15% cap including the PM confirmed, Part V).
- **Art 361B**: a member disqualified under the Tenth Schedule is barred from holding any remunerative political post until re-elected or until term ends.
- **Tenth Schedule para 3** (split of one-third of a legislature party) omitted; only the merger exception (para 4) remains.
**Learn:**
- Aim: stop legislators from buying office by defection; the 15% cap limits "ministerial bloat".
- Art 75(1A) and 164(1A) apply to the Union and States respectively.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** *Kihoto Hollohan* (1992) (Tenth Schedule, Speaker's decision); 52nd (1985); Art 361B; Art 75(1A), 164(1A).
**Trap:** The 15% cap is on the **total** ministers, not per-party; the split exception is gone, but the merger exception stays. Do not write that the 91st abolished anti-defection law.
**Back:** *"Ninety-one: fifteen per cent ministers, no splits, no sinecure."*

### Flashcard Amend-092 — 92nd Amendment (2003)
**Front:** 92nd Amendment (2003) — Bodo, Dogri, Maithili and Santali added to the Eighth Schedule
**Provision:** Enacted 2003; commenced 7 Jan 2004. Adds **Bodo, Dogri, Maithili and Santali** to the **Eighth Schedule**, taking the total to **22**.
**Learn:**
- The Eighth Schedule's language count: 14 (original) → 15 (21st, Sindhi, 1967) → 18 (71st, 1992) → 22 (92nd, 2003).
- Being in the Eighth Schedule enables the Sahitya Akademi recognition and the official-language commission (Art 344).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Eighth Schedule, Art 344(1), Art 351, 21st (1967), 71st (1992), 96th (2011, Odia).
**Trap:** Santali is spelt "Santali" in the Schedule (not "Santhali" in some sources). Do not write 18 as the current total.
**Back:** *"Ninety-two: B-D-M-S makes twenty-two."*

### Flashcard Amend-093 — 93rd Amendment (2005)
**Front:** 93rd Amendment (2005) — reservation in educational institutions (Art 15(5))
**Provision:** Enacted 2005; commenced 20 Jan 2006. Inserts **Art 15(5)**: the State may make special provisions for the advancement of SEBCs, SCs and STs in admission to educational institutions, **including private (unaided) institutions**, but **excluding minority institutions** referred to in Art 30(1) (✅ Wikisource Const. 2020, Part III, Art 15(5)). Art 15(5) is the basis of the 27% OBC quota in central institutions.
**Learn:**
- Trigger: *T.M.A. Pai Foundation* (2002) held that the State cannot impose reservation on unaided minority and non-minority private institutions; the 93rd responded.
- The Central Educational Institutions (Reservation in Admission) Act, 2006 gave 27% OBC quota in central institutions.
- Upheld in *Ashoka Kumar Thakur v. Union of India* (**10 Apr 2008**; ✅ Wikipedia: the Court upheld the 27% OBC quotas in government-funded institutions; the page does not discuss Art 15(5) or the bench).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** *T.M.A. Pai Foundation* (2002), *P.A. Inamdar* (2005), *Ashoka Kumar Thakur* (2008), Art 15(4), Art 30(1), Art 15(6) (103rd).
**Trap:** Art 15(5) excludes minority institutions under Art 30(1); Art 15(4) (1951) is the older, institution-neutral provision for advancement of SEBCs, SCs and STs. Do not write that 93rd covers minority institutions. The 27% OBC quota is in central educational institutions under the 2006 Act, not directly in Art 15(5).
**Back:** *"Ninety-three: quotas in schools and colleges, minority institutions excepted."*

### Flashcard Amend-094 — 94th Amendment (2006)
**Front:** 94th Amendment (2006) — Minister in charge of tribal welfare (Art 164(1))
**Provision:** Enacted 2006; commenced 12 Jun 2006. Amends **Art 164(1)**: the provision that a Minister in charge of tribal welfare shall be appointed in the States of **Chhattisgarh, Jharkhand, Madhya Pradesh and Odisha** (✅ Wikisource Const. 2020, Part VI, Art 164(1) proviso: "in the States of Chhattisgarh, Jharkhand, Madhya Pradesh and Odisha"; "Bihar" substituted w.e.f. 12-6-2006 by the 94th; the minister may also handle SC and backward-class welfare).
**Learn:**
- Art 164(1) originally applied to Bihar, MP and Odisha; the 94th recast the list after Jharkhand and Chhattisgarh were formed in 2000.
- Art 164(1) is a directive to appoint, not a power of the Chief Minister to hold tribal welfare in personal discretion.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 164(1), Art 164(1A) (91st), Art 46 (DPSP on weaker sections), Fifth Schedule (Scheduled Areas).
**Trap:** The 94th is about a **minister**, not about Scheduled Areas; the Fifth Schedule is separate. Do not write "Bihar remains mandatory".
**Back:** *"Ninety-four: tribal welfare minister, four States named."*

### Flashcard Amend-095 — 95th Amendment (2009)
**Front:** 95th Amendment (2009) — SC/ST and Anglo-Indian reservation extended to 2020
**Provision:** Enacted 2009; commenced 25 Jan 2010. Amends **Art 334** to extend legislative reservation for SCs and STs and Anglo-Indian nomination until **2020**.
**Learn:**
- Part of the decade cycle: 79th (2010), 95th (2020), 104th (2030).
- Commencement 25 Jan 2010 is the day the previous extension lapsed; the Act was passed before the lapse.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 334, 79th, 104th, 62nd.
**Trap:** The date "2020" is the extension end; the 104th added to 2030 (2019 Act). Do not write the 95th as "2030".
**Back:** *"Ninety-five: legislative quota to twenty-twenty."*

### Flashcard Amend-096 — 96th Amendment (2011)
**Front:** 96th Amendment (2011) — "Oriya" changed to "Odia" in the Eighth Schedule
**Provision:** Enacted 2011; commenced 23 Sep 2011. Substitutes **"Odia"** for **"Oriya"** in the **Eighth Schedule**.
**Learn:**
- A spelling and nomenclature change to match the language's name in the language's own script and usage; the language itself is unchanged.
- Eighth Schedule total remains 22.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Eighth Schedule, 92nd (2003, total 22), Art 344, Art 351.
**Trap:** The 96th does not add a language; the count stays at 22.
**Back:** *"Ninety-six: Oriya becomes Odia, same language, same count."*

### Flashcard Amend-097 — 97th Amendment (2011)
**Front:** 97th Amendment (2011) — right to form co-operatives (Art 19(1)(c)); Part IXB
**Provision:** Titled 2011; commenced 12 Jan 2012. Changes:
- **Art 19(1)(c)** — the right to form associations includes the right to form **co-operative societies**.
- **Art 43B** (DPSP) — State to promote voluntary formation, autonomous functioning, democratic control and professional management of co-operatives.
- **Part IXB (Arts 243ZH–243ZT)** — co-operatives; the Part's provisions on board composition, elections and supersession (Arts 243ZH–243ZT).
**Learn:**
- Co-operatives are a State subject (Entry 32 State List), so Part IXB has a federal dimension.
- Part IXB's protections were challenged for lacking State ratification (Art 368(2)).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** *Rajendra N. Shah v. Union of India* (2021): Part IXB struck down **as it applies to State co-operatives** (as it needed State ratification under Art 368(2) and it affects State subjects), but upheld for **multi-State co-operatives** (Part IXB, Union competence). Art 368(2) proviso; Art 19(1)(c); Art 43B.
**Trap:** The 2021 ruling is partial: the multi-State co-operative part survives; the State part was struck down. Do not write that the whole 97th was struck down (common Prelims trap). Art 43B is DPSP (not FR).
**Back:** *"Ninety-seven: co-op right in, co-op part trimmed by Rajendra N. Shah (2021)."*

### Flashcard Amend-098 — 98th Amendment (2012)
**Front:** 98th Amendment (2012) — special status for Hyderabad-Karnataka region (Art 371J)
**Provision:** Enacted 2012; Wikipedia list records commencement **1 Jan 2013** (✅); the Presidential Order implementing Art 371J is reported as 1 Oct 2013 (⚠️ unverified; the Wikipedia Kalyana Karnataka page does not give it). Art 371J(1)–(2) confirmed (✅ Wikisource Const. 2020, Part XXI): development board, report to the State Assembly, reserved seats and posts. Inserts **Art 371J** in Karnataka: the President may provide for a **separate development board** for the Hyderabad-Karnataka (Kalyana Karnataka) region, with reserved proportion of posts and admission to educational institutions in the region.
**Learn:**
- Art 371J is the Karnataka analogue of the Art 371 family (371, 371A–371I, 371J).
- The Kalyana Karnataka region is the former Hyderabad-Karnataka territory of the Nizam's dominions.
- Operative through a Presidential Order and a Development Board; Art 371J does not create a legislature.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 371J, Art 371 family (371A Nagaland, 371G Mizoram, 371H Arunachal, 371I Goa), Art 371D/E (Telangana/Andhra, 32nd Amendment).
**Trap:** 371J is about Karnataka's region, not Telangana (371D). Do not write that 371J created a new State or legislature.
**Back:** *"Ninety-eight: Kalyana Karnataka gets a development board, Article 371J."*

### Flashcard Amend-099 — 99th Amendment (2014)
**Front:** 99th Amendment (2014) — National Judicial Appointments Commission (NJAC), struck down 2015
**Provision:** Enacted 2014 (with the NJAC Act 2014); commenced **13 Apr 2015**. Inserts **Arts 124A (NJAC), 124B (functions), 124C (Parliament to regulate)** and amends **Arts 127, 128, 217, 222, 224A, 231**. NJAC composition: CJI (chair), two senior-most SC judges, Union Law Minister, and two eminent persons nominated by a committee of PM, CJI and Leader of Opposition (✅ Wikisource Const. 2020, Part V, Art 124A(1)). ✅ corrected: was "a veto power in two eminent persons"; per Wikipedia (NJAC), the Commission "shall not recommend a person for appointment if any two of its members do not agree", so any two members can block, not a veto specific to the eminent persons.
**Learn:**
- Trigger: the collegium system (judges appoint judges) was attacked as opaque; the 99th proposed a commission with executive and eminent-person membership.
- Judgment: ***Supreme Court Advocates-on-Record Association v. Union of India*** (Fourth Judges Case), **16 Oct 2015**, 4:1 (✅ Wikipedia NJAC page: Khehar, Lokur, Joseph and Goel JJ. in majority; Chelameswar J. dissenting), held that the 99th and NJAC Act violated **judicial independence**, a basic feature; the **collegium** system was restored.
- The 99th Amendment and the NJAC Act 2014 were both struck down.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** *SCAORA v UoI* (2015); *Kesavananda Bharati* (1973) (judicial independence as basic feature); *S.P. Gupta* (1981) and *Second Judges* (1993) and *Third Judges* (1998) (collegium origin); Arts 124, 217, 222.
**Trap:** The 99th is "struck down" in 2015, not "repealed by Parliament". The collegium was re-established, not created by the 99th. Do not write "2014 judgment". PYQ route: 2019 Q45 (44th & 99th judicial implications).
**Back:** *"Ninety-nine: NJAC, killed in 2015 for hurting judicial independence."*

### Flashcard Amend-100 — 100th Amendment (2015)
**Front:** 100th Amendment (2015) — India–Bangladesh land boundary agreement (enclave exchange)
**Provision:** Enacted 2015; commenced **31 Jul 2015** (✅ Wikipedia list) (the Land Boundary Agreement implemented from **1 Aug 2015** midnight). Amends the **First Schedule** (territories of States) and implements the **Land Boundary Agreement (1974) with Bangladesh, as modified by its 2011 Protocol**: exchange of enclaves, adjustment of boundary in Assam, West Bengal, Meghalaya and Tripura.
**Learn:**
- Trigger: the 1974 agreement (Indira–Mujib) and the 2011 protocol (Manmohan–Hasina) required a constitutional change because it altered the First Schedule.
- *In re Berubari Union* (1960) held that ceding territory needs a constitutional amendment, not just an Art 3 law, so the 100th was passed as a Constitution amendment; the exact procedural basis is ⚠️ to verify.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 3 (formation of States/altering areas), Art 1, First Schedule, 9th (1960, Berubari), *In re Berubari Union* (1960) (Art 143 advisory opinion on territory transfer; ⚠️ verify), 100th.
**Trap:** The 100th did not change the Indo-Bangladesh boundary by treaty alone; the Constitution needed the First Schedule change. Do not confuse with 9th (1960, Berubari). The enclave exchange happened on 31 Jul / 1 Aug 2015 — the date is a common trap.
**Back:** *"One hundred: enclaves swapped, First Schedule rewritten."*

### Flashcard Amend-101 — 101st Amendment (2016)
**Front:** 101st Amendment (2016) — Goods and Services Tax (GST)
**Provision:** Enacted 2016 (assent 8 Sep 2016; ✅ Wikipedia 101st page); commenced **1 Jul 2017** (✅ Wikipedia list). Inserts:
- **Art 246A** — Parliament and State legislatures may make laws on GST (CGST, SGST, IGST).
- **Art 269A** — IGST levy on inter-State supplies, collected by the Union and apportioned.
- **Art 279A** — GST Council (Chair: Union Finance Minister; members: State Finance Ministers). Decisions require **three-fourths** of weighted votes (Centre 1/3, States 2/3); the Council's recommendations are not binding on the legislatures.
- **Art 268A** (service tax) omitted; Entry 92C (services tax, Union List) omitted; amends Arts 248, 249, 250, 268, 269, 270, 271, 286, 366, 368; Schedules 6 and 7.
- Ratified by half of the State legislatures (Art 368(2)).
**Learn:**
- Replaces many indirect taxes: excise duty, service tax, VAT/sales tax, entertainment tax (State) and octroi (local), with a dual system.
- Three-tier GST: CGST (Centre), SGST (States), IGST (inter-State).
- Art 279A(9) gives the Centre and States a **weighted** vote (Centre one-third, States two-thirds), which is a federal innovation (✅ corrected: was "Art 279A(8)", which concerns the Council's procedure; Wikisource Const. 2020, Part XII).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 246A, 269A, 279A, 268A (omitted), Arts 286 (inter-State sale restriction), 46th (1982), 6th (1956 sales tax), 88th (2003), 80th (2000), 30 GST Council (owner file).
**Trap:** Alcohol for human consumption and petroleum products are outside GST (Art 366(12) and Entry 84 state-level ✓ — verify Entry 84 wording). GST Council decisions are a **3/4 weighted** majority (Centre 1/3); a common trap is "simple majority". Do not say the GST Council's recommendations bind Parliament or States.
**Back:** *"One-oh-one: GST, three-fourths of weighted votes, Centre one-third."*

### Flashcard Amend-102 — 102nd Amendment (2018)
**Front:** 102nd Amendment (2018) — constitutional National Commission for Backward Classes (Art 338B)
**Provision:** Enacted 2018; commenced **11 Aug 2018**. Inserts **Art 338B** (National Commission for Backward Classes as a constitutional body) and **Art 342A** (President, after consultation with the Governor, specifies **socially and educationally backward classes** by public notification; Parliament may include or exclude by law); inserts **Art 366(26C)** (definition of SEBC).
**Learn:**
- Trigger: the Maratha reservation judgment (*Indra Sawhney* in mind) and the need to tie OBC lists to a constitutional body.
- Art 342A(1): the President, after consulting the Governor, specifies the lists by public notification; Art 342A(2): Parliament may by law include or exclude. The 105th (2021) restored State power to make their own lists.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 338B, 342A, 366(26C); 105th (2021); *Jaishri Laxmanrao Patil* (2021); Art 15(4)/(5), 16(4); 31 National Commissions owner file.
**Trap:** Art 338B is OBC (NCBC); Art 338 SC, Art 338A ST. The 102nd's Presidential list power was curbed by the 105th for State lists. Do not say the 102nd gave States power to make lists.
**Back:** *"One-oh-two: OBC commission in the Constitution; the President lists the backward."*

### Flashcard Amend-103 — 103rd Amendment (2019)
**Front:** 103rd Amendment (2019) — 10% reservation for Economically Weaker Sections (EWS)
**Provision:** Enacted 2019; commenced **12 Jan 2019**. Inserts **Art 15(6)** and **Art 16(6)**: reservation of up to **10%** of seats/posts in education and public employment for "economically weaker sections" (other than the SC, ST and OBC categories covered under Art 15(4)/(5), 16(4)), by law. ✅ corrected: the claimed Art 46 amendment is not supported. Wikisource Const. 2020, Part IV gives Art 46 as "weaker sections of the people ... in particular, of the Scheduled Castes and the Scheduled Tribes", with no economically-weaker wording; Gazette not checked (⚠️).
**Learn:**
- First economic-criteria quota; EWS definition is set by government (family income and property thresholds).
- The 50% cap of *Indra Sawhney* was argued to be exceeded by the EWS quota; the Court upheld it on the basis that EWS is outside the existing categories (✅ Wikipedia Janhit Abhiyan: majority held EWS a valid economic classification not infringing existing SEBC/SC/ST reservations; the page states no holding on the 50% ceiling, ⚠️).
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** *Janhit Abhiyan v. Union of India* (**7 Nov 2022**): upheld 103rd by **3:2** (majority: Maheshwari, Trivedi and Pardiwala JJ.); dissent (CJI U.U. Lalit and Bhat J.) (✅ Wikipedia: five-judge bench, as stated). *Indra Sawhney* (1992): 50% ceiling. Art 15(4), 15(5), 16(4).
**Trap:** EWS quota is 10% of seats **in addition** to existing quotas (SC/ST/OBC), with a 50% ceiling for the older categories. Do not write the EWS quota as "10% of total seats in all cases", and do not extend EWS to SC/ST/OBC beneficiaries already covered by Arts 15(4)–(5) and 16(4).
**Back:** *"One-oh-three: ten per cent for the economically weaker, upheld three-two."*

### Flashcard Amend-104 — 104th Amendment (2019)
**Front:** 104th Amendment (2019) — SC/ST seats extended to 2030; Anglo-Indian nomination ended
**Provision:** Titled 2019; commenced **25 Jan 2020**. Amends **Art 334** to extend legislative reservation for SCs and STs until **2030**; ends Anglo-Indian nomination through Art 334(b) (✅ corrected: was "omits the Anglo-Indian nomination provisions (Arts 331, 333)"; Wikisource Const. 2020, Part XVI gives Art 334 cessation as "eighty years" for clause (a), SC/ST, and "seventy years" for clause (b), Anglo-Indian; Arts 331 and 333 remain printed and are not shown as omitted).
**Learn:**
- Final extension in the decade cycle (45th 1980–90, 62nd to 2000, 79th to 2010, 95th to 2020, 104th to 2030).
- Anglo-Indian nomination ended; the Anglo-Indian community's representation now comes through the general electoral process.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 334, Art 331 (Lok Sabha Anglo-Indian nomination), Art 333 (Assembly Anglo-Indian nomination), 95th (2009).
**Trap:** The end of Anglo-Indian nomination is in the 104th, not the 95th; the 2030 date is the extension end. Do not state that services reservation (Art 16(4)) is extended — it has no sunset.
**Back:** *"One-oh-four: legislative quota to thirty; the nominated Anglo-Indians go."*

### Flashcard Amend-105 — 105th Amendment (2021)
**Front:** 105th Amendment (2021) — States regain power to identify OBCs/SEBCs
**Provision:** Enacted 2021; assent 18 Aug 2021 and gazette 19 Aug 2021 (✅ Wikipedia 105th page). Commencement is **10 Aug 2021** in the Wikipedia list but "deemed to be in force from 15 August 2021" on the Amendment's own page (✅ both quoted; conflict unresolved, ⚠️). Amends **Art 342A** (adds a clause; ⚠️ clause number (3) unverified; the power of the State to prepare and maintain its own list of SEBCs/OBCs is restored, subject to the Presidential list for the Centre) and **Art 366(26C)**; the Presidential list under Art 342A(1) continues for the Central list.
**Learn:**
- Trigger: *Jaishri Laxmanrao Patil v. Chief Minister, Maharashtra* (**5 May 2021**; ⚠️ date unverified, Wikipedia pages not reachable): the Supreme Court held that after the 102nd (2018), States lost power to identify SEBCs, invalidating the Maharashtra SEBC Act 2018 (Maratha quota).
- The 105th reversed that outcome for the State-level lists.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** *Jaishri Laxmanrao Patil* (2021); 102nd (2018); Art 342A, 366(26C), Art 338B; *Indra Sawhney* (1992), 50% ceiling.
**Trap:** The 105th restores **State power to identify SEBCs**; it does not decide the Maratha quota or the 50% ceiling. Do not write that the 105th raised the reservation cap.
**Back:** *"One-oh-five: States make their own OBC lists again."*

### Flashcard Amend-106 — 106th Amendment (2023)
**Front:** 106th Amendment (2023) — Nari Shakti Vandan Adhiniyam (one-third women's reservation)
**Provision:** Titled **2023** ("Constitution (One Hundred and Sixth Amendment) Act, 2023", introduced as the 128th Constitution Amendment Bill ⚠️ bill number unverified). Lok Sabha passed it 20 Sep 2023 (454 for, 2 against); Rajya Sabha 21 Sep 2023 (214 for, none against); assent **28 Sep 2023** (✅ Wikipedia 106th page, which also gives 454–2 and 214–0). Inserts:
- **Art 330A** — one-third of Lok Sabha seats reserved for women (including one-third of SC and ST seats for SC/ST women).
- **Art 332A** — same for State Legislative Assemblies and the Delhi Assembly.
- **Art 334A** — reservation begins after delimitation based on the first census published after commencement; rotation of seats after each delimitation; reservation **ceases 15 years** from commencement.
- Amends **Art 239AA** for Delhi.
**Learn:**
- **Commencement is not operation.** Wikipedia (citing a Deccan Herald report dated 16 Apr 2026) says the Act was brought into force by gazette notification on **16 Apr 2026** (⚠️ softened: single secondary source; gazette not reached; the Wikipedia page itself says it needs more citations; the owner-ledger basis was not checked); the reservation is still inoperative until the first census published after that date and a later delimitation. Do not compute an effective year.
- The 131st Amendment Bill, 2026 (which proposed changes to Art 81 and the census basis for delimitation) was **defeated in the Lok Sabha on 17 Apr 2026 (298:230)** (⚠️ Wikipedia only; its cited link appears mismatched); it did not change the 106th.
- Women's reservation is not in Art 15/16 but in the composition of the Lok Sabha and Assemblies.
**Cases / Amendments / Links:** Art 330, 332, 334 (sunset), 330A, 332A, 334A, 239AA, 84th (2001) and 87th (2003) (freeze and census base), 131st Amendment Bill, 2026 (defeated; not law).
**Trap:** Commenced ≠ operational ≠ implemented; the 15-year period runs from commencement, not from 2029. Do not claim the quota applies in 2024 or 2029 elections. The 2023 Act is "106th", not "128th" (the bill number). Mains hook: *Shakti Vandan* vs delimitation delay vs rotation.
**Back:** *"One-oh-six: a third for women, once the census and delimitation arrive."*

## D. ARTICLES — one flashcard per Article

### PART I — The Union and its Territory

### Flashcard Art-1 — Article 1
**Front:** Article 1 — Name and territory of the Union
**Provision:** (1) India, that is Bharat, shall be a Union of States. (2) The States and their territories are those specified in the First Schedule. (3) The territory of India comprises (a) the territories of the States, (b) the Union Territories specified in the First Schedule, (c) such other territories as may be acquired.
**Learn:**
- Two official names: "India" and "Bharat". Union, not "Federation".
- Ambedkar's reasoning (Constituent Assembly): the Indian federation is **not the result of an agreement among States**, and **no State has the right to secede**. Hence "indestructible Union of destructible States".
- Three-part territory: States + UTs + acquired territories (Goa, Dadra and Nagar Haveli, Puducherry, Sikkim).
- **Territory of India is wider than Union of India** (Union = States only; UTs are part of the territory but not members of the federation).
**Cases / Links:** *State of West Bengal v. Union of India* (1962) — India is not a classical federation; States have no sovereignty; strong unitary bias. *S.R. Bommai* (1994) — federalism is part of the basic structure. 7th Amendment (1956) rewrote Art 1(2) when Part A/B/C/D States were abolished.
**Trap:** "Federation" never appears in the Constitution — it says Union. Do not equate "Union of India" with "territory of India".
**Back:** *"1 = Name + Map: Union of States, Territory of three kinds (S-U-A)."*

### Flashcard Art-2 — Article 2
**Front:** Article 2 — Admission or establishment of new States
**Provision:** Parliament may by law admit into the Union, or establish, new States on such terms and conditions as it thinks fit.
**Learn:**
- Standard doctrine: **Art 2 = admit/establish States beyond the existing internal map**; **Art 3 = reorganise units already inside India**. ✅ The Constitution itself does not use the labels "outside" and "inside" — they are classroom shorthand.
- Terms and conditions are set by Parliament and may differ State to State.
- Law under Art 2 is **not an Art 368 amendment** (Art 4(2)): simple majority.
**Cases / Links:** Sikkim — associate State (Art 2A, 35th Amendment 1974) → full State (36th Amendment 1975, the 22nd State; Art 371F). 
**Trap:** Art 2 does not cover internal boundary changes; Art 3 does. Neither needs State consent.
**Back:** *"2 = New (admit/establish); 3 = Existing (alter)."*

### Flashcard Art-2A — Article 2A (omitted)
**Front:** Article 2A — Sikkim to be associated with the Union
**Provision:** Inserted by the **35th Amendment (1974)** to make Sikkim an **Associate State** of India (with representation in Parliament through the Tenth Schedule as it then stood). **Omitted by the 36th Amendment (1975)** when Sikkim became a full State.
**Learn:**
- 35th Amendment 1974: Sikkim = Associate State (Art 2A + the original Tenth Schedule).
- 36th Amendment 1975: Sikkim = 22nd State (26 April 1975); Art 2A and the original Tenth Schedule omitted; Art 371F added for Sikkim's special provisions; First and Fourth Schedules amended.
- The **present Tenth Schedule** (anti-defection) was inserted later by the **52nd Amendment (1985)** — a classic confusion.
**Trap:** Associate State existed only 1974–75 and only for Sikkim. Tenth Schedule today = anti-defection, not Sikkim.
**Back:** *"35 = Sikkim Associate; 36 = Sikkim State (22nd)."*

### Flashcard Art-3 — Article 3
**Front:** Article 3 — Formation of new States and alteration of areas, boundaries or names of existing States
**Provision:** Parliament may by law (a) form a new State by separation of territory from a State, by uniting two or more States or parts, or by uniting any territory to a part of a State; (b) increase the area of any State; (c) diminish the area of any State; (d) alter the boundaries of any State; (e) alter the name of any State. **Proviso:** no Bill for this purpose may be introduced except on the **recommendation of the President**, and where the proposal affects the area, boundaries or name of any State, the President must **refer the Bill to that State's legislature** for its views within a specified period (extendable). **Explanation I:** "State" in (a)–(e) includes a Union Territory, but not in the proviso. **Explanation II:** (a) includes power to form a State or UT by uniting a part of a State or UT to another State or UT (18th Amendment 1966).
**Learn:**
- Process: President's recommendation → reference to State legislature (views **not binding**) → Bill passed by **simple majority** in Parliament (Art 4).
- **No reference to any legislature is needed for a UT.**
- State consent is **not required** → "indestructible Union of destructible States".
- Parliament may accept or reject the State's views, and may pass amendments to the Bill without a fresh reference.
- Art 3 covers internal readjustment; **cession of Indian territory to a foreign country needs an Art 368 amendment**.
**Cases / Links:** *In re Berubari Union* (1960) — Art 3 does not permit cession of territory → 9th Amendment 1960. *Babulal Parate v. State of Bombay* (1960) — State's views not binding; no fresh reference for every amendment. *Maganbhai Ishwarbhai Patel* (1969) — settling a boundary dispute is an executive act, not cession. Uses: Bombay Reorganisation Act 1960 (Maharashtra, Gujarat), Punjab Reorganisation Act 1966 (Haryana), 2000 (Chhattisgarh, Uttarakhand, Jharkhand), Andhra Pradesh Reorganisation Act 2014 (Telangana, 29th State), J&K Reorganisation Act 2019 (UTs of J&K and Ladakh).
**Trap:** (1) State consent is not needed; (2) reference is not binding; (3) not an Art 368 amendment; (4) first linguistic State was **Andhra (1953)**, not Maharashtra. PYQ route: 2025 Prelims GS-I Q52 (Nagaland 1963, Tripura 1972, Arunachal Pradesh 1987).
**Back:** *"3 = Alter, Name, Area, Border — President proposes, State is only consulted."*

### Flashcard Art-4 — Article 4
**Front:** Article 4 — Laws under Articles 2 and 3 are not Art 368 amendments
**Provision:** (1) A law under Art 2 or 3 shall contain provisions for amending the First Schedule and the Fourth Schedule and such supplemental, incidental and consequential provisions (including representation in Parliament and State legislatures) as Parliament thinks necessary. (2) No such law is to be deemed an amendment of the Constitution for Art 368.
**Learn:**
- **Simple majority** (not special majority; no State ratification).
- Automatic changes: **First Schedule** (names/territories of States and UTs) and **Fourth Schedule** (Rajya Sabha seats).
- Consequential provisions: seats in Lok Sabha, Rajya Sabha, State legislature.
**Trap:** Do not say reorganisation needs a constitutional amendment. Cession (Berubari) does.
**Back:** *"4 = Law, not Amendment: change First + Fourth Schedules by simple majority."*

### PART II — Citizenship

### Flashcard Art-5 — Article 5
**Front:** Article 5 — Citizenship at the commencement of the Constitution
**Provision:** At the commencement (26 January 1950), every person **domiciled in the territory of India** and (a) born in India, or (b) either of whose parents was born in India, or (c) ordinarily resident in India for not less than **five years** immediately before commencement, is a citizen.
**Learn:**
- **Domicile + one of three tests (birth / parent's birth / 5 years' residence).**
- Domicile (legal home) ≠ residence (fact) ≠ citizenship (membership).
- Arts 5, 6, 7, 8 and 9 came into force on **26 November 1949**; the rest of the Constitution on 26 January 1950 (Art 394).
**Trap:** Domicile alone is not enough. Constitution covers only 1950 citizens; later citizens come under the **Citizenship Act 1955**. Citizenship is **not a Fundamental Right**.
**Back:** *"5 = Domicile + (B-P-R): Born, Parent, 5-year Resident."*

### Flashcard Art-6 — Article 6
**Front:** Article 6 — Migrants from Pakistan
**Provision:** A person who migrated to India from the territory now in Pakistan is deemed a citizen if (a) they, a parent, or a grandparent was born in India (as defined in the Government of India Act 1935), and (b) (i) if they migrated **before 19 July 1948**, they have been ordinarily resident in India since migration; or (ii) if they migrated **on or after 19 July 1948**, they were **registered** as a citizen on application, after at least **six months'** residence before applying.
**Learn:**
- The cut-off **19 July 1948** = the date the **permit system** for entry from Pakistan began.
- Before 19 July 1948 → automatic; on/after → registration + 6 months' residence.
- "India as defined in the GoI Act 1935" = undivided India.
**Trap:** The 19 July 1948 date in Art 6 differs from the **1 March 1947** date in Art 7. Do not confuse with the **24 March 1971** Assam Accord cut-off or the **31 December 2014** CAA cut-off.
**Back:** *"6 = Pakistan IN: before 19-7-48 automatic; after, register (6 months)."*

### Flashcard Art-7 — Article 7
**Front:** Article 7 — Migrants to Pakistan
**Provision:** A person who **after 1 March 1947** migrated from India to Pakistan is **not** deemed a citizen. Exception: a person who, after migrating to Pakistan, **returned under a permit for resettlement or permanent return** issued by authority of law is treated as having migrated to India after 19 July 1948 (so Art 6(b)(ii) registration route applies).
**Learn:**
- Date: **1 March 1947**.
- Loss of citizenship for migrants to Pakistan, except permit-return cases.
- Practical impact: the 1950s disputes about property and status of such persons.
**Trap:** Art 7 relates to **migration to Pakistan**; Art 6 to **migration from Pakistan**. Return on a **short visit** is not "permit for resettlement or permanent return".
**Back:** *"7 = Pakistan OUT after 1-3-47 = no citizenship (unless permit-return)."*

### Flashcard Art-8 — Article 8
**Front:** Article 8 — Persons of Indian origin residing outside India
**Provision:** A person who, or whose parent or grandparent, was born in India (as defined in the GoI Act 1935) and who is **ordinarily residing outside India** is deemed a citizen if **registered by the Indian diplomatic or consular representative** in the country of residence on application in the prescribed form (before or after commencement).
**Learn:**
- Covers the early diaspora (Indian origin up to grandparents).
- Registration through **embassy/consulate**.
- Distinct from later statutory **OCI** (2003 amendment of the Citizenship Act; PIO and OCI merged in 2015), which is **not** citizenship.
**Trap:** OCI ≠ Art 8. OCI is **not dual citizenship**; OCI holders cannot vote or hold constitutional office.
**Back:** *"8 = Abroad Indians: register at the Embassy."*

### Flashcard Art-9 — Article 9
**Front:** Article 9 — Voluntary acquisition of foreign citizenship
**Provision:** No person shall be a citizen of India by virtue of Art 5, or be deemed a citizen by virtue of Art 6 or 8, if they have **voluntarily acquired the citizenship of any foreign State**.
**Learn:**
- Mirrors the single-citizenship principle: **no dual citizenship** at commencement.
- Later (post-1950) voluntary acquisition is governed by **Section 9 of the Citizenship Act 1955** — **termination** (automatic loss).
- Art 9 alone is not the complete permanent rule.
**Trap:** Termination (automatic) ≠ renunciation (voluntary declaration) ≠ deprivation (compulsory, by the Centre). **OCI is not a dual citizenship.**
**Back:** *"9 = Foreign passport (voluntary) = lose Indian status."*

### Flashcard Art-10 — Article 10
**Front:** Article 10 — Continuance of citizenship
**Provision:** Every person who is or is deemed a citizen under Part II shall, **subject to any law made by Parliament**, continue to be a citizen.
**Learn:**
- Citizenship acquired under Arts 5–8 continues.
- Continuance is **subject to Parliament's law** (Art 11): Parliament can regulate loss.
**Trap:** Continuance is not absolute or constitutionally entrenched.
**Back:** *"10 = Continue — unless Parliament says otherwise."*

### Flashcard Art-11 — Article 11
**Front:** Article 11 — Parliament to regulate citizenship by law
**Provision:** Nothing in Part II derogates from Parliament's power to make provisions on **acquisition and termination of citizenship** and all other matters relating to citizenship.
**Learn:**
- **Plenary power**; Union List Entry 17 (citizenship, naturalisation and aliens).
- Exercised by the **Citizenship Act 1955**:
  - **Acquisition (5):** birth, descent, registration, naturalisation, incorporation of territory — 🔑 **"BeD RaIN"**.
  - **Loss (3):** renunciation, termination, deprivation — 🔑 **"R-T-D"**.
- Birth rule: 26 Jan 1950–30 June 1987 = any birth in India; 1 July 1987–2 Dec 2004 = either parent a citizen; from **3 Dec 2004** = both parents citizens, or one citizen and the other not an illegal migrant.
- Naturalisation: 12 months' continuous residence + **11 of the preceding 14 years**.
- Major amendments: 1986 (birth), 1985 (Assam Accord, **Section 6A**, cut-off **24 March 1971**), 2003 (OCI), 2005, 2015 (PIO+OCI merged), **2019 (CAA)** — six communities from **Pakistan, Bangladesh, Afghanistan** entering **on or before 31 December 2014**; rules notified March 2024.
- Deprivation: only for registered/naturalised citizens — fraud, disloyalty, trading with the enemy, 2 years' imprisonment within 5 years, 7 years' residence abroad. PYQ route: **2021 Prelims GS-I Q89**.
